In this section, we first state and prove these
fluctuation averaging lemmas.

Definition 3.1

(Minors)
.

For any
S\subseteq[N]
, let
G^{(S)}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times N}
be the matrix with
columns

G^{(S)}\mathbf{e}_{i}=\begin{cases}\mathbf{g}_{i}&\text{if $i\notin S$}\\
0&\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}

Let

K^{(S)}=\frac{1}{N}G^{(S)}G^{(S)*}=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i\in[N]\setminus S}\mathbf{g}_{i}\mathbf{g}_{i}^{*},\qquad\widetilde{K}^{(S)}=\frac{1}{N}G^{(S)*}G^{(S)},

and denote their resolvents and Stieltjes transforms

R^{(S)}(z)=(K^{(S)}-zI_{n})^{-1},\quad m^{(S)}(z)=n^{-1}\operatorname{Tr}R^{(S)}(z),

\widetilde{R}^{(S)}(z)=(\widetilde{K}^{(S)}-zI_{N})^{-1},\quad\widetilde{m}^{(S)}(z)=N^{-1}\operatorname{Tr}\widetilde{R}^{(S)}(z).

The expectation with respect to the columns
\{\mathbf{g}_{i}\}_{i\in S}
of
G
is
denoted as

\mathbb{E}_{S}[\cdot]:=\mathbb{E}[\;\cdot\mid\{\mathbf{g}_{i}\}_{i\in[N]\setminus S}].

We will often omit the spectral argument
z
for brevity when the meaning is
clear.

Definition 3.2

(Stochastic domination)
.

For
(N,n)
-dependent random variables
X\in\mathbb{R}
and
Y\geq 0
, we write

X\prec Y\quad\text{ or }\quad X=O_{\prec}(Y)

if, for any constants
\varepsilon,D>0
, there exists
C\equiv C(\varepsilon,D)>0
such that

\mathbb{P}[|X|\geq N^{\varepsilon}Y]\leq CN^{-D}
. (Equivalently, there exists
N_{0}\equiv N_{0}(\varepsilon,D)
such that
\mathbb{P}[|X|\geq N^{\varepsilon}Y]\leq N^{-D}
for all
N\geq N_{0}
.) For
X\equiv X(u)
and
Y\equiv Y(u)
depending on a parameter
u\in\mathcal{U}^{(N)}
, we say that

X\prec Y\quad\text{ or }\quad X=O_{\prec}(Y)\qquad\text{ uniformly over }u\in\mathcal{U}^{(N)}

if the preceding holds for a uniform constant
C\equiv C(\varepsilon,D)>0
and all
u\in\mathcal{U}^{(N)}
.

This constant
C(\varepsilon,D)
may depend on other constant quantities in the
context of the statement, including
\tau
and the regularity constants

C,c>0
in Definition
2.3
for a regular spectral domain.

We will often use implicitly the following well-known properties of stochastic domination
\prec
. For the proof of the following lemma, we refer to
[
43
, Lemma D.2]
.

Lemma 3.3
.

(a)

If
X(u,v)\prec\zeta(u,v)
uniformly over
u\in U
and
v\in V
,
and
|V|\leq n^{C}
for some constant
C>0
, then uniformly over
u\in U
,

\displaystyle\sum_{v\in V}X(u,v)\prec\sum_{v\in V}\zeta(u,v)

(b)

If
X_{1}\prec\zeta_{1}
and
X_{2}\prec\zeta_{2}

uniformly over
u\in U
, then also
X_{1}X_{2}\prec\zeta_{1}\zeta_{2}

uniformly over
u\in U
.

(c)

Suppose
X\prec\zeta
uniformly over
u\in U
, where
\zeta
is
deterministic,
\zeta>n^{-C}
,
and
\mathbb{E}[|X|^{k}]\leq n^{C_{k}}
for all
k\in[1,\infty)
and some constants

C,C_{k}>0
. Then
\mathbb{E}[X\mid\mathscr{G}]\prec\zeta
uniformly over

u\in U
and over all sub-sigma-fields
\mathscr{G}\subseteq\mathscr{F}

of the underlying probability space
(\Omega,\mathscr{F},\mathbb{P})
.

For any
z=E+i\eta\in\mathbb{C}^{+}
and
S\subset[N]
,
let us define the control parameter

\Gamma^{(S)}(z)=\max_{i\notin S}|\widetilde{R}_{ii}^{(S)}(z)-\widetilde{m}_{0}(z)|.

The following lemma will be used to show the averaged local law of
Theorem
2.5
.

Lemma 3.4
.

Suppose Assumptions
1
and
2
hold,
and
\mathbf{D}\subset\mathbb{C}^{+}
is a regular spectral domain. Suppose
there exists a constant
\tau^{\prime}>0
and a deterministic function

\Phi:\mathbf{D}\rightarrow[N^{-1/2},N^{-\tau^{\prime}}]
such that for any fixed

L\geq 1
, uniformly over all
S\subseteq[N]
with
|S|\leq L
,
all
z\in\mathbf{D}
, we have

\Gamma^{(S)}\prec N^{-\tau^{\prime}},\qquad\frac{\|R^{(S)}\|_{F}}{N}\prec\Phi,

Then uniformly over all
z\in\mathbf{D}
and deterministic matrices
A\in\mathbb{C}^{n\times n}
with
\|A\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq 1
,

\left|\sum_{i=1}^{N}\frac{N^{-1}\operatorname{Tr}(\mathbf{g}_{i}\mathbf{g}_{i}^{*}-\Sigma)R^{(i)}A}{1+N^{-1}\mathbf{g}_{i}^{*}R^{(i)}\mathbf{g}_{i}}\right|\prec N\Phi^{2}.

To establish anisotropic local laws, our fluctuation averaging lemmas will
assume, as a stronger input, the condition
N^{-1/2}\|R^{(S)}\mathbf{x}\|_{2}\prec\Phi

uniformly over unit vectors
\mathbf{x}\in\mathbb{C}^{n}
. We note that this implies
the preceding condition
N^{-1}\|R^{(S)}\|_{F}\prec\Phi
, since

N^{-1}\|R^{(S)}\|_{F}\leq\max_{\alpha=1}^{n}N^{-1/2}\|R^{(S)}\mathbf{e}_{\alpha}\|_{2}
.
The following lemma will be used to show Theorem
2.10
outside
the spectrum.

Lemma 3.5
.

Suppose Assumptions
1
and
2
hold,
and
\mathbf{D}\subset\mathbb{C}^{+}
is a regular spectral domain. Suppose
there exists a constant
\tau^{\prime}>0
and a deterministic function

\Phi:\mathbf{D}\rightarrow[N^{-1/2},N^{-\tau^{\prime}}]
such that for any fixed

L\geq 1
, uniformly over all
S\subseteq[N]
with
|S|\leq L
,
all
z\in\mathbf{D}
, and all deterministic unit vectors
\mathbf{x}\in\mathbb{C}^{n}
, we have

\Gamma^{(S)}\prec N^{-\tau^{\prime}},\qquad\frac{\|R^{(S)}\mathbf{x}\|_{2}}{\sqrt{N}}\prec\Phi.

Then

(a)

Uniformly over all
z\in\mathbf{D}
and deterministic unit
vectors
\mathbf{u}\in\mathbb{C}^{n}
,

\left|\sum_{i=1}^{N}\frac{N^{-1}\mathbf{u}^{*}(\mathbf{g}_{i}\mathbf{g}_{i}^{*}-\Sigma)R^{(i)}\mathbf{u}}{1+N^{-1}\mathbf{g}_{i}^{*}R^{(i)}\mathbf{g}_{i}}\right|\prec\sqrt{N}\Phi^{2}.

(b)

Uniformly over all
z\in\mathbf{D}
and deterministic unit vectors
\mathbf{v}\in\mathbb{C}^{N}
,

\left|\sum_{i\neq j}\frac{\bar{\mathbf{v}}[i]\mathbf{v}[j]N^{-1}\mathbf{g}_{i}^{*}R^{(ij)}\mathbf{g}_{j}}{(1+N^{-1}\mathbf{g}_{i}^{*}R^{(i)}\mathbf{g}_{i})(1+N^{-1}\mathbf{g}_{j}^{*}R^{(ij)}\mathbf{g}_{j})}\right|\prec N\Phi^{3}.

(c)

Uniformly over all
z\in\mathbf{D}
and deterministic unit vectors
\mathbf{v}\in\mathbb{C}^{N}
and
\mathbf{u}\in\mathbb{C}^{n}
,

\left|\sum_{i=1}^{N}\frac{\bar{\mathbf{v}}(i)N^{-1/2}\mathbf{g}_{i}^{*}R^{(i)}\mathbf{u}}{1+N^{-1}\mathbf{g}_{i}^{*}R^{(i)}\mathbf{g}_{i}}\right|\prec\sqrt{N}\Phi^{2}.

The following strengthening of Lemma
3.4
will show,
under the additional condition of Assumption
3
, the full
anisotropic local law of Theorem
2.8
.
For a resolvent with spectral decomposition

R=\sum_{\alpha}(\lambda_{\alpha}-z)^{-1}\mathbf{v}_{\alpha}\mathbf{v}_{\alpha}^{*}
,
we write
\|R\|_{1}=\sum_{\alpha}|\lambda_{\alpha}-z|^{-1}
.

Lemma 3.6
.

Suppose, in addition to the conditions of Lemma

3.5
, that Assumption
3

also holds, and there exists a constant
\delta\geq 0
such that for any fixed

L\geq 1
, uniformly over all
S\subseteq[N]
with
|S|\leq L
,
all
z\in\mathbf{D}
, and all deterministic unit vectors
\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}\in\mathbb{C}^{n}
, we have

N^{-1}\|R^{(S)}\|_{1}\prec 1,\qquad\qquad|\mathbf{x}^{*}R^{(S)}\mathbf{y}|\prec N^{\delta}.

Then

(a)

Uniformly over all
z\in\mathbf{D}
and
deterministic unit vectors
\mathbf{u}\in\mathbb{C}^{n}
,

(3.1)

\left|\sum_{i=1}^{N}\frac{N^{-1}\mathbf{u}^{*}(\mathbf{g}_{i}\mathbf{g}_{i}^{*}-\Sigma)R^{(i)}\mathbf{u}}{1+N^{-1}\mathbf{g}_{i}^{*}R^{(i)}\mathbf{g}_{i}}\right|\prec N^{\delta}\Phi.

(b)

Uniformly over all
z\in\mathbf{D}
and deterministic unit vectors
\mathbf{v}\in\mathbb{C}^{N}
,

\left|\sum_{i\neq j}\frac{\bar{\mathbf{v}}(i)\mathbf{v}(j)N^{-1}\mathbf{g}_{i}^{*}R^{(ij)}\mathbf{g}_{j}}{(1+N^{-1}\mathbf{g}_{i}^{*}R^{(i)}\mathbf{g}_{i})(1+N^{-1}\mathbf{g}_{j}^{*}R^{(ij)}\mathbf{g}_{j})}\right|\prec N^{2\delta}\Phi.

(c)

Uniformly over all
z\in\mathbf{D}
and deterministic unit vectors
\mathbf{u}\in\mathbb{C}^{n}
and
\mathbf{v}\in\mathbb{C}^{N}
,

\left|\sum_{i=1}^{N}\frac{\bar{\mathbf{v}}(i)N^{-1/2}\mathbf{g}_{i}^{*}R^{(i)}\mathbf{u}}{1+N^{-1}\mathbf{g}_{i}^{*}R^{(i)}\mathbf{g}_{i}}\right|\prec N^{\delta}\Phi.

In the remainder of this section, we prove
Lemmas
3.4
,

3.5
, and

3.6
.

3.1.
Preliminaries

Lemma 3.7

(Resolvent identities)
.

For any
S\subseteq[N]
:

(a)

For any distinct
i,j\notin S
,

(3.2)

\widetilde{R}^{(S)}_{ii}=-\frac{1}{z}\frac{1}{1+N^{-1}\mathbf{g}_{i}^{*}R^{(iS)}\mathbf{g}_{i}},\qquad\widetilde{R}^{(S)}_{ij}=z\widetilde{R}^{(S)}_{ii}\widetilde{R}^{(iS)}_{jj}\cdot N^{-1}\mathbf{g}_{i}^{*}R^{(ijS)}\mathbf{g}_{j}.

(b)

For any
i,j,k\notin S
(including
i=j
) with
k\notin\{i,j\}
,

(3.3)

\widetilde{R}^{(S)}_{ij}=\widetilde{R}^{(kS)}_{ij}+\frac{\widetilde{R}^{(S)}_{ik}\widetilde{R}^{(S)}_{kj}}{\widetilde{R}^{(S)}_{kk}},\qquad\frac{1}{\widetilde{R}_{ii}^{(S)}}=\frac{1}{\widetilde{R}^{(kS)}_{ii}}-\frac{\widetilde{R}^{(S)}_{ik}\widetilde{R}^{(S)}_{ki}}{\widetilde{R}^{(kS)}_{ii}\widetilde{R}^{(S)}_{kk}\widetilde{R}_{ii}^{(S)}}.

(c)

(Sherman-Morrison) For any
i\notin S
,

(3.4)

R^{(S)}=R^{(iS)}-\frac{N^{-1}R^{(iS)}\mathbf{g}_{i}\mathbf{g}_{i}^{*}R^{(iS)}}{1+N^{-1}\mathbf{g}_{i}^{*}R^{(iS)}\mathbf{g}_{i}}=R^{(iS)}+z\widetilde{R}^{(S)}_{ii}\cdot N^{-1}R^{(iS)}\mathbf{g}_{i}\mathbf{g}_{i}^{*}R^{(iS)}.

(d)

(Ward’s identity)

(3.5)

\|R^{(S)}\|_{F}^{2}:=\operatorname{Tr}R^{(S)}R^{(S)*}=\frac{\operatorname{Im}\operatorname{Tr}R^{(S)}}{\operatorname{Im}z}.

Proof.

Parts (a–b) follow from applying Schur’s complement to the
linearized resolvent
\Pi(z)
. Part (c) is the standard Sherman-Morrison formula
for a rank-one update of the matrix inverse
R^{(S)}
.
Part (d) follows from the identity

R^{(S)}-R^{(S)*}=R^{(S)}(z-\bar{z})R^{(S)*}=(2\operatorname{Im}{z})R^{(S)}R^{(S)*}
.
∎

Lemma 3.8

(Concentration)
.

Under Assumptions
1
and
2
, we have

(a)

(Linear and quadratic forms)
Uniformly over all deterministic
A\in\mathbb{C}^{n\times n}
,
\mathbf{u}\in\mathbb{C}^{n}
,
and
i,j\in[N]
with
i\neq j
,

\mathbf{g}_{i}^{*}A\mathbf{g}_{j}\prec\|A\|_{F},\quad\|A\mathbf{g}_{i}\|_{2}\prec\|A\|_{F},\quad\mathbf{u}^{*}\mathbf{g}_{i}\prec\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}.

(b)

(Operator norm)
For every
S\subseteq[N]
,
\|K^{(S)}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\|K\|_{\mathrm{op}}\prec 1
.

Proof.

For (a), we have
|\mathbf{u}^{*}\mathbf{g}_{i}|^{2}=\mathbf{g}_{i}^{*}\mathbf{u}\mathbf{u}^{*}\mathbf{g}_{i}=\mathbf{u}^{*}\Sigma\mathbf{u}+O_{\prec}(\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}^{2})=O_{\prec}(\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}^{2})
, where the second equality follows from
Assumption
2
applied to the matrix
\mathbf{u}\mathbf{u}^{*}
. Thus

\mathbf{u}^{*}\mathbf{g}_{i}\prec\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}
. Next, since
\mathbf{g}_{i}
and
\mathbf{g}_{j}
are independent, this
implies

\mathbf{g}_{i}^{*}A\mathbf{g}_{j}\prec\|A\mathbf{g}_{j}\|_{2}.

Moreover,

\|A\mathbf{g}_{j}\|_{2}^{2}=\mathbf{g}_{j}^{*}A^{*}A\mathbf{g}_{j}=\operatorname{Tr}\Sigma A^{*}A+O_{\prec}(\|A^{*}A\|_{F})=O_{\prec}(\|A\|_{F}^{2}),

so
\|A\mathbf{g}_{j}\|_{2}\prec\|A\|_{F}
and thus
\mathbf{g}_{i}^{*}A\mathbf{g}_{j}\prec\|A\|_{F}
. This proves (a).

For (b), note that
0\preceq K^{(S)}\preceq K
in the positive-definite
ordering, so
\|K^{(S)}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\|K\|_{\mathrm{op}}
. To show
\|K\|_{\mathrm{op}}\prec 1
, define
A_{i}=N^{-1}(\mathbf{g}_{i}\mathbf{g}_{i}^{*}-\Sigma)
. Then
K=\sum_{i=1}^{N}A_{i}+\Sigma
.
By Assumption
1
,
\|\Sigma\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq C
, so it
suffices to show
\|\sum_{i=1}^{N}A_{i}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\prec 1
. Since the
A_{i}
are
independent, mean-zero, symmetric random matrices, the matrix Rosenthal inequality implies that for any
k\geq 1
, there exists a constant
C_{k}>0
such that

\mathbb{E}\,\frac{1}{N}\operatorname{Tr}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{N}A_{i}\right)^{2k}\leq C_{k}\underbrace{\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathbb{E}A_{i}^{2}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{k}}_{=I}+C_{k}\underbrace{\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathbb{E}\|A_{i}\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{2k}}_{=II}.

By Assumption
1
, for each
i\in[N]
,

\|A_{i}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq N^{-1}\|\mathbf{g}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}+N^{-1}\|\Sigma\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq N^{-1}\|\mathbf{g}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}+C/N
.
Moreover,
\|\mathbf{g}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}=\mathbf{g}_{i}^{*}I_{n}\mathbf{g}_{i}=\operatorname{Tr}\Sigma+O_{\prec}(\|I_{n}\|_{F})=O_{\prec}(n)
,
so
N^{-1}\|\mathbf{g}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}\prec 1
. Thus
\|A_{i}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\prec 1
. It follows that
for any fixed
k\geq 1
,
\|A_{i}\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{2k}\prec 1
and hence
II\prec N
.

For the first term
I
, consider any unit vector
\mathbf{v}\in\mathbb{C}^{n}
. Then,
since
0\preceq\mathbb{E}A_{i}^{2}=N^{-2}[\mathbb{E}(\mathbf{g}_{i}\mathbf{g}_{i}^{*})^{2}-\Sigma^{2}]\preceq N^{-2}\mathbb{E}(\mathbf{g}_{i}\mathbf{g}_{i}^{*})^{2}
, we have

\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathbf{v}^{*}\mathbb{E}A_{i}^{2}\mathbf{v}\leq\sum_{i=1}^{N}N^{-2}\mathbb{E}\mathbf{v}^{*}(\mathbf{g}_{i}\mathbf{g}_{i}^{*})^{2}\mathbf{v}
.
Since also
\mathbf{v}^{*}\mathbf{g}_{i}\prec 1
and
\|\mathbf{g}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}\prec n
as shown above,
this shows
\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathbf{v}^{*}\mathbb{E}A_{i}^{2}\mathbf{v}\prec 1
. Thus
for any fixed
k\geq 1
,
\|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}A_{i}^{2}\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{k}\prec 1
and hence

I\prec 1
. Putting this together yields

\mathbb{E}\,\frac{1}{N}\operatorname{Tr}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{N}A_{i}\right)^{2k}\prec N.

The claim now follows from Markov’s inequality: For any
\varepsilon,D>0
, there exist
constants
k=k(\varepsilon,D)>0
and
C\equiv C(\varepsilon,D)>0
large enough such that

\mathbb{P}\left(\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{N}A_{i}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}\geq N^{\varepsilon}\right)\leq N^{-2\varepsilon k}\mathbb{E}\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{N}A_{i}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{2k}\leq N^{-2\varepsilon k}\mathbb{E}\operatorname{Tr}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{N}A_{i}\right)^{2k}\leq CN^{-2\varepsilon k}N^{2+\varepsilon}\leq CN^{-D}.

∎

3.2.
Sherman-Morrison recursions

For any
A\in\mathbb{C}^{n\times n}
,
\mathbf{u}\in\mathbb{C}^{n}
,
S\subset[N]
, and
i,j,k\in[N]\setminus S
, define

\displaystyle\mathcal{Y}_{i}^{(S)}[A]

\displaystyle=\operatorname{Tr}(\mathbf{g}_{i}\mathbf{g}_{i}^{*}-\Sigma)\frac{R^{(S)}A}{\sqrt{N}},

\displaystyle\mathcal{Z}_{ijk}^{(S)}[A]

\displaystyle=\operatorname{Tr}(\mathbf{g}_{i}\mathbf{g}_{i}^{*}-\Sigma)\frac{R^{(S)}}{N}\mathbf{g}_{j}\mathbf{g}_{k}^{*}\frac{R^{(S)}A}{\sqrt{N}},\quad i\notin\{j,k\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{X}_{i}^{(S)}[\mathbf{u}]

\displaystyle=\mathbf{g}_{i}^{*}\frac{R^{(S)}\mathbf{u}}{\sqrt{N}},

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}_{jk}^{(S)}

\displaystyle=\mathbf{g}_{j}^{*}\frac{R^{(S)}}{N}\mathbf{g}_{k},\quad j\neq k,

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{i}^{(S)}

\displaystyle=\operatorname{Tr}(\mathbf{g}_{i}\mathbf{g}_{i}^{*}-\Sigma)\frac{R^{(S)}}{N},

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}_{i}^{(S)}

\displaystyle=\Big(1+\mathbf{g}_{i}^{*}\frac{R^{(S)}}{N}\mathbf{g}_{i}\Big)^{-1},

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}^{(S)}

\displaystyle=\Big(1+\operatorname{Tr}\Sigma\frac{R^{(S)}}{N}\Big)^{-1}.

Note that each
R^{(S)}
in the expressions is scaled by
N^{-1}
, and each

R^{(S)}A
and
R^{(S)}\mathbf{u}
is scaled by
N^{-1/2}
.
We remark that the vectors
\mathbf{g}_{i}
are real and
R^{(S)}
is symmetric (in the
real sense, without complex conjugation) so
\mathcal{B}_{jk}^{(S)}=\mathcal{B}_{kj}^{(S)}
.

We first establish basic bounds for these quantities under the conditions of
Lemmas
3.4
and

3.5
.

Lemma 3.9
.

Suppose the conditions of Lemma
3.4
hold.
Then for any fixed
L\geq 1
,
uniformly over all
S\subseteq[N]
with
|S|\leq L
, all
i,j,k\in[N]\setminus S
with
i\notin\{j,k\}
, all
A\in\mathbb{C}^{n\times n}
with

\|A\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq 1
, all unit vectors
\mathbf{u}\in\mathbb{C}^{n}
, and all
z=E+i\eta\in\mathbf{D}
,
we have

|\mathcal{Y}_{i}^{(S)}[A]|\prec N^{1/2}\Phi,\quad|\mathcal{Z}_{ijk}^{(S)}[A]|\prec N^{1/2}\Phi^{2},

|\mathcal{B}_{ij}^{(S)}|\prec\Phi,\quad|\mathcal{P}_{i}^{(S)}|\prec\Phi,\quad|\mathcal{Q}_{i}^{(S)}|\prec 1,\quad|\mathcal{C}^{(S)}|\prec 1.

If, in addition, the condition
N^{-1/2}\|R^{(S)}\mathbf{u}\|_{2}\prec\Phi
of Lemma

3.5
holds, then

|\mathcal{Y}_{i}^{(S)}[\mathbf{u}\mathbf{u}^{*}]|\prec\Phi,\quad|\mathcal{Z}_{ijk}^{(S)}[\mathbf{u}\mathbf{u}^{*}]|\prec\Phi^{2},\quad|\mathcal{X}_{i}^{(S)}[\mathbf{u}]|\prec\Phi.

Proof.

For the first two bounds for
\mathcal{Y}_{i}^{(S)}
and
\mathcal{Z}_{ijk}^{(S)}
,
Assumption
2
and Lemma
3.8
(a) give

\displaystyle|\mathcal{Y}_{i}^{(S)}[A]|

\displaystyle\prec N^{-1/2}\|R^{(S)}A\|_{F},

\displaystyle|\mathcal{Z}_{ijk}^{(S)}[A]|

\displaystyle\prec\|N^{-1}R^{(S)}\mathbf{g}_{j}\mathbf{g}_{k}^{*}N^{-1/2}R^{(S)}A\|_{F}\prec N^{-1}\|R^{(S)}\|_{F}\cdot N^{-1/2}\|R^{(S)}A\|_{F}.

Since
\|R^{(S)}A\|_{F}\leq\|R^{(S)}\|_{F}\|A\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\|R^{(S)}\|_{F}
and

N^{-1}\|R^{(S)}\|_{F}\prec\Phi
, the first two bounds follow.
For a unit vector
\mathbf{u}
and
A=\mathbf{u}\mathbf{u}^{*}
,
we have
\|R^{(S)}\mathbf{u}\mathbf{u}^{*}\|_{F}=\|R^{(S)}\mathbf{u}\|_{2}
, so
under the additional assumption
N^{-1/2}\|R^{(S)}\mathbf{u}\|_{2}\prec\Phi
,
this shows
|\mathcal{Y}_{i}^{(S)}[\mathbf{u}\mathbf{u}^{*}]|\prec\Phi
and
|\mathcal{Z}_{ijk}^{(S)}[\mathbf{u}\mathbf{u}^{*}]|\prec\Phi
as well as

|\mathcal{X}_{i}^{(S)}[\mathbf{u}]|\prec N^{-1/2}\|R^{(S)}\mathbf{u}\|_{2}\prec\Phi.

The bounds
|\mathcal{B}_{ij}^{(S)}|\prec\Phi
and
|\mathcal{P}_{i}^{(S)}|\prec\Phi
follow
directly from Assumption
2
and the condition

N^{-1}\|R^{(S)}\|_{F}\prec\Phi
.
For
\mathcal{Q}_{i}^{(S)}
and
\mathcal{C}^{(S)}
, Lemma
3.7
(a)
and the condition
\Gamma^{(S)}\prec N^{-\tau^{\prime}}
imply

\mathcal{Q}_{i}^{(S)}=-z\widetilde{R}_{ii}^{(S\setminus\{i\})}=-z\widetilde{m}_{0}+O_{\prec}(\Gamma^{(S\setminus\{i\})})=-z\widetilde{m}_{0}+O_{\prec}(N^{-\tau^{\prime}})
.
The conditions
|z|\asymp 1
and
|\widetilde{m}_{0}(z)|\asymp 1

in Definition
2.3
for regularity of
\mathbf{D}
then imply that
for some constants
C,c>0
and any
D>0
,

\mathbb{P}[c\leq|\mathcal{Q}_{i}^{(S)}|\leq C]\geq 1-C(D)n^{-D}.

This shows
|\mathcal{Q}_{i}^{(S)}|\prec 1
. Moreover,

(\mathcal{Q}_{i}^{(S)})^{-1}=1+\mathbf{g}_{i}^{*}(N^{-1}R^{(S)})\mathbf{g}_{i}=1+\operatorname{Tr}\Sigma(N^{-1}R^{(S)})+\operatorname{Tr}(\mathbf{g}_{i}\mathbf{g}_{i}^{*}-\Sigma)(N^{-1}R^{(S)})=(\mathcal{C}^{(S)})^{-1}+O_{\prec}(\Psi).

Since
\Psi\leq N^{-\tau^{\prime}}
, the above bounds for
|\mathcal{Q}_{i}^{(S)}|
then also
imply
|\mathcal{C}^{(S)}|\prec 1
.
∎

The following lemma describes a system of recursions for these quantities,
derived from the Sherman-Morrison identity (
3.4
).

Lemma 3.10
.

For any
A\in\mathbb{C}^{n\times n}
,
\mathbf{u}\in\mathbb{C}^{n}
,
S\subseteq[N]
,

i,j,k,l\in[N]\setminus S
with
l\notin\{i,j,k\}
and
i\notin\{j,k\}
, and
z\in\mathbb{C}^{+}
, we have

(3.6)

\displaystyle\mathcal{Y}_{i}^{(S)}[A]

\displaystyle=\mathcal{Y}_{i}^{(Sl)}[A]-\mathcal{Z}_{ill}^{(Sl)}[A]\mathcal{Q}_{l}^{(Sl)},

(3.7)

\displaystyle\mathcal{Z}_{ijk}^{(S)}[A]

\displaystyle=\mathcal{Z}_{ijk}^{(Sl)}[A]-\mathcal{Z}_{ijl}^{(Sl)}[A]\mathcal{B}_{kl}^{(Sl)}\mathcal{Q}_{l}^{(Sl)}-\mathcal{Z}_{ilk}^{(Sl)}[A]\mathcal{B}_{lj}^{(Sl)}\mathcal{Q}_{l}^{(Sl)}+\mathcal{Z}_{ill}^{(Sl)}[A]\mathcal{B}_{kl}^{(Sl)}\mathcal{B}_{lj}^{(Sl)}(\mathcal{Q}_{l}^{(Sl)})^{2},

(3.8)

\displaystyle\mathcal{Z}_{ikk}^{(S)}[A]

\displaystyle=\mathcal{Z}_{ikk}^{(Sl)}[A]-\mathcal{Z}_{ikl}^{(Sl)}[A]\mathcal{B}_{kl}^{(Sl)}\mathcal{Q}_{l}^{(Sl)}-\mathcal{Z}_{ilk}^{(Sl)}[A]\mathcal{B}_{lk}^{(Sl)}\mathcal{Q}_{l}^{(Sl)}+\mathcal{Z}_{ill}^{(Sl)}[A]\mathcal{B}_{kl}^{(Sl)}\mathcal{B}_{lk}^{(Sl)}(\mathcal{Q}_{l}^{(Sl)})^{2},

(3.9)

\displaystyle\mathcal{X}_{i}^{(S)}[\mathbf{u}]

\displaystyle=\mathcal{X}_{i}^{(Sl)}[\mathbf{u}]-\mathcal{X}_{l}^{(Sl)}[\mathbf{u}]\mathcal{B}_{il}^{(Sl)},

(3.10)

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}_{ij}^{(S)}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{B}_{ij}^{(Sl)}-\mathcal{B}_{il}^{(Sl)}\mathcal{B}_{lj}^{(Sl)}\mathcal{Q}_{l}^{(Sl)}.

Moreover, fix any constants
L\geq 1
and
D>0
.
If the conditions of Lemma
3.4
hold, then
uniformly over all
z\in\mathbf{D}
,
S\subseteq[N]
with
|S|\leq L
, and
i,l\in[N]\setminus S
with
i\neq l
,

(3.11)

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}_{i}^{(S)}

\displaystyle=\sum_{m=0}^{D}(\mathcal{Q}_{i}^{(Sl)})^{m+1}(\mathcal{B}_{il}^{(Sl)}\mathcal{B}_{li}^{(Sl)}\mathcal{Q}_{l}^{(Sl)})^{m}+O_{\prec}(N^{-2\tau^{\prime}(D+1)}),

(3.12)

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}_{i}^{(S)}

\displaystyle=\sum_{m=0}^{D}(\mathcal{C}^{(S)})^{m+1}(-\mathcal{P}_{i}^{(S)})^{m}+O_{\prec}(N^{-\tau^{\prime}(D+1)}).

Proof.

The identities (
3.6
-
3.10
) follow directly
from applying the Sherman-Morrison formula (
3.4
) to the
left sides. For (
3.11
), by Lemma
3.9

we have

|\mathcal{Q}_{i}^{(S)}|,|\mathcal{C}^{(S)}|\prec 1,\quad|\mathcal{B}_{ij}^{(S)}|,|\mathcal{P}_{i}^{(S)}|\prec\Phi\leq N^{-\tau^{\prime}}.

Applying Sherman-Morrison to
(\mathcal{Q}_{i}^{(S)})^{-1}
gives

(\mathcal{Q}_{i}^{(S)})^{-1}=1+\mathbf{g}_{i}^{*}(N^{-1}R^{(S)})\mathbf{g}_{i}=(\mathcal{Q}_{i}^{(Sl)})^{-1}-\mathcal{B}_{il}^{(Sl)}\mathcal{B}_{li}^{(Sl)}\mathcal{Q}_{l}^{(Sl)}.

Using the identity for scalar values
a,b\neq 0

(3.13)

a^{-1}=\sum_{m=0}^{D}b^{-(m+1)}(b-a)^{m}+a^{-1}b^{-(D+1)}(b-a)^{D+1},

we obtain

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}_{i}^{(S)}

\displaystyle=\sum_{m=0}^{D}(\mathcal{Q}_{i}^{(Sl)})^{m+1}(\mathcal{B}_{il}^{(Sl)}\mathcal{B}_{li}^{(Sl)}\mathcal{Q}_{l}^{(Sl)})^{m}+(\mathcal{Q}_{i}^{(S)})(\mathcal{Q}_{i}^{(Sl)})^{D+1}(\mathcal{B}_{il}^{(Sl)}\mathcal{B}_{li}^{(Sl)}\mathcal{Q}_{l}^{(Sl)})^{D+1}

\displaystyle=\sum_{m=0}^{D}(\mathcal{Q}_{i}^{(Sl)})^{m+1}(\mathcal{B}_{il}^{(Sl)}\mathcal{B}_{li}^{(Sl)}\mathcal{Q}_{l}^{(Sl)})^{m}+O_{\prec}(N^{-2\tau^{\prime}(D+1)}),

since
|\mathcal{B}_{il}^{(Sl)}\mathcal{B}_{li}^{(Sl)}\mathcal{Q}_{l}^{(Sl)}|\prec N^{-2\tau^{\prime}}
and

|\mathcal{Q}_{i}^{(S)}|,|\mathcal{Q}_{i}^{(Sl)}|\prec 1
. For (
3.12
), apply (
3.13
) to

(\mathcal{Q}_{i}^{(S)})^{-1}=1+\mathbf{g}_{i}^{*}(N^{-1}R^{(S)})\mathbf{g}_{i}=(\mathcal{C}^{(S)})^{-1}+\mathcal{P}_{i}^{(S)},

yielding similarly

\mathcal{Q}_{i}^{(S)}=\sum_{m=0}^{D}(\mathcal{C}^{(S)})^{m+1}(-\mathcal{P}_{i}^{(S)})^{m}+O_{\prec}(N^{-\tau^{\prime}(D+1)}).

∎

3.3.
Proof of Lemma
3.4

We fix a matrix
A\in\mathbb{C}^{n\times n}
with
\|A\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq 1
, and abbreviate

\mathcal{Y}_{i}^{(S)}=\mathcal{Y}_{i}^{(S)}[A],\qquad\mathcal{Z}_{ijk}^{(S)}=\mathcal{Z}_{ijk}^{(S)}[A].

All subsequent
O_{\prec}(\cdot)
bounds are implicitly uniform over all such
matrices
A
.

The following lemma characterizes the combinatorics of an expansion of

\mathcal{Y}_{i}^{(i)}\mathcal{Q}_{i}^{(i)}
to resolve the dependence on variables

\{\mathbf{g}_{j}\}_{j\in S}
in a set
S\subset[N]
, using the recursions of
Lemma
3.10
.

Lemma 3.11
.

Suppose the conditions of Lemma
3.4
hold.
Fix any
L\geq 1
and
D>0
. Then uniformly over
z\in\mathbf{D}
,

S\subset[N]
with
|S|\leq L
, and
i\in S
, the following holds:

Denote
S^{(i)}=S\setminus\{i\}
. There exists a collection of monomials

\mathcal{M}_{i,S}
such that the quantity

\mathcal{Y}_{i}^{(i)}\mathcal{Q}_{i}^{(i)}
can be expanded as

\displaystyle\mathcal{Y}_{i}^{(i)}\mathcal{Q}_{i}^{(i)}

\displaystyle=\sum_{q\in\mathcal{M}_{i,S}}q\left(\mathcal{Y}_{i}^{(S)},\{\mathcal{Z}_{ijk}^{(S)}\}_{j,k\in S^{(i)}},\{\mathcal{B}_{ij}^{(S)}\}_{j\in S^{(i)}},\{\mathcal{B}_{jk}^{(S)}\}_{j\neq k\in S^{(i)}},\mathcal{P}_{i}^{(S)},\{\mathcal{P}_{j}^{(S)}\}_{j\in S^{(i)}},\mathcal{C}^{(S)}\right)

(3.14)

\displaystyle\hskip 72.26999pt+O_{\prec}(N^{-\tau^{\prime}(D+1)+1/2}).

Each monomial
q\in\mathcal{M}_{i,S}
is a product of
\pm 1
and one or more of its
inputs, allowing repetition.
We have
q=O_{\prec}(N^{1/2}\Phi)
uniformly over
q\in\mathcal{M}_{i,S}
, and the
number of monomials
|\mathcal{M}_{i,S}|
is at most a constant depending only on

L,D
.

Furthermore, for each
q\in\mathcal{M}_{i,S}
, letting
m_{Y},m_{Z},m_{B}^{*},m_{B}

denote the numbers of factors of the forms
\mathcal{Y}_{i}^{(S)}
,

\{\mathcal{Z}_{ijk}^{(S)}\}_{j,k\in S^{(i)}}
,

\{\mathcal{B}_{ij}^{(S)}\}_{j\in S^{(i)}}
, and

\{\mathcal{B}_{jk}^{(S)}\}_{j\neq k\in S^{(i)}}
in
q
, we have:

(a)

m_{Y}+m_{Z}=1
.

(b)

m_{B}^{*}
is even.

(c)

The number of distinct indices of
S^{(i)}
that appear as lower
indices across all factors of
q
is at most
m_{Z}+m_{B}+\frac{1}{2}m_{B}^{*}
.

Proof.

We arbitrarily order the indices of
S^{(i)}
as

l_{1},\ldots,l_{|S|-1}
. Beginning with the term
\mathcal{Y}_{i}^{(i)}\mathcal{Q}_{i}^{(i)}
,
iteratively for
j=1,\ldots,|S|-1
, we replace all factors with superscript

(il_{1}\ldots l_{j-1})
by a sum of terms with superscript
(il_{1}\ldots l_{j})
,
using the recursions (
3.6
-
3.8
) for
\mathcal{Y}

and
\mathcal{Z}
, (
3.10
) for
\mathcal{B}
, and (
3.11
) for

\mathcal{Q}
. After we have replaced all superscripts to be
(S)=(il_{1}\ldots l_{|S|-1})
,
we then apply the recursion (
3.12
) for
\mathcal{Q}
to replace each
factor
\mathcal{Q}_{l}^{(S)}
by factors
\mathcal{C}^{(S)}
and
\mathcal{P}_{l}^{(S)}
. It is then direct to check that this gives a representation of the form (
3.13
), where:

•

Each application of (
3.6
-
3.8
)
replaces a factor
\mathcal{Y}_{i}^{(\ldots)}
or
\mathcal{Z}_{ijk}^{(\ldots)}
by
terms having exactly one such factor. Thus, each monomial
q\in\mathcal{M}_{i,S}
has
exactly one factor
\mathcal{Y}_{i}^{(S)}
or
\mathcal{Z}_{ijk}^{(S)}
, i.e.
m_{Y}+m_{Z}=1
.

•

The number of total applications of (
3.6
-
3.8
), (
3.10
),
(
3.11
), and (
3.12
) is bounded by a constant
depending on
L,D
, so
|\mathcal{M}_{i,S}|
and the number of factors of each
q\in\mathcal{M}_{i,S}
are also bounded by constants depending on
L,D
.
By the bounds of Lemma
3.9
, the factor

\mathcal{Y}_{i}^{(S)}
or
\mathcal{Z}_{ijk}^{(S)}
of
q
is
O_{\prec}(N^{1/2}\Phi)
, and all
other factors are
O_{\prec}(1)
. Thus
each
q\in\mathcal{M}_{i,S}
satisfies
O_{\prec}(N^{1/2}\Phi)
, and the remainder in
(
3.13
) is at most
O_{\prec}(N^{-\tau^{\prime}(D+1)+1/2})
.

•

Each application of (
3.11
) replaces
\mathcal{Q}_{i}^{(\ldots)}

or
\mathcal{Q}_{j}^{(\ldots)}

by terms having an even number of factors
\{\mathcal{B}_{ij}^{(\ldots)}\}_{j\in S^{(i)}}
, and each application of
(
3.6
-
3.8
) does not change the number
of factors of the form
\{\mathcal{B}_{ij}^{(\ldots)}\}_{j\in S^{(i)}}
.
Thus
m_{B}^{*}
is even.

•

If an index of
S^{(i)}
appears as a lower index on the left side of (
3.6
-
3.8
),
(
3.10
), or (
3.11
), then it also appears as
a lower index on some factor of each term of the right side.
Furthermore, each term on the
right side of (
3.6
-
3.8
) and
(
3.10
) that contains any factor
with the new lower index
l
has at least one more factor of the form

\{\mathcal{Z}_{ijk}^{(\ldots)}\}_{j,k\in S^{(i)}}
or
\{\mathcal{B}_{jk}^{(\ldots)}\}_{j\neq k\in S^{(i)}}
than the left side, and similarly each term on the right side of
(
3.11
) that contains the new lower index
l
has at least two
more factors of the form
\{\mathcal{B}_{ij}^{(\ldots)}\}_{j\in S^{(i)}}

than the left side. I.e., whenever a new
lower index of
S^{(i)}
is introduced, either
m_{Z}+m_{B}
increases by at least
1, or
m_{B}^{*}
increases by at least 2. Thus the number of distinct lower indices
of
S^{(i)}
across all factors of
q
is at most
m_{Z}+m_{B}+\frac{1}{2}m_{B}^{*}
.

Combining these observations yields the lemma.
∎

Proof of Lemma
3.4
.

Fix constants
L\geq 1
and
D>0
. A high moment expansion gives

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left|\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathcal{Y}_{i}^{(i)}\mathcal{Q}_{i}^{(i)}\right|^{2L}

\displaystyle=N^{-L}\sum_{i_{1},\ldots,i_{2L}=1}^{N}\underbrace{\mathbb{E}\prod_{l=1}^{L}\mathcal{Y}_{i_{l}}^{(i_{l})}\mathcal{Q}_{i_{l}}^{(i_{l})}\prod_{l=L+1}^{2L}\overline{\mathcal{Y}_{i_{l}}^{(i_{l})}\mathcal{Q}_{i_{l}}^{(i_{l})}}}_{\mathbb{E}m(i_{1},\ldots,i_{2L})}

Let
S=\{i_{1},\ldots,i_{2L}\}
denote the set of distinct indices in

i_{1},\ldots,i_{2L}
. Applying Lemma
3.11

to expand each
\mathcal{Y}_{i_{l}}^{(i_{l})}\mathcal{Q}_{i_{l}}^{(i_{l})}
over
S
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}m(i_{1},\ldots,i_{2L})

\displaystyle=\sum_{q_{1}\in\mathcal{M}_{i_{1},S}}\cdots\sum_{q_{2L}\in\mathcal{M}_{i_{2L,S}}}\mathbb{E}\underbrace{\prod_{l=1}^{L}q_{l}\prod_{l=L+1}^{2L}\bar{q}_{l}}_{:=q}+O_{\prec}(N^{-\tau^{\prime}(D+1)+L}).

Hence, noting that the number of index tuples
(i_{1},\ldots,i_{2L})
with

|\{i_{1},\ldots,i_{2L}\}|=s
is at most
C(L)N^{s}
for some
constant
C(L)>0
,

(3.15)

\mathbb{E}\left|\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathcal{Y}_{i}^{(i)}\mathcal{Q}_{i}^{(i)}\right|^{2L}\prec\max_{\begin{subarray}{c}i_{1},\ldots,i_{2L}\in[N]\\
q_{1}\in\mathcal{M}_{i_{1},S},\ldots,q_{2L}\in\mathcal{M}_{i_{2L},S}\end{subarray}}\left\{N^{|S|-L}\mathbb{E}|\mathbb{E}_{S}q|\right\}+N^{-\tau^{\prime}(D+1)+L}

where
\mathbb{E}_{S}
denotes the partial expectation over
\{\mathbf{g}_{i}:i\in S\}
.

We now bound
\mathbb{E}_{S}q=\mathbb{E}_{S}\prod_{l=1}^{L}q_{l}\prod_{l=L+1}^{2L}\bar{q}_{l}
.
Let
K\subseteq S
correspond to the indices appearing exactly once in the list

(i_{1},\ldots,i_{2L})
. For each
l\in[2L]
, let

m_{Z}(l),m_{B}(l),m_{B}^{*}(l)
denote the counts
m_{Z},m_{B},m_{B}^{*}
of Lemma

3.11
for
q_{l}
, and let
m_{P}^{*}(l)
denote also the number
of factors
\mathcal{P}_{i_{l}}^{(S)}
in
q_{l}
. Set

(3.16)

m_{Z}=\sum_{l=1}^{2L}m_{Z}(l),\quad m_{B}=\sum_{l=1}^{2L}m_{B}(l),\quad m_{B}^{*}=\sum_{l=1}^{2L}m_{B}^{*}(l),\quad m_{P}^{*}=\sum_{l=1}^{2L}m_{P}^{*}(l).

We consider three cases for each index
i_{l}\in K
:

•

i_{l}
does not appear as a lower index on any factor of
q_{l}
other than

\mathcal{Y}_{i_{l}}^{(S)}
or
\mathcal{Z}_{i_{l}jk}^{(S)}
, or on any factor of

\{q_{l^{\prime}}:l^{\prime}\neq l\}
. In this case, Lemma
3.11
(a)
implies that the only factor of
q

which depends on
\mathbf{g}_{i_{l}}
is the (exactly one) factor
\mathcal{Y}_{i_{l}}^{(S)}
or

\mathcal{Z}_{i_{l}jk}^{(S)}
of
q_{l}
. Since

\mathbb{E}_{i_{l}}[\mathcal{Y}_{i_{l}}^{(S)}]=\mathbb{E}_{i_{l}}[\mathcal{Z}_{i_{l}jk}^{(S)}]=0
, it follows
that
\mathbb{E}_{S}q=0
.

•

i_{l}
appears as a lower index in some
\{q_{l^{\prime}}:l^{\prime}\neq l\}
. Since
i_{l}

is distinct from
i_{l^{\prime}}
for each
l^{\prime}\neq l
and hence belongs to

S^{(i_{l}^{\prime})}
, Lemma
3.11
(c)
ensures that the total number of such indices
i_{l}\in K

is at most
m_{Z}+m_{B}+\frac{1}{2}m_{B}^{*}
.

•

i_{l}
does not appear as a lower index on any
\{q_{l^{\prime}}:l^{\prime}\neq l\}
,
but appears as a lower index on at least one factor
\mathcal{P}_{i_{l}}^{(S)}

or
\{\mathcal{B}_{i_{l}j}^{(S)}\}_{j\in S^{(i_{l})}}
of
q_{l}
. Then either
m_{P}^{*}(l)\geq 1
, or
m_{P}^{*}(l)=0
in which case Lemma
3.11
(b)
ensures that
m_{B}^{*}(l)\geq 2
. So the number of such indices
i_{l}\in K

is at most
m_{P}^{*}+\frac{1}{2}m_{B}^{*}
.

Combining these cases, we see that either
\mathbb{E}_{S}q=0
, or

m_{Z}+m_{B}+\frac{1}{2}m_{B}^{*}+m_{P}^{*}+\frac{1}{2}m_{B}^{*}=m_{Z}+m_{B}+m_{B}^{*}+m_{P}^{*}\geq|K|.

In the latter case, using Lemma
3.9
to bound

|\mathcal{Y}_{i}^{(S)}|\prec N^{1/2}\Phi
,

|\mathcal{Z}_{ijk}^{(S)}|\prec N^{1/2}\Phi^{2}
,

|\mathcal{B}_{ij}^{(S)}|\prec\Phi
(for the factors counted by both
m_{B}
and

m_{B}^{*}
),
|\mathcal{P}_{i}^{(S)}|\prec\Phi
,
and each other factor of
q
by
O_{\prec}(1)
, since
q
has exactly
2L
factors

|\mathcal{Y}_{i}^{(S)}|
and
|\mathcal{Z}_{ijk}^{(S)}|
by Lemma
3.11
(a),
we get

(3.17)

|\mathbb{E}_{S}q|\prec\Phi^{|K|}(N^{1/2}\Phi)^{2L}.

Hence

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left|\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathcal{Y}_{i}^{(i)}\mathcal{Q}_{i}^{(i)}\right|^{2L}\prec N^{|S|}\Phi^{|K|+2L}+N^{-\tau^{\prime}(D+1)+L}.

Since indices in
K
appear exactly once, the remaining
2L-|K|
indices must
appear at least twice, so
(2L-|K|)/2+|K|=2L+|K|/2\geq|S|

(the number of distinct indices). Thus
N^{|S|}\Phi^{|K|+2L}\leq(N\Phi^{2})^{|K|/2+L}\leq(N\Phi^{2})^{2L}
, where the last
inequality uses
|K|\leq 2L
. Since
\Phi\geq N^{-1/2}
, this is larger than
the second term
N^{-\tau^{\prime}(D+1)+L}
for a sufficiently large choice of
constant
D\equiv D(\tau^{\prime},L)>0
. Thus

\mathbb{E}\left|\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathcal{Y}_{i}^{(i)}\mathcal{Q}_{i}^{(i)}\right|^{2L}\prec(N\Phi^{2})^{2L}.

Then Markov’s inequality implies

|N^{-1/2}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathcal{Y}_{i}^{(i)}\mathcal{Q}_{i}^{(i)}|\prec N\Phi^{2}
, which shows
Lemma
3.4
.
∎

3.4.
Proof of Lemma
3.5

Fixing a unit vector
\mathbf{u}\in\mathbb{C}^{n}
, let us now abbreviate

\mathcal{Y}_{i}^{(S)}=\mathcal{Y}_{i}^{(S)}[\mathbf{u}\mathbf{u}^{*}],\qquad\mathcal{Z}_{ijk}^{(S)}=\mathcal{Z}_{ijk}^{(S)}[\mathbf{u}\mathbf{u}^{*}],\qquad\mathcal{X}_{i}^{(S)}=\mathcal{X}_{i}^{(S)}[\mathbf{u}].

All subsequent
O_{\prec}(\cdot)
bounds are implicitly uniform in
\mathbf{u}
.
We now show Lemma
3.5
under the additional
condition that
N^{-1/2}\|R^{(S)}\mathbf{u}\|_{2}\prec\Phi
.

Proof of Lemma
3.5
(a).

The proof is identical to Lemma
3.4
, where now
by Lemma
3.9
we have the bounds

|\mathcal{Y}_{i}^{(S)}|\prec\Phi
and
|\mathcal{Z}_{ijk}^{(S)}|\prec\Phi^{2}
. This gives,
instead of (
3.17
),
|\mathbb{E}_{S}q|\prec\Phi^{|K|}\Phi^{2L}
, and hence

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left|\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathcal{Y}_{i}^{(i)}\mathcal{Q}_{i}^{(i)}\right|^{2L}\prec N^{|S|-L}\Phi^{|K|+2L}+N^{-\tau^{\prime}(D+1)+L}\leq N^{-L}(N\Phi^{2})^{2L}+N^{-\tau^{\prime}(D+1)+L}

Choosing
D
large enough and applying Markov’s inequality shows
Lemma
3.5
(a).
∎

We next show Lemma
3.5
(b).
The following lemma is similar to Lemma
3.11
.

Lemma 3.12
.

Suppose the conditions of Lemma
3.5
hold.
Fix any
L\geq 1
and
D>0
. Then uniformly over
z\in\mathbf{D}
,
S\subset[N]
with
|S|\leq L
,
and
i,j\in S
with
i\neq j
, the following holds:

Denote
S^{(ij)}=S\setminus\{i,j\}
. Then there exists a collection of monomials
\mathcal{M}_{ij,S}
such that
\mathcal{B}_{ij}^{(ij)}\mathcal{Q}_{i}^{(ij)}\mathcal{Q}_{j}^{(ij)}
can be expanded as

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}_{ij}^{(ij)}\mathcal{Q}_{i}^{(i)}\mathcal{Q}_{j}^{(ij)}

\displaystyle=\sum_{q\in\mathcal{M}_{ij,S}}q\left(\{\mathcal{B}_{ij}^{(S)}\},\{\mathcal{B}_{ik}^{(S)}\}_{k\in S^{(ij)}},\{\mathcal{B}_{jk}^{(S)}\}_{k\in S^{(ij)}},\{\mathcal{B}_{kl}^{(S)}\}_{k\neq l\in S^{(ij)}},\mathcal{P}_{i}^{(S)},\mathcal{P}_{j}^{(S)},\{\mathcal{P}_{k}^{(S)}\}_{k\in S^{(ij)}},\mathcal{C}^{(S)}\right)

(3.18)

\displaystyle\hskip 72.26999pt+O_{\prec}(N^{-\tau^{\prime}(D+1)}).

Each monomial
q\in\mathcal{M}_{ij,S}
is a product of
\pm 1
and one or more
of its inputs, allowing repetition.
We have
q=O_{\prec}(\Phi)
uniformly over
q\in\mathcal{M}_{ij,S}
, and the number
of monomials
|\mathcal{M}_{ij,S}|
is at most a constant depending only on
L,D
.

Furthermore, for each
q\in\mathcal{M}_{ij,S}
, letting

m_{B}^{**},m_{B}^{*1},m_{B}^{*2},m_{B}
denote the numbers of factors of the forms

\{\mathcal{B}_{ij}^{(S)}\},\{\mathcal{B}_{ik}^{(S)}\}_{k\in S^{(ij)}},\{\mathcal{B}_{jk}^{(S)}\}_{k\in S^{(ij)}},\{\mathcal{B}_{kl}^{(S)}\}_{k\neq l\in S^{(ij)}}
appearing in
q
, we have:

(a)

Either
m_{B}^{**}\geq 1
, or both
m_{B}^{*1}\geq 1
and

m_{B}^{*2}\geq 1
.

(b)

The number of occurrences of
i
as a lower index in all factors

\mathcal{B}_{...}^{(S)}
of
q
(i.e.
m_{B}^{**}+m_{B}^{*1}
) is odd. Similarly,
the number of such occurrences of
j
(i.e.
m_{B}^{**}+m_{B}^{*2}
) is odd.
For each
k\in S^{(ij)}
, the number of
occurrences of
k
as a lower index across all factors

\mathcal{B}_{...}^{(S)}
of
q
is even.

(c)

The number of distinct indices of
S^{(ij)}
that appear as lower indices
across all factors of
q
is at most
m_{B}+\frac{1}{2}(m_{B}^{*1}+m_{B}^{*2})
.

Proof.

We may first apply (
3.11
) to expand
\mathcal{Q}_{i}^{(i)}
in
j
,
to get

(3.19)

\mathcal{B}_{ij}^{(ij)}\mathcal{Q}_{i}^{(i)}\mathcal{Q}_{j}^{(ij)}=\sum_{m=0}^{D}[\mathcal{B}_{ij}^{(ij)}]^{2m+1}[\mathcal{Q}_{i}^{(ij)}]^{m+1}[\mathcal{Q}_{j}^{(ij)}]^{m+1}+O_{\prec}(N^{-2\tau^{\prime}(D+1)}).

We may then successively expand
\mathcal{B}
and
\mathcal{Q}
in the
indices of
S^{(ij)}
using (
3.10
) and (
3.11
), and finally expand each
\mathcal{Q}_{\ldots}^{(S)}
in
\mathcal{C}^{(S)}
and

\mathcal{P}_{\ldots}^{(S)}
using (
3.12
). This gives a representation of
the form (
3.18
).
Since
|\mathcal{B}_{ij}^{(S)}|\prec\Phi
and
|\mathcal{Q}_{i}^{(S)}|\prec 1
, we have

q=O_{\prec}(\Phi)
, and the remainder is
O_{\prec}(N^{-\tau^{\prime}(D+1)})
.

Each term of (
3.19
) has at least one
factor of
\mathcal{B}_{ij}^{(ij)}
. If (
3.10
) is applied to expand

\mathcal{B}_{ij}^{(...)}
, then the resulting terms either have a factor
of
\mathcal{B}_{ij}^{(...)}
or have a factor each of
\mathcal{B}_{ik}^{(...)}
and

\mathcal{B}_{jl}^{(...)}
for some
k,l\in S^{(ij)}
, and this is preserved
in later steps of the expansion. This shows property (a).

Property (b) holds because
i
and
j
each occur an odd number of times as a
lower index of
\mathcal{B}_{...}^{(ij)}
in (
3.19
), and each
k\in S^{(ij)}
occurs 0 times. The parities of these occurrences do not change in the
applications of (
3.10
) and (
3.11
).

Property (c) holds as in Lemma
3.11

because each application of (
3.10
) or (
3.11
) which introduces a new lower index introduces at least one additional
factor of
\{\mathcal{B}_{kl}^{(...)}\}_{k,l\in S^{(ij)}}

or two additional factors of

\{\mathcal{B}_{ik}^{(...)}\}_{k\in S^{(ij)}}\cup\{\mathcal{B}_{jk}^{(...)}\}_{k\in S^{(ij)}}
.
∎

Proof of Lemma
3.5
(b).

Fix any
L\geq 1
and
D>0
. For any unit vector
\mathbf{v}\in\mathbb{R}^{N}
, we may expand

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left|\sum_{i\neq j}\bar{\mathbf{v}}(i)\mathbf{v}(j)\mathcal{B}_{ij}^{(ij)}\mathcal{Q}_{i}^{(i)}\mathcal{Q}_{j}^{(ij)}\right|^{2L}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i_{1}\neq j_{1}}\cdots\sum_{i_{2L}\neq j_{2L}}\prod_{l=1}^{L}\bar{\mathbf{v}}(i_{l})\mathbf{v}(j_{l})\prod_{l=L+1}^{2L}\mathbf{v}(i_{l})\bar{\mathbf{v}}(j_{l})\mathbb{E}\left[\prod_{l=1}^{L}\mathcal{B}_{i_{l}j_{l}}^{(i_{l}j_{l})}\mathcal{Q}_{i_{l}}^{(i_{l})}\mathcal{Q}_{j_{l}}^{(i_{l}j_{l})}\prod_{l=L+1}^{2L}\overline{\mathcal{B}_{i_{l}j_{l}}^{(i_{l}j_{l})}\mathcal{Q}_{i_{l}}^{(i_{l})}\mathcal{Q}_{j_{l}}^{(i_{l}j_{l})}}\right].

Writing
S=\{i_{1},\ldots,i_{2L},j_{1},\ldots,j_{2L}\}
for the set of distinct
indices,
q_{l}\in\mathcal{M}_{i_{l}j_{l},S}
for the monomials in the expansion
of
\mathcal{B}_{i_{l}j_{l}}^{(i_{l}j_{l})}\mathcal{Q}_{i_{l}}^{(i_{l})}\mathcal{Q}_{j_{l}}^{(i_{l}j_{l})}
in
Lemma
3.12
, and
q=\prod_{l=1}^{L}q_{l}\prod_{L+1}^{2L}\bar{q}_{l}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left|\sum_{i\neq j}\bar{\mathbf{v}}(i)\mathbf{v}(j)\mathcal{B}_{ij}^{(ij)}\mathcal{Q}_{i}^{(i)}\mathcal{Q}_{j}^{(ij)}\right|^{2L}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i_{1}\neq j_{1}}\cdots\sum_{i_{2L}\neq j_{2L}}\prod_{l=1}^{2L}|\mathbf{v}(i_{l})\mathbf{v}(j_{l})|

\displaystyle\hskip 14.45377pt\left(\sum_{q_{1}\in\mathcal{M}_{i_{1}j_{1},S},\ldots,q_{2L}\in\mathcal{M}_{i_{2L}j_{2L},S}}\mathbb{E}|\mathbb{E}_{S}q|+O_{\prec}(N^{-\tau^{\prime}(D+1)})\right).

Let
K\subseteq S
denote the subset of indices that appear exactly once in the
list
(i_{1},\ldots,i_{2L},j_{1},\ldots,j_{2L})
.
Applying
\sum_{i}|\mathbf{v}(i)|\leq\sqrt{N}
and
\sum_{i}|\mathbf{v}(i)|^{p}\leq 1
for each

p\geq 2
, for any fixed
k\in\{1,\ldots,4L\}
and a constant
C\equiv C(L)>0
we then have

\mathop{\sum_{i_{1}\neq j_{1},\ldots,i_{2L}\neq j_{2L}}}_{|K|=k}\prod_{l=1}^{2L}|\mathbf{v}(i_{l})\mathbf{v}(j_{l})|\leq CN^{k/2}\leq CN^{2L}.

Thus

(3.20)

\mathbb{E}\left|\sum_{i\neq j}\bar{\mathbf{v}}(i)\mathbf{v}(j)\mathcal{B}_{ij}^{(ij)}\mathcal{Q}_{i}^{(i)}\mathcal{Q}_{j}^{(ij)}\right|^{2L}\prec\max_{\begin{subarray}{c}i_{1},\ldots,i_{2L},j_{1},\ldots,j_{2L}\in[N]\\
q_{1}\in\mathcal{M}_{i_{1}j_{1},S},\ldots,q_{2L}\in\mathcal{M}_{i_{2L}j_{2L},S}\end{subarray}}\Big\{N^{|K|/2}\mathbb{E}|\mathbb{E}_{S}q|\Big\}+O_{\prec}(N^{-\tau^{\prime}(D+1)+2L}).

Here
S=\{i_{1},\ldots,i_{2L},j_{1},\ldots,j_{2L}\}
denotes the set of distinct
indices and
K\subseteq S
denotes the subset appearing exactly once, both of
which depend implicitly on
(i_{1},\ldots,i_{2L},j_{1},\ldots,j_{2L})
.

For each
l\in[2L]
, let
m_{B}^{**}(l),m_{B}^{*1}(l),m_{B}^{*2}(l),m_{B}(l)
denote the
counts
m_{B}^{**},m_{B}^{*1},m_{B}^{*2},m_{B}
of Lemma
3.12
for

q_{l}
, and let
m_{P}^{*1}(l),m_{P}^{*2}(l)
denote also the total number of factors

\mathcal{P}_{i_{l}}^{(S)},\mathcal{P}_{j_{l}}^{(S)}
in
q_{l}
respectively. Set

m_{B}^{**}=\sum_{l=1}^{2L}m_{B}^{**}(l),\quad m_{B}^{*}=\sum_{l=1}^{2L}m_{B}^{*1}(l)+m_{B}^{*2}(l),\quad m_{B}=\sum_{l=1}^{2L}m_{B}(l),\quad m_{P}^{*}=\sum_{l=1}^{2L}m_{P}^{*1}(l)+m_{P}^{*2}(l),

and denote also

E(l)=m_{B}^{**}(l)+\frac{1}{2}(m_{B}^{*1}(l)+m_{B}^{*2}(l))+m_{P}^{*1}(l)+m_{P}^{*2}(l)-1.

Note that Lemma
3.12
(a) implies
E(l)\geq 0
for each

l=1,\ldots,2L
. Consider three cases for an index
i_{l}\in K
(or
j_{l}\in K
),
analogous to the proof of Lemma
3.4
:

•

i_{l}
appears as a lower index on exactly one factor of

q_{l}
(which must be
\mathcal{B}_{i_{l}k}^{(S)}
for some
k\neq i_{l}
),
and it does not appear as a lower index on any factor of

\{q_{l^{\prime}}:l^{\prime}\neq l\}
. Then
\mathbb{E}_{S}q=0
.

•

i_{l}
appears as a lower index in some
\{q_{l^{\prime}}:l^{\prime}\neq l\}
.
Since
i_{l}
is distinct from
\{i_{l^{\prime}},j_{l^{\prime}}\}
for each
l^{\prime}\neq l
,
Lemma
3.12
(c) ensures that the total number of such
indices
i_{l},j_{l}\in K
is at most
m_{B}+\frac{1}{2}m_{B}^{*}
.

•

i_{l}
does not appear as a lower index on any
\{q_{l^{\prime}}:l^{\prime}\neq l\}
,
but appears as a lower index on at least two factors of
q_{l}
. Then Lemma

3.12
(a–b) ensures that either
m_{P}^{*1}(l)\geq 1

and
m_{B}^{**}(l)+\frac{1}{2}(m_{B}^{*1}(l)+m_{B}^{*2}(l))\geq 1
, or

m_{P}^{*1}(l)=0
and
m_{B}^{**}(l)+\frac{1}{2}(m_{B}^{*1}(l)+m_{B}^{*2}(l))\geq 2

(since
m_{B}^{**}(l)+m_{B}^{*1}(l)\geq 2
is odd, and also

m_{B}^{*2}(l)\geq 1
if
m_{B}^{**}(l)=0
). This implies
E(l)\geq 1
.

Furthermore, if also
j_{l}\in K
and
j_{l}
appears
as a lower index on at least two factors of
q_{l}
, then similarly, either

m_{P}^{*1}(l)+m_{P}^{*2}(l)\geq 2
and

m_{B}^{**}(l)+\frac{1}{2}(m_{B}^{*1}(l)+m_{B}^{*2}(l))\geq 1
, or

m_{P}^{*1}(l)+m_{P}^{*2}(l)=1
and

m_{B}^{**}(l)+\frac{1}{2}(m_{B}^{*1}(l)+m_{B}^{*2}(l))\geq 2
, or

m_{P}^{*1}(l)+m_{P}^{*2}(l)=0
and

m_{B}^{**}(l)+\frac{1}{2}(m_{B}^{*1}(l)+m_{B}^{*2}(l))\geq 3
. This
implies
E(l)\geq 2
.

Thus, the total number of such indices
i_{l},j_{l}\in K

is at most
\sum_{l=1}^{2L}E(l)
.

Combining these cases, we see that either
\mathbb{E}_{S}q=0
, or

m_{B}+\frac{1}{2}m_{B}^{*}+\sum_{l=1}^{2L}E(l)=m_{B}^{**}+m_{B}^{*}+m_{B}+m_{P}^{*}-2L\geq|K|.

In the latter case, using
Lemma
3.9
to bound
|\mathcal{B}_{ij}^{(S)}|,|\mathcal{P}_{i}^{(S)}|\prec\Phi
and each other factor of
q
by
O_{\prec}(1)
, we get
|\mathbb{E}_{S}q|\prec\Phi^{2L+|K|}
, and hence

\mathbb{E}\left|\sum_{i\neq j}\bar{\mathbf{v}}(i)\mathbf{v}(j)\mathcal{B}_{ij}^{(ij)}\mathcal{Q}_{i}^{(i)}\mathcal{Q}_{j}^{(ij)}\right|^{2L}\prec N^{|K|/2}\Phi^{2L+|K|}+N^{-\tau^{\prime}(D+1)+2L}\leq(\sqrt{N}\Phi)^{4L}\Phi^{2L}+N^{-\tau^{\prime}(D+1)+2L}

where the last inequality applies
\sqrt{N}\Phi\geq 1
and
|K|\leq 4L
.
For sufficiently large
D
, the second term is at most the first, so
Lemma
3.5
(b) again follows from Markov’s
inequality.
∎

Finally, we show Lemma
3.5
(c).
The following lemma is similar to Lemmas
3.11

and
3.12
.

Lemma 3.13
.

Suppose the conditions of Lemma
3.4
hold.
Fix any
L\geq 1
and
D>0
. Then uniformly over
z\in\mathbf{D}
,
S\subset[N]
with
|S|\leq L
,
and
i\in S
, the following holds:

Denote
S^{(i)}=S\setminus{i}
. There exists a collection of monomials

\mathcal{M}_{i,S}
such that the quantity
\mathcal{X}_{i}^{(i)}\mathcal{Q}_{i}^{(i)}
can be expanded as

\displaystyle\mathcal{X}_{i}^{(i)}\mathcal{Q}_{i}^{(i)}

\displaystyle=\sum_{q\in\mathcal{M}_{i,S}}q\left(\mathcal{X}_{i}^{(S)},\{\mathcal{X}_{j}^{(S)}\}_{j\in S^{(i)}},\{\mathcal{B}_{ij}^{(S)}\}_{j\in S^{(i)}},\{\mathcal{B}_{jk}^{(S)}\}_{j\neq k\in S^{(i)}},\mathcal{P}_{i}^{(S)},\{\mathcal{P}_{j}^{(S)}\}_{j\in S^{(i)}},\mathcal{C}^{(S)}\right)

\displaystyle+O_{\prec}(N^{-\tau^{\prime}(D+1)})

Each monomial
q\in\mathcal{M}_{i,S}
is a product of
\pm 1
and one or more of its
inputs, allowing repetition. We
have
q=O_{\prec}(\Phi)
uniformly over
q\in\mathcal{M}_{i,S}
, and the
number of monomials
|\mathcal{M}_{i,S}|
is at most a constant depending only on
L,D
.

Furthermore, for each
q\in\mathcal{M}_{i,S}
, letting
m_{X}^{*},m_{X},m_{B}^{*},m_{B}
denote
the numbers of factors of the forms
\mathcal{X}_{i}^{(S)}
,

\{\mathcal{X}_{j}^{(S)}\}_{j\in S^{(i)}}
,

\{\mathcal{B}_{ij}^{(S)}\}_{j\in S^{(i)}}
, and

\{\mathcal{B}_{jk}^{(S)}\}_{j\neq k\in S^{(i)}}
in
q
, we have:

(a)

m_{X}^{*}+m_{X}=1
.

(b)

m_{X}^{*}+m_{B}^{*}
is odd, and for each
j\in S^{(i)}
, the number of
occurrences of
j
as a lower index of the factors

\{\mathcal{X}_{j}^{(S)}\}_{j\in S^{(i)}}
and

\{\mathcal{B}_{jk}^{(S)}\}_{j\neq k\in S^{(i)}}
is even.

(c)

The number of distinct indices of
S^{(i)}
that appear as a lower
index across all factors of
q
is at most
\frac{1}{2}m_{X}+m_{B}+\frac{1}{2}m_{B}^{*}
.

Proof.

The proof is similar to the proofs of Lemmas
3.11

and
3.12
, where we instead apply the recursion (
3.9
) and the bound
|\mathcal{X}_{j}^{(S)}|\prec\Phi
.
We omit the details for brevity.
∎

Proof of Lemma
3.5
(c).

Fixing any
L\geq 1
and
D>0
, a high moment expansion gives

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left|\sum_{i=1}^{N}\bar{\mathbf{v}}(i)\mathcal{X}_{i}^{(i)}\mathcal{Q}_{i}^{(i)}\right|^{2L}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i_{1},\ldots,i_{2L}=1}^{N}\prod_{l=1}^{L}\bar{\mathbf{v}}(i_{l})\prod_{l=L+1}^{2L}\mathbf{v}(i_{l})\mathbb{E}\prod_{l=1}^{L}\mathcal{X}_{i_{l}}^{(i_{l})}\mathcal{Q}_{i_{l}}^{(i_{l})}\prod_{l=L+1}^{2L}\overline{\mathcal{X}_{i_{l}}^{(i_{l})}\mathcal{Q}_{i_{l}}^{(i_{l})}}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i_{1},\ldots,i_{2L}=1}^{N}\prod_{l=1}^{2L}|\mathbf{v}(i_{l})|\left(\sum_{q_{1}\in\mathcal{M}_{i_{1},S},\ldots,q_{2L}\in\mathcal{M}_{i_{2L},S}}\mathbb{E}|\mathbb{E}_{S}q|+O_{\prec}(N^{-\tau^{\prime}(D+1)})\right),

where
S=\{i_{1},\ldots,i_{2L}\}
is the set of distinct indices,

q_{l}\in\mathcal{M}_{i_{l},S}
are the monomials in the expansion of

\mathcal{X}_{i_{l}}^{(i_{l})}\mathcal{Q}_{i_{l}}^{(i_{l})}
in Lemma
3.13
,
and
q=\prod_{l=1}^{L}q_{l}\prod_{l=L+1}^{2L}\bar{q}_{l}
.
Let
K\subseteq S
be those indices appearing exactly once in

(i_{1},\ldots,i_{2L})
. Then, applying
\sum_{i}|\mathbf{v}(i)|\leq\sqrt{N}

and
\sum_{i}|\mathbf{v}(i)|^{p}\leq 1
for each
p\geq 2
, this implies as in
Lemma
3.4
(b) that

(3.21)

\mathbb{E}\left|\sum_{i=1}^{N}\bar{\mathbf{v}}(i)\mathcal{X}_{i}^{(i)}\mathcal{Q}_{i}^{(i)}\right|^{2L}\prec\mathop{\max_{i_{1},\ldots,i_{2L}\in[N]}}_{q_{1}\in\mathcal{M}_{i_{1},S},\ldots,q_{2L}\in\mathcal{M}_{i_{2L},S}}\Big\{N^{|K|/2}\mathbb{E}|\mathbb{E}_{S}q|\Big\}+O_{\prec}(N^{-\tau^{\prime}(D+1)+L}).

For each
l\in[2L]
, let
m_{X}^{*}(l),m_{X}(l),m_{B}^{*}(l),m_{B}(l)
denote the counts of
Lemma
3.13
for
q_{l}
, and let
m_{P}^{*}(l)
denote the total
number of factors
\mathcal{P}_{i_{l}}^{(S)}
in
q_{l}
. Set

m_{X}^{*}=\sum_{l=1}^{2L}m_{X}(l),\quad m_{X}=\sum_{l=1}^{2L}m_{X}(l),\quad m_{B}^{*}=\sum_{l=1}^{2L}m_{B}^{*}(l),\quad m_{B}=\sum_{l=1}^{2L}m_{B}(l),\quad m_{P}^{*}=\sum_{l=1}^{2L}m_{P}^{*}(l),

and set also

E(l)=m_{X}^{*}(l)+\frac{1}{2}(m_{X}(l)+m_{B}^{*}(l))+m_{P}^{*}(l)-1.

Note that Lemma
3.13
(a) implies
E(l)\geq 0
for each

l=1,\ldots,2L
. Consider three cases for an index
i_{l}\in K
:

•

i_{l}
appears as a lower index on exactly one factor of

q_{l}
(which must be
\mathcal{X}_{i_{l}}^{(S)}
or
\mathcal{B}_{i_{l}k}^{(S)}
for some

k\neq i_{l}
), and it does not appear as a lower index on any factor of

\{q_{l^{\prime}}:l^{\prime}\neq l\}
. Then
\mathbb{E}_{S}q=0
.

•

i_{l}
appears as a lower index in some
\{q_{l^{\prime}}:l^{\prime}\neq l\}
.
Lemma
3.13
(c) ensures that the total number of such
indices
i_{l}\in K
is at most
\frac{1}{2}m_{X}+m_{B}+\frac{1}{2}m_{B}^{*}
.

•

i_{l}
does not appear as a lower index on any
\{q_{l^{\prime}}:l^{\prime}\neq l\}
,
but appears as a lower index on at least two factors of
q_{l}
. Then Lemma

3.13
(a–b) ensures that either
m_{P}^{*}(l)\geq 1

and
m_{X}^{*}(l)+\frac{1}{2}(m_{X}(l)+m_{B}^{*}(l))\geq 1
, or
m_{P}^{*}(l)=0
and

m_{X}^{*}(l)+\frac{1}{2}(m_{X}(l)+m_{B}^{*}(l))\geq 2
, so
E(l)\geq 1
.
Thus the total number of such indices
i_{l}\in K
is at most
\sum_{l=1}^{2L}E(l)
.

Combining these cases, either
\mathbb{E}_{S}q=0
, or

\frac{1}{2}m_{X}+m_{B}+\frac{1}{2}m_{B}^{*}+\sum_{l=1}^{2L}E(l)=m_{X}^{*}+m_{X}+m_{B}^{*}+m_{B}+m_{P}^{*}-2L\geq|K|.

Bounding
|\mathcal{X}_{i}^{(S)}|,|\mathcal{B}_{ij}^{(S)}|,|\mathcal{P}_{i}^{(S)}|\prec\Phi

gives
|\mathbb{E}_{S}q|\prec\Phi^{2L+|K|}
. Then

\mathbb{E}\left|\sum_{i=1}^{N}\bar{\mathbf{v}}(i)\mathcal{X}_{i}^{(i)}\mathcal{Q}_{i}^{(i)}\right|^{2L}\prec N^{|K|/2}\Phi^{2L+|K|}+N^{-\tau^{\prime}(D+1)+L}\leq(\sqrt{N}\Phi)^{2L}\Phi^{2L}+N^{-\tau^{\prime}(D+1)+L}

where the last inequality uses
\sqrt{N}\Phi\geq 1
and
|K|\leq 2L
.
For large enough
D>0
, the second term is at most the first, so
Lemma
3.5
(c)
follows again by Markov’s inequality.
∎

3.5.
Proof of Lemma
3.6

3.5.1.
Weak cumulant tensor bound

We will use implicitly the observation that if Assumption

3
holds, then the condition (
2.1
) is
valid also for complex inputs
\mathbf{s}_{1},\ldots,\mathbf{s}_{m}\in\mathbb{C}^{n}
and
T\in(\mathbb{C}^{n})^{\otimes k-m}
, as it may be applied separately to the real and complex
parts. In what follows,
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle
represents the non-conjugate, scalar product on
(\mathbb{C}^{n})^{\otimes k}
.

The condition in Assumption
3
pertains to settings where

m,k-m\in\{1,\ldots,k-1\}
. The following lemma clarifies that for
all values of
m
including
\{0,k\}
, Assumption
3

has the following weaker implication.

Lemma 3.14

(Weak cumulant tensor bound)
.

Suppose Assumption
3
holds. Fix any
k\geq 3
,
let
\|\cdot\|_{\mathcal{U}_{k}}
be as in Assumption

3
, and for a scalar quantity
T\in(\mathbb{C}^{n})^{0}
define

\|T\|_{\mathcal{U}_{k}}=|T|
. Then for any
\varepsilon>0
, there exists a constant
C\equiv C(\varepsilon,k)>0
such that for all
i\in[N]
,

0\leq m\leq k
,
\mathbf{s}_{1},\dots,\mathbf{s}_{m}\in\mathbb{C}^{n}
, and
T\in(\mathbb{C}^{n})^{\otimes k-m}
,

|\langle\kappa_{k}(\mathbf{g}_{i}),\mathbf{s}_{1}\otimes\cdots\mathbf{s}_{m}\otimes T\rangle|\leq Cn^{\varepsilon}(\sqrt{n})^{k-m}\|T\|_{\mathcal{U}_{k}}\prod_{t=1}^{m}\|\mathbf{s}_{t}\|_{2}.

Proof.

For
1\leq m\leq k-1
, the result is implied by Assumption

3
. For
m=k
, the result also follows by identifying a single
vector
\mathbf{s}_{t}
as
T
in Assumption
3
and applying

\|\mathbf{s}_{t}\|_{\mathcal{U}_{k}}\leq C_{k}\|\mathbf{s}_{t}\|_{2}
.

For
m=0
, let
T\in(\mathbb{C}^{n})^{\otimes k}
be any
k
-th order tensor. We can write

T=\sum_{\alpha_{1},\dots,\alpha_{k}}T[\alpha_{1},\dots,\alpha_{k}]\mathbf{e}_{\alpha_{1}}\otimes\cdots\otimes\mathbf{e}_{\alpha_{k}}=\sum_{\alpha_{1}}\mathbf{e}_{\alpha_{1}}\otimes\underbrace{\sum_{\alpha_{2},\dots,\alpha_{k}}T[\alpha_{1},\dots,\alpha_{k}]\mathbf{e}_{\alpha_{2}}\otimes\cdots\otimes\mathbf{e}_{\alpha_{k}}}_{\widetilde{T}(\alpha_{1})\in(\mathbb{C}^{n})^{\otimes k-1}}.

Fix
\varepsilon>0
. By Assumption
3
, for a constant
C\equiv C(\varepsilon,k)>0
, we have

\displaystyle|\langle\kappa_{k}(\mathbf{g}_{i}),T\rangle|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\alpha_{1}}\left|\langle\kappa_{k}(\mathbf{g}_{i}),\mathbf{e}_{\alpha_{1}}\otimes\widetilde{T}(\alpha_{1})\rangle\right|

\displaystyle\leq n\cdot Cn^{\varepsilon}(\sqrt{n})^{k-1-1}\max_{\alpha_{1}}\left(\|\mathbf{e}_{\alpha_{1}}\|_{2}\cdot\|\widetilde{T}(\alpha_{1})\|_{\mathcal{U}_{k}}\right)=Cn^{\varepsilon}(\sqrt{n})^{k}\max_{\alpha_{1}}\|\widetilde{T}(\alpha_{1})\|_{\mathcal{U}_{k}}.

The proof is completed by the definition of the
\mathcal{U}_{k}
-norm,

\displaystyle\max_{\alpha_{1}}\|\widetilde{T}(\alpha_{1})\|_{\mathcal{U}_{k}}

\displaystyle=\max_{\alpha_{1}}\max_{\mathbf{u}_{1},\dots,\mathbf{u}_{k-1}\in\mathcal{U}_{k}}\left|\langle\widetilde{T}(\alpha_{1}),\mathbf{u}_{1}\otimes\cdots\mathbf{u}_{k-1}\rangle\right|

\displaystyle=\max_{\alpha_{1}}\max_{\mathbf{u}_{1},\dots,\mathbf{u}_{k-1}\in\mathcal{U}_{k}}\left|\langle T,\mathbf{e}_{\alpha_{1}}\otimes\mathbf{u}_{1}\otimes\cdots\mathbf{u}_{k-1}\rangle\right|\leq\|T\|_{\mathcal{U}_{k}}.

∎

3.5.2.
Moment-cumulant expansion

For any random vector
\mathbf{g}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
with finite moments of all orders, any integer
k\in\mathbb{N}
, and any partition
\pi
of
[k]
, we define the tensor
\kappa_{\pi}(\mathbf{g})\in(\mathbb{R}^{n})^{\otimes k}
as

\kappa_{\pi}(\mathbf{g})=\bigotimes_{B\in\pi}\kappa_{|B|}(\mathbf{g}),

where
\kappa_{|B|}(\mathbf{g})
is the
|B|
-th order cumulant tensor of
\mathbf{g}
, and
each factor
\kappa_{|B|}(\mathbf{g})
of the tensor product corresponds to the indices
of
B\in\pi
. We have the following moment-cumulant decomposition:

Lemma 3.15
.

Suppose
\mathbf{g}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
is a random vector with
\mathbb{E}\mathbf{g}=0
,
\mathbb{E}\mathbf{g}\mathbf{g}^{*}=\Sigma
,
and finite moments of all orders. Then for any integers
k,m\geq 0
, we have

\mathbb{E}[(\mathbf{g}\mathbf{g}^{*}-\Sigma)^{\otimes k}\otimes\mathbf{g}^{\otimes m}]=\sum_{\pi\in\dot{P}_{k,m}}\kappa_{\pi}(\mathbf{g}),

where
\dot{P}_{k,m}
is the set of all partitions of
[2k+m]
that does not
have any singleton block or any of the cardinality-2 blocks

\{1,2\},\{3,4\},\ldots,\{2k-1,2k\}
.

Proof.

We prove by induction on
k
: For
k=0
, let
P_{m}
denote the set of all
partitions of
[m]
. By the moment-cumulant relations, for any vectors

\mathbf{v}_{1},\ldots,\mathbf{v}_{m}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
,

\mathbb{E}\langle\mathbf{g}^{\otimes m},\mathbf{v}_{1}\otimes\ldots\otimes\mathbf{v}_{m}\rangle=\sum_{\pi\in P_{m}}\prod_{B\in\pi}\kappa_{|B|}(\mathbf{g}^{*}\mathbf{v}_{i}:i\in B),

hence
\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{g}^{\otimes m}]=\sum_{\pi\in P_{m}}\kappa_{\pi}(\mathbf{g})
. If
\pi
has a
singleton block, then
\kappa_{\pi}(\mathbf{g})=0
since
\kappa_{1}(\mathbf{g})=\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{g}]=0
, and the
result follows for
m=0
.

Now suppose the claim holds for
k-1
for all
m\geq 0
. Let
Q:(\mathbb{R}^{n})^{2k+m}\to(\mathbb{R}^{n})^{2k+m}
be the permutation for which

Q(T)(i_{1},\ldots,i_{2k+m})=T(i_{1},\ldots,i_{2k-2},i_{2k+m-1},i_{2k+m},i_{2k-1},\ldots,i_{2k+m-2})
,
i.e. rotating the last two coordinates to positions
2k-1
and
2k
.
Then

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[(\mathbf{g}\mathbf{g}^{*}-\Sigma)^{\otimes k}\otimes\mathbf{g}^{\otimes m}]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[(\mathbf{g}\mathbf{g}^{*}-\Sigma)^{\otimes k-1}\otimes\mathbf{g}^{\otimes m+2}]-Q\Big(\mathbb{E}[(\mathbf{g}\mathbf{g}^{*}-\Sigma)^{\otimes k-1}\otimes\mathbf{g}^{\otimes m}]\otimes\Sigma\Big)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\pi\in\dot{P}_{k-1,m+2}}\kappa_{\pi}(\mathbf{g})-\sum_{\pi\in\dot{P}_{k-1,m}}Q\Big(\kappa_{\pi}(\mathbf{g})\otimes\Sigma\Big).

where the second equality holds by the induction hypothesis.
The first sum is over partitions
\pi
of
[2k+m]
that do not contain
singletons or the blocks
\{1,2\},\ldots,\{2k-3,2k-2\}
. The second sum may be
understood as the sum of
\kappa_{\pi^{\prime}}(\mathbf{g})
for partitions
\pi^{\prime}
of
[2k+m]

that do not contain singletons or the blocks

\{1,2\},\ldots,\{2k-3,2k-2\}
, but contains the block
\{2k-1,2k-2\}
. Hence
their difference is exactly
\sum_{\pi\in\dot{P}_{k,m}}\kappa_{\pi}(\mathbf{g})
,
completing the induction.
∎

3.5.3.
Tensor network representation

We again fix a deterministic unit vector
\mathbf{u}\in\mathbb{C}^{n}
and abbreviate

\mathcal{Y}_{i}^{(S)}=\mathcal{Y}_{i}^{(S)}[\mathbf{u}\mathbf{u}^{*}],\qquad\mathcal{Z}_{ijk}^{(S)}=\mathcal{Z}_{ijk}^{(S)}[\mathbf{u}\mathbf{u}^{*}],\qquad\mathcal{X}_{i}^{(S)}=\mathcal{X}_{i}^{(S)}[\mathbf{u}].

All subsequent
O_{\prec}(\cdot)
bounds are implicitly uniform over all such
vectors
\mathbf{u}
.

To prove Lemma
3.6
,
we shall now develop a tensor network language to express the high moment
expansions of the quantities to be bounded.

For any tensor
T\in(\mathbb{C}^{n})^{\otimes k}
, we write
\text{deg}(T)=k
to mean the degree
or order of the tensor. In particular, this means for vectors
\mathbf{x}\in\mathbb{C}^{n}

that
\text{deg}(\mathbf{x})=1
and for matrices
A\in\mathbb{C}^{n\times n}
that
\text{deg}(A)=2
.

Definition 3.16
.

For any integer
L\geq 1
and
S\subset[N]
with
|S|\leq L
,
we say that
(\mathcal{G},f_{\mathcal{G}})
is a

(S,L)
-valid tensor network
if there exist constants
M,C\geq 1

depending only on
L
such that the following holds:

Let
\{\mathbf{v}_{\alpha}\}_{\alpha=1}^{n}

be the (random, real) eigenvectors of
R^{(S)}
, let
\mathbf{u}\in\mathbb{C}^{n}
be a
fixed deterministic unit vector, and let
\mathcal{U}=\bigcup_{k=1}^{M}\mathcal{U}_{k}

where
\{\mathcal{U}_{k}\}_{k\geq 1}
are the sets of Assumption
3
.
Then
\mathcal{G}=(V_{\mathcal{G}},E_{\mathcal{G}})
is an undirected multi-graph with no self loops
and
|V_{\mathcal{G}}|<C
, and

f_{\mathcal{G}}:V_{\mathcal{G}}\rightarrow\mathcal{T}
is a labeling function on the vertices
V_{\mathcal{G}}

taking values in
a set of tensors
\mathcal{T}=\mathcal{L}\cup\mathcal{R}\cup\mathcal{E}\cup\mathcal{I}_{m}\cup\mathcal{I}_{t}
, where

\mathcal{L}:=\{\mathbf{u},\bar{\mathbf{u}}\},\quad\mathcal{R}:=\mathcal{R}_{o}\cup\mathcal{R}_{n},\quad\mathcal{R}_{o}:=\left\{\frac{R^{(S)}}{\sqrt{N}}\mathbf{u},\frac{\bar{R}^{(S)}}{\sqrt{N}}\bar{\mathbf{u}}\right\},\quad\mathcal{R}_{n}:=\bigcup_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathcal{U}}\left\{\frac{R^{(S)}}{\sqrt{N}}\mathbf{x},\frac{\bar{R}^{(S)}}{\sqrt{N}}\mathbf{x}\right\},

\mathcal{E}:=\{\mathbf{v}_{1},\ldots,\mathbf{v}_{n}\},\quad\mathcal{I}_{m}:=\left\{\frac{R^{(S)}}{N},\frac{\bar{R}^{(S)}}{N}\right\}\quad\mathcal{I}_{t}:=\bigcup_{k=2}^{M}\{\kappa_{k}(\mathbf{g}_{1}),\ldots,\kappa_{k}(\mathbf{g}_{N})\}\text{
(where $\kappa_{2}(\mathbf{g}_{i})=\Sigma$)}.

We say that
v\in V_{\mathcal{G}}
is a
left leaf
, a
right leaf
, a
right original leaf
, a
right new leaf
, an
eigen-leaf
, a
matrix vertex
, or a
tensor vertex

if
f_{\mathcal{G}}(v)
belongs to
\mathcal{L},\mathcal{R},\mathcal{R}_{o},\mathcal{R}_{n},\mathcal{E},\mathcal{I}_{m}
, or
\mathcal{I}_{t}

respectively. We denote the corresponding sets of vertices

V_{\mathcal{G}}^{l},V_{\mathcal{G}}^{r},V_{\mathcal{G}}^{ro},V_{\mathcal{G}}^{rn},V_{\mathcal{G}}^{e},V_{\mathcal{G}}^{m},V_{\mathcal{G}}^{t}\subseteq V_{\mathcal{G}}.

Moreover, we require that the following hold for
(\mathcal{G},f_{\mathcal{G}})
:

(a)

For all
v\in V_{\mathcal{G}}
, we have
\text{deg}(v)=\text{deg}(f_{\mathcal{G}}(v))
, where

\text{deg}(v)
is the vertex degree of
v\in V_{\mathcal{G}}
and
\text{deg}(f_{\mathcal{G}}(v))
is the
degree/order of its label
f_{\mathcal{G}}(v)
. In particular,
\mathcal{G}
has no vertices of
degree 0, each vertex of
V_{\mathcal{G}}^{l}\cup V_{\mathcal{G}}^{r}\cup V_{\mathcal{G}}^{e}
has degree 1,
and each vertex of
V_{\mathcal{G}}^{m}
has degree 2.

(b)

\mathcal{G}
is a bipartite multi-graph between

V_{\mathcal{G}}^{l}\cup V_{\mathcal{G}}^{r}\cup V_{\mathcal{G}}^{e}\cup V_{\mathcal{G}}^{m}
and
V_{\mathcal{G}}^{t}
, i.e., each
edge of
E_{\mathcal{G}}
connects some
u\in V_{\mathcal{G}}^{l}\cup V_{\mathcal{G}}^{r}\cup V_{\mathcal{G}}^{e}\cup V_{\mathcal{G}}^{m}
with some
v\in V_{\mathcal{G}}^{t}
.

(c)

If
u\in V_{\mathcal{G}}^{m}
and
v\in V_{\mathcal{G}}^{t}
have 2 edges between them,
then
\text{deg}(v)\geq 3
. We refer to such a vertex
u\in V_{\mathcal{G}}^{m}
as a

type 1 matrix vertex
, and we refer to each other matrix vertex

u\in V_{\mathcal{G}}^{m}
(connecting to two distinct vertices
v,v^{\prime}\in V_{\mathcal{G}}^{t}
)
as a
type 2 matrix vertex
. We denote
the sets of such vertices by
V_{\mathcal{G}}^{m1},V_{\mathcal{G}}^{m2}\subseteq V_{\mathcal{G}}^{m}
.

For each tensor vertex
v\in V_{\mathcal{G}}^{t}
, we will write

l(v),r(v),ro(v),rn(v),e(v),m(v),m1(v),m2(v)
for the numbers of left leaves,
right leaves, right original leaves, right new leaves, eigen-leaves, matrix
vertices, type 1 matrix vertices, and type 2 matrix vertices adjacent to
v
.
(Each adjacent type 1 matrix neighbor contributes a count of 1 to
m1(v)
,
even though it has 2 edges connecting to
v
.)

The proof of Lemma
3.6
(a)
will consider networks having only labels

\mathcal{L},\mathcal{R}_{o},\mathcal{R}_{n},\mathcal{I}_{m},\mathcal{I}_{t}
(thus no eigen-leaves), while the proof
of Lemma
3.6
(b–c) will pertain to
networks having only labels
\mathcal{E},\mathcal{R}_{o},\mathcal{R}_{n},\mathcal{I}_{m},\mathcal{I}_{t}
(thus no left leaves).
For convenience, we state here several general results that pertain to networks
which may have vertices of all types.

Definition 3.17
.

For a
(S,L)
-valid tensor network
(\mathcal{G},f_{\mathcal{G}})
, we define its contracted value to be

\mathrm{val}(\mathcal{G},f_{\mathcal{G}}):=\sum_{\alpha\in[n]^{E_{\mathcal{G}}}}\prod_{v\in V_{\mathcal{G}}}[f_{\mathcal{G}}(v)]_{\alpha(\partial v)}

where
\alpha(\partial v)
denotes the multi-set of indices
\alpha_{e}

associated with the edges
e
incident to
v
. Note that this is well-defined
since all tensors
f_{\mathcal{G}}(v)\in\mathcal{T}
are
symmetric (under permutations of coordinates, without complex conjugation).

Remark 3.18
.

\mathrm{val}(\mathcal{G},f_{\mathcal{G}})
is multiplicative across disjoint connected components
of the graph. I.e., if
(\mathcal{G},f_{\mathcal{G}})
is a
(S,L)
-valid tensor network such
that
\mathcal{G}
splits into two connected components
\mathcal{G}_{1},\mathcal{G}_{2}
, then both

(\mathcal{G}_{1},f_{\mathcal{G}_{1}}),(\mathcal{G}_{2},f_{\mathcal{G}_{2}})
must be
(S,L)
-valid tensor network,
with
f_{\mathcal{G}_{i}}
defined as the restriction of
f_{\mathcal{G}}
to
\mathcal{G}_{i}
, and

\mathrm{val}(\mathcal{G},f_{\mathcal{G}})=\mathrm{val}(\mathcal{G}_{1},f_{\mathcal{G}_{1}})\times\mathrm{val}(\mathcal{G}_{2},f_{\mathcal{G}_{2}})
.

We will proceed to bound the value of a
(S,L)
-valid tensor network via
operations that successively remove tensor vertices
v\in V_{\mathcal{G}}^{t}
with

\text{deg}(v)\geq 3
from
\mathcal{G}
.
This procedure is formalized by the following definition.

Definition 3.19
.

For a
(S,L)
-valid tensor network
(\mathcal{G},f_{\mathcal{G}})
and
v\in V_{\mathcal{G}}^{t}
with

\text{deg}(v)\geq 3
, we say that
(\mathcal{G}^{\prime},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{G}^{\prime}})
is
the family of
networks generated by removing
v
if
G^{\prime}
is constructed by removing
v
, its
incident edges, and all of its adjacent leaves and adjacent
type 1 matrix vertices, and

\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{G}^{\prime}}
is the set of all labelings
f_{\mathcal{G}^{\prime}}:V_{\mathcal{G}^{\prime}}\rightarrow\mathcal{T}

satisfying the following:

•

If
u\in V_{\mathcal{G}}
is not adjacent to
v
, then
f_{\mathcal{G}}(u)=f_{\mathcal{G}^{\prime}}(u).

•

If
u\in V_{\mathcal{G}}^{m2}
is adjacent to
v
, i.e.
u
is a type 2 matrix
neighbor of
v
(and hence remains in
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
), then
f_{\mathcal{G}^{\prime}}(u)\in\mathcal{R}_{n}
.

In words, each
(\mathcal{G}^{\prime},f_{\mathcal{G}^{\prime}})
is constructed by
removing
v
and replacing each type 2 matrix neighbor of
v
by a
right new leaf, and
\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{G}^{\prime}}
comprises all possible labelings of these right
new leaves.

We note that this procedure of removing
v
from
G
does not change the degree
of any vertices which remain in
G
.
The following lemma gives a basic bound for
\mathrm{val}(\mathcal{G},f_{\mathcal{G}})

via such a removal.

Lemma 3.20
.

Suppose the conditions of Lemma
3.6
hold.
Let
(\mathcal{G},f_{\mathcal{G}})
be a
(S,L)
-valid tensor network, let

v\in V_{\mathcal{G}}^{t}
be a tensor vertex satisfying
\text{deg}(v)\geq 3
, and let

(\mathcal{G}^{\prime},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{G}^{\prime}})
be the family of networks generated by removing
v
. Then

|\mathrm{val}(\mathcal{G},f_{\mathcal{G}})|\prec\Phi^{r(v)}\times\max_{f_{\mathcal{G}^{\prime}}\in\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{G}^{\prime}}}|\mathrm{val}(\mathcal{G}^{\prime},f_{\mathcal{G}^{\prime}})|

uniformly over
z\in\mathbf{D}
and all
S\subseteq[N]
with
|S|\leq L
.

Proof.

By Remark
3.18
, we may consider without loss of
generality the case where
\mathcal{G}
is a single connected component. A
representative form of
(\mathcal{G},f_{\mathcal{G}})
is given by the following picture, where

v\in V_{\mathcal{G}}^{t}
has label
\kappa_{k}(\mathbf{g}_{i})
,
\mathbf{x}
denotes a deterministic
vector in
\mathcal{U}
,
\mathbf{v}_{\alpha}
denotes an eigenvector of
R^{(S)}
,
and
T
denotes the contraction of all tensors
connecting to
v
via its neighboring type 2 matrix vertices:

\kappa_{k}(\mathbf{g}_{i})
\mathbf{u}
\mathbf{u}
\dots
l(v)
\mathbf{v}_{\alpha}
\mathbf{v}_{\alpha}
\dots
e(v)
\frac{R^{(S)}}{N}
\frac{R^{(S)}}{N}
\dots
T
\frac{R^{(S)}}{N}
\frac{R^{(S)}}{N}
\dots
m1(v)
\frac{R^{(S)}\mathbf{u}}{\sqrt{N}}
\frac{R^{(S)}\mathbf{u}}{\sqrt{N}}
\dots
ro(v)
\frac{R^{(S)}\mathbf{x}}{\sqrt{N}}
\frac{R^{(S)}\mathbf{x}}{\sqrt{N}}
\dots
rn(v)

(Other forms of
(\mathcal{G},f_{\mathcal{G}})
may replace certain copies of
R^{(S)}
or
\mathbf{u}

by their complex conjugates, have differing vectors
\mathbf{x}\in\mathcal{U}
for the

rn(v)
neighbors that are right new leaves, and/or have differing
eigenvectors
\mathbf{v}_{\alpha}
for the
e(v)
neighbors that are eigen-leaves.
For all such networks,
\mathrm{val}(\mathcal{G},f_{\mathcal{G}})
may be bounded
similarly. Throughout this and the subsequent proofs, we will focus on a single
representative example of
\mathrm{val}(\mathcal{G},f_{\mathcal{G}})
for notational convenience.)

Writing the spectral decomposition

R^{(S)}=\sum_{\alpha}(\lambda_{\alpha}-z)^{-1}\mathbf{v}_{\alpha}^{\otimes 2}
,

|\mathrm{val}(\mathcal{G},f_{\mathcal{G}})|
for the above network may be bounded using
Lemma
3.14
as

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle\kappa_{k}(\mathbf{g}_{i}),(N^{-1}R^{(S)})^{\otimes m1(v)}\otimes\mathbf{v}_{\alpha}^{\otimes e(v)}\otimes\mathbf{u}^{\otimes l(v)}\otimes(N^{-1/2}R^{(S)}\mathbf{u})^{\otimes ro(v)}\otimes(N^{-1/2}R^{(S)}\mathbf{x})^{\otimes rn(v)}\otimes T\right\rangle\right|

\displaystyle\leq N^{-m1(v)}\times\sum_{\alpha_{1},\ldots,\alpha_{m1(v)}}\prod_{l=1}^{m1(v)}\frac{1}{|\lambda_{\alpha_{l}}-z|}\times

\displaystyle\quad\left|\left\langle\kappa_{k}(\mathbf{g}_{i}),\bigotimes_{l=1}^{m1(v)}\mathbf{v}_{\alpha_{l}}^{\otimes 2}\otimes\mathbf{v}_{\alpha}^{\otimes e(v)}\otimes\mathbf{u}^{\otimes l(v)}\otimes(N^{-1/2}R^{(S)}\mathbf{u})^{\otimes ro(v)}\otimes(N^{-1/2}R^{(S)}\mathbf{x})^{\otimes rn(v)}\otimes T\right\rangle\right|

\displaystyle\leq(N^{-1}\|R^{(S)}\|_{1})^{m1(v)}\times

\displaystyle\quad\max_{\alpha_{1},\ldots,\alpha_{m1(v)}}\left|\left\langle\kappa_{k}(\mathbf{g}_{i}),\bigotimes_{l=1}^{m1(v)}\mathbf{v}_{\alpha_{l}}^{\otimes 2}\otimes\mathbf{v}_{\alpha}^{\otimes e(v)}\otimes\mathbf{u}^{\otimes l(v)}\otimes(N^{-1/2}R^{(S)}\mathbf{u})^{\otimes ro(v)}\otimes(N^{-1/2}R^{(S)}\mathbf{x})^{\otimes rn(v)}\otimes T\right\rangle\right|

\displaystyle\overset{(a)}{\prec}(N^{-1}\|R^{(S)}\|_{1})^{m1(v)}\times(\sqrt{N})^{\text{deg}(T)}\times\|N^{-1/2}R^{(S)}\mathbf{u}\|_{2}^{ro(v)}\times\|N^{-1/2}R^{(S)}\mathbf{x}\|_{2}^{rn(v)}\times\|T\|_{\mathcal{U}_{k}}

\displaystyle\overset{(b)}{\prec}\Phi^{r(v)}\times\|(\sqrt{N})^{\text{deg}(T)}T\|_{\mathcal{U}_{k}}\overset{(c)}{\prec}\Phi^{r(v)}\times\max_{f_{\mathcal{G}^{\prime}}\in\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{G}^{\prime}}}|\mathrm{val}(\mathcal{G}^{\prime},f_{\mathcal{G}^{\prime}})|.

Here (a) applies Lemma
3.14
, (b) applies the
bounds
N^{-1}\|R^{(S)}\|_{1}\prec 1
,

N^{-1/2}\|R^{(S)}\mathbf{u}\|_{2}\prec\Phi
, and
N^{-1/2}\|R^{(S)}\mathbf{x}\|_{2}\prec\Phi\|\mathbf{x}\|_{2}\prec\Phi
uniformly over all vectors
\mathbf{x}\in\mathcal{U}
,
and (c) uses that
\|(\sqrt{N})^{\text{deg}(T)}T\|_{\mathcal{U}_{k}}
is the maximum of

|\mathrm{val}(\mathcal{G}^{\prime},f_{\mathcal{G}^{\prime}})|
over a subset of the labelings
\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{G}^{\prime}}
in
the network generated by removing
v
.
∎

For certain types of vertices
v\in V_{\mathcal{G}}^{t}
, we will require a refinement of
the above bound using Assumption
3
directly, instead of its
weaker implication of Lemma
3.14
.

Lemma 3.21
.

Suppose the conditions of Lemma
3.6
hold.
Let
(\mathcal{G},f_{\mathcal{G}})
be a
(S,L)
-valid tensor network, let
v\in V_{\mathcal{G}}^{t}
be
a tensor vertex satisfying
\text{deg}(v)\geq 3
, and let

(\mathcal{G}^{\prime},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{G}^{\prime}})
be the the family of networks generated by removing
v
.
Then:

(a)

If
m1(v)\geq 1
, or if
l(v)+e(v)=1

and
r(v)+m1(v)=0
, then

|\mathrm{val}(\mathcal{G},f_{\mathcal{G}})|\prec\frac{N^{\delta}}{\sqrt{N}}\times\Phi^{r(v)}\times\max_{f_{\mathcal{G}^{\prime}}\in\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{G}^{\prime}}}|\mathrm{val}(\mathcal{G}^{\prime},f_{\mathcal{G}^{\prime}})|

uniformly over all
S\subset[N]
with
|S|\leq L
and over
z\in\mathbf{D}
.

(b)

If
r(v)\geq 1
, then

|\mathrm{val}(\mathcal{G},f_{\mathcal{G}})|\prec\frac{N^{\delta}}{\sqrt{N}}\times\Phi^{r(v)-1}\times\max_{f_{\mathcal{G}^{\prime}}\in\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{G}^{\prime}}}|\mathrm{val}(\mathcal{G}^{\prime},f_{\mathcal{G}^{\prime}})|.

uniformly over all
S\subset[N]
with
|S|\leq L
and over
z\in\mathbf{D}
.

Proof.

We may again consider the case where
\mathcal{G}
is a single connected component.

For (a), suppose first
m1(v)\geq 1
and the only vertices adjacent to
v
are
type 1 matrix vertices. This implies that in fact
m1(v)\geq 2
,
because
\text{deg}(v)\geq 3
. Then
v
and these adjacent vertices form a connected
component, and thus must be all of
\mathcal{G}
since
\mathcal{G}
is connected.
In this case,
\mathrm{val}(\mathcal{G},f_{\mathcal{G}})
takes the representative
form
|\langle\kappa_{k}(\mathbf{g}_{i}),(N^{-1}R^{(S)})^{\otimes m1(v)}\rangle|
, for which we have

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle\kappa_{k}(\mathbf{g}_{i}),(N^{-1}R^{(S)})^{\otimes m1(v)}\right\rangle\right|

\displaystyle\leq N^{-(m1(v)-1)}\sum_{\alpha_{1},\ldots,\alpha_{m1(v)-1}}\left(\prod_{t=1}^{m1(v)-1}\frac{1}{|\lambda_{\alpha_{t}}-z|}\right)\left|\left\langle\kappa_{k}(\mathbf{g}_{i}),\bigotimes_{t=1}^{m1(v)-1}\mathbf{v}_{\alpha_{t}}^{\otimes 2}\otimes N^{-1}R^{(S)}\right\rangle\right|

\displaystyle\leq(N^{-1}\|R^{(S)}\|_{1})^{m1(v)-1}\max_{\alpha_{1},\ldots,\alpha_{m1(v)-1}}\left|\left\langle\kappa_{k}(\mathbf{g}_{i}),\bigotimes_{t=1}^{m1(v)-1}\mathbf{v}_{\alpha_{t}}^{\otimes 2}\otimes N^{-1}R^{(S)}\right\rangle\right|

\displaystyle\prec(N^{-1}\|R^{(S)}\|_{1})^{m1(v)-1}(\sqrt{N})^{2-1}\|N^{-1}R^{(S)}\|_{\mathcal{U}_{k}}\prec N^{-1/2}N^{\delta}.

by Assumption
3
and the bounds

\|N^{-1}R^{(S)}\|_{1}\prec 1
and

\|R^{(S)}\|_{\mathcal{U}_{k}}\prec N^{\delta}
. This
implies the lemma since
r(v)=0
and
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
is empty.

If
m1(v)\geq 1
and the only vertices adjacent to
v
are matrix vertices, of
which at least one is type 2, then a representative form of

\mathrm{val}(\mathcal{G},f_{\mathcal{G}})
is bounded using Assumption
3

similarly as

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle\kappa_{k}(\mathbf{g}_{i}),(N^{-1}R^{(S)})^{\otimes m1(v)}\otimes T\right\rangle\right|

\displaystyle\leq N^{-m1(v)}\sum_{\alpha_{1},\ldots,\alpha_{m1(v)}}\prod_{t=1}^{m1(v)}\frac{1}{|\lambda_{\alpha_{t}}-z|}\left|\left\langle\kappa_{k}(\mathbf{g}_{i}),\bigotimes_{t=1}^{m1(v)}\mathbf{v}_{\alpha_{t}}^{\otimes 2}\otimes T\right\rangle\right|

\displaystyle\prec(N^{-1}\|R^{(S)}\|_{1})^{m1(v)}(\sqrt{N})^{\text{deg}(T)-1}\|T\|_{\mathcal{U}_{k}}\prec N^{-1/2}\times\max_{f_{\mathcal{G}^{\prime}}\in\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{G}^{\prime}}}|\mathrm{val}(\mathcal{G}^{\prime},f_{\mathcal{G}^{\prime}})|.

This implies the lemma since
r(v)=0
and
\delta\geq 0
.

If
m1(v)\geq 1
and
v
is adjacent to at least one leaf vertex,
i.e.
l(v)+e(v)+r(v)\geq 1
, then a representative form of

\mathrm{val}(\mathcal{G},f_{\mathcal{G}})
is bounded similarly using
Assumption
3
as

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle\kappa_{k}(\mathbf{g}_{i}),\mathbf{u}^{\otimes l(v)}\otimes\mathbf{v}_{\beta}^{\otimes e(v)}\otimes(N^{-1/2}R^{(S)}\mathbf{u})^{ro(v)}\otimes(N^{-1/2}R^{(S)}\mathbf{x})^{\otimes rn(v)}\otimes(N^{-1}R^{(S)})^{\otimes m1(v)}\otimes T\right\rangle\right|

\displaystyle\leq N^{-(m1(v)-1)}\sum_{\alpha_{1},\ldots,\alpha_{m1(v)-1}}\left(\prod_{t=1}^{m1(v)-1}\frac{1}{|\lambda_{\alpha_{t}}-z|}\right)

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle\kappa_{k}(\mathbf{g}_{i}),\mathbf{u}^{\otimes l(v)}\otimes\mathbf{v}_{\beta}^{\otimes e(v)}\otimes(N^{-1/2}R^{(S)}\mathbf{u})^{ro(v)}\otimes(N^{-1/2}R^{(S)}\mathbf{x})^{\otimes rn(v)}\otimes\bigotimes_{t=1}^{m1(v)-1}\mathbf{v}_{\alpha_{t}}^{\otimes 2}\otimes(N^{-1}R^{(S)})\otimes T\right\rangle\right|

\displaystyle\prec(N^{-1}\|R^{(S)}\|_{1})^{m1(v)-1}(\sqrt{N})^{\text{deg}(T)+2-1}\|N^{-1/2}R^{(S)}\mathbf{u}\|_{2}^{ro(v)}\|N^{-1/2}R^{(S)}\mathbf{x}\|_{2}^{rn(v)}\|N^{-1}R^{(S)}\|_{\mathcal{U}_{k}}\|T\|_{\mathcal{U}_{k}}

\displaystyle\prec N^{-1/2}N^{\delta}\times\Phi^{r(v)}\times\|(\sqrt{N})^{\text{deg}(T)}T\|_{\mathcal{U}_{k}}

\displaystyle\prec N^{-1/2}N^{\delta}\times\Phi^{r(v)}\times\max_{f_{\mathcal{G}^{\prime}}\in\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{G}^{\prime}}}|\mathrm{val}(\mathcal{G}^{\prime},f_{\mathcal{G}^{\prime}})|.

This covers all cases where
m1(v)\geq 1
.

Next, suppose
l(v)+e(v)=1
and
r(v)+m1(v)=0
. Then, since
\text{deg}(v)\geq 3
,
v
is adjacent to at least one type 2 matrix vertex, so a
representative form of
\mathrm{val}(\mathcal{G},f_{\mathcal{G}})
is bounded as

\left|\left\langle\kappa_{k}(\mathbf{g}_{i}),\mathbf{u}^{\otimes l(v)}\otimes\mathbf{v}_{\beta}^{\otimes e(v)}\otimes T\right\rangle\right|\prec(\sqrt{N})^{\text{deg}(T)-1}\|T\|_{\mathcal{U}_{k}}\prec N^{-1/2}\times\max_{f_{\mathcal{G}^{\prime}}\in\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{G}^{\prime}}}|\mathrm{val}(\mathcal{G}^{\prime},f_{\mathcal{G}^{\prime}})|.

This again implies the lemma since
r(v)=0
and
\delta\geq 0
.
This shows part (a).

For (b), if
m1(v)\geq 1
, then the statement is implied by (a) as
\Phi\leq 1
. Suppose then that
m1(v)=0
. If
v
is adjacent to at least one
type 2 matrix vertex,
then a representative form of
\mathrm{val}(\mathcal{G},f_{\mathcal{G}})
is bounded as

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle\kappa_{k}(\mathbf{g}_{i}),\mathbf{u}^{\otimes l(v)}\otimes\mathbf{v}_{\beta}^{\otimes e(v)}\otimes(N^{-1/2}R^{(S)}\mathbf{u})^{ro(v)}\otimes(N^{-1/2}R^{(S)}\mathbf{x})^{\otimes rn(v)}\otimes T\right\rangle\right|

\displaystyle\prec(\sqrt{N})^{\text{deg}(T)-1}\Phi^{r(v)}\|T\|_{\mathcal{U}_{k}}\prec N^{-1/2}\times\Phi^{r(v)}\times\max_{(\mathcal{G}^{\prime},f_{\mathcal{G}^{\prime}})\in\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{G}^{\prime}}}|\mathrm{val}(\mathcal{G}^{\prime},f_{\mathcal{G}^{\prime}})|

which again implies the lemma.
Finally, if
v
is not adjacent to any matrix vertex, then all neighbors of
v

have degree 1, and
v
and its neighbors constitute all of
\mathcal{G}
which is a
star. Isolating one factor of
R^{(S)}\mathbf{u}
or
R^{(S)}\mathbf{x}
as
T

in Assumption
3
,
a representative form of
\mathrm{val}(\mathcal{G},f_{\mathcal{G}})
is bounded as

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle\kappa_{k}(\mathbf{g}_{i}),\mathbf{u}^{\otimes l(v)}\otimes\mathbf{v}_{\beta}^{\otimes e(v)}\otimes(N^{-1/2}R^{(S)}\mathbf{u})^{ro(v)}\otimes(N^{-1/2}R^{(S)}\mathbf{x})^{\otimes rn(v)}\right\rangle\right|

\displaystyle\prec\max\{N^{-1/2}\|R^{(S)}\mathbf{x}\|_{\mathcal{U}_{k}},N^{-1/2}\|R^{(S)}\mathbf{u}\|_{\mathcal{U}_{k}}\}\times\max\{N^{-1/2}\|R^{(S)}\mathbf{u}\|_{2},N^{-1/2}\|R^{(S)}\mathbf{x}\|_{2}\}^{r(v)-1}

\displaystyle\prec N^{-1/2}N^{\delta}\times\Phi^{r(v)-1}.

This implies the lemma since
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
is empty, completing the proof.
∎

3.5.4.
Proof of Lemma
3.6
(a)

We now focus attention on networks with left leaves and no eigen-leaves, and
show Lemma
3.6
(a).
In addition to Definition
3.16
, we define the following.

Definition 3.22
.

Let
(\mathcal{G},f_{\mathcal{G}})
be a
(S,L)
-valid tensor network that contains no
eigen-leaves. We say that a left leaf
u\in V_{\mathcal{G}}^{l}
is
singular
if
its adjacent tensor vertex
v\in V_{\mathcal{G}}^{t}
is not adjacent to any left leaves
other than
u
, and we call
v
a
singular tensor vertex
. We write

V_{\mathcal{G}}^{sl},V_{\mathcal{G}}^{st}
for the sets of such vertices, and
t(u)=v

for the one-to-one correspondence between these two sets.

The following lemma now expresses products of monomials arising from
Lemma
3.11
via tensor network values.

Lemma 3.23
.

Fix any integer
L\geq 1
. For any
i_{1},\ldots,i_{2L}\in[N]
, denote

S=\{i_{1},\ldots,i_{2L}\}
the set of distinct indices. Let
q_{1},\ldots,q_{2L}

be a sequence of monomials satisfying the conditions of Lemma

3.11
, where each
q_{l}
is a monomial from the expansion
of
\mathcal{Y}_{i_{l}}^{(i_{l})}\mathcal{Q}_{i_{l}}^{(i_{l})}
, and let

q=\prod_{l=1}^{L}q_{l}\prod_{l=L+1}^{2L}\bar{q}_{l}
.
Then there exists a constant
C\equiv C(L)>0
, an exponent
m\in[0,C]
,
and a set
\mathcal{N}
of
(S,2L)
-valid tensor networks with
|\mathcal{N}|\leq C
,
such that

|\mathbb{E}_{S}q|=|\mathcal{C}^{(S)}|^{m}\cdot\sum_{(\mathcal{G},f_{\mathcal{G}})\in\mathcal{N}}|\mathrm{val}(\mathcal{G},f_{\mathcal{G}})|

Moreover, for all
(\mathcal{G},f_{\mathcal{G}})\in\mathcal{N}
, there exist disjoint subsets of singular left leaves
U_{\mathcal{G}}^{1},U_{\mathcal{G}}^{2}\subseteq V_{\mathcal{G}}^{sl}
such that the following holds:

(a)

We have
|V_{\mathcal{G}}^{l}|=|V_{\mathcal{G}}^{r}|=|V_{\mathcal{G}}^{ro}|=2L
and

|V_{\mathcal{G}}^{rn}|=|V_{\mathcal{G}}^{e}|=0
. (I.e., there are no right new leaves or
eigen-leaves, and exactly
2L
left leaves and
2L
right original leaves.)

(b)

Let
K\subseteq S
be the set of indices that appear exactly once
in the list
(i_{1},\ldots,i_{2L})
. Then
|K|=|U_{\mathcal{G}}^{1}|+|U_{\mathcal{G}}^{2}|
.

(c)

We have
|V_{\mathcal{G}}^{m2}|\geq|U_{\mathcal{G}}^{1}|
.

(d)

For all
u\in U_{\mathcal{G}}^{2}
, we have
\text{deg}(t(u))\geq 3

and
m1(t(u))\geq 1
.

Proof.

For each distinct index
i\in S
, collecting all appearances of
\mathbf{g}_{i}

and
(\mathbf{g}_{i}\mathbf{g}_{i}^{*}-\Sigma)
across
q_{1},\ldots,q_{2L}
into a single tensor, and
then evaluating the expectation
\mathbb{E}_{S}
over the independent vectors
\{\mathbf{g}_{i}\}_{i\in S}
, it is clear from the forms of

\mathcal{Y}_{i}^{(S)},\mathcal{Z}_{ijk}^{(S)},\mathcal{B}_{jk}^{(S)},\mathcal{P}_{j}^{(S)},\mathcal{C}^{(S)}

that
\mathbb{E}_{S}q
may be expressed as a product of factors
\mathcal{C}^{(S)}
,

\overline{\mathcal{C}^{(S)}}
and contractions of tensors of the form

\{\mathbb{E}[(\mathbf{g}_{i}\mathbf{g}_{i}^{*}-\Sigma)^{\otimes k}\otimes\mathbf{g}_{i}^{\otimes m}]:i\in S\text{ and }k,m\in[0,C(L)]\}
with matrices and vectors of the forms

\mathcal{L}:=\{\mathbf{u},\bar{\mathbf{u}}\},\quad\mathcal{R}_{o}:=\left\{\frac{R^{(S)}\mathbf{u}}{\sqrt{N}},\frac{\overline{R^{(S)}\mathbf{u}}}{\sqrt{N}}\right\},\quad\mathcal{I}_{m}:=\left\{\frac{R^{(S)}}{N},\frac{\overline{R^{(S)}}}{N}\right\}.

For each distinct index
i\in S
, we may then decompose the corresponding
moment tensor using Lemma
3.15
,

\mathbb{E}[(\mathbf{g}_{i}\mathbf{g}_{i}^{*}-\Sigma)^{\otimes k}\otimes\mathbf{g}_{i}^{\otimes m}]=\sum_{\pi\in\dot{P}_{k,m}}\kappa_{\pi}(\mathbf{g}_{i}).

Then expanding by multi-linearity, we claim that
\mathbb{E}_{S}q
is a product of
factors
\mathcal{C}^{(S)},\overline{\mathcal{C}^{(S)}}
with

\sum_{(\mathcal{G},f_{\mathcal{G}})\in\mathcal{N}}|\mathrm{val}(\mathcal{G},f_{\mathcal{G}})|
over a family
\mathcal{N}

of
(S,2L)
-valid tensor networks, where
|\mathcal{N}|\leq C(L)
.

To see this claim, consider as an illustrative example

q_{1}=\mathcal{Z}_{ijj}^{(ij)}\mathcal{C}^{(ij)},\qquad q_{2}=\mathcal{Z}_{jii}^{(ij)}\mathcal{C}^{(ij)},\qquad q=q_{1}\bar{q}_{2}

for two distinct indices
S=\{i,j\}
. Then, defining a permutation

Q:(\mathbb{C}^{n})^{8}\to(\mathbb{C}^{n})^{8}
such that

Q(T)(i_{1},\ldots,i_{8})=T(i_{1},i_{2},i_{7},i_{8},i_{5},i_{6},i_{3},i_{4})
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\{ij\}}[q]

\displaystyle=|\mathcal{C}^{(ij)}|^{2}\mathbb{E}_{\{ij\}}\Big[\mathbf{u}^{*}(\mathbf{g}_{i}\mathbf{g}_{i}^{*}-\Sigma)(N^{-1}R^{(ij)})\mathbf{g}_{j}\mathbf{g}_{j}^{*}(N^{-1/2}R^{(ij)}\mathbf{u})\times

\displaystyle\hskip 144.54pt\overline{\mathbf{u}^{*}(\mathbf{g}_{j}\mathbf{g}_{j}^{*}-\Sigma)(N^{-1}R^{(ij)})\mathbf{g}_{i}\mathbf{g}_{i}^{*}(N^{-1/2}R^{(ij)}\mathbf{u})}\Big]

\displaystyle=|\mathcal{C}^{(ij)}|^{2}\left\langle\mathbb{E}\left[(\mathbf{g}_{i}\mathbf{g}_{i}^{*}-\Sigma)\otimes\mathbf{g}_{i}^{\otimes 2}\right]\otimes\mathbb{E}\left[(\mathbf{g}_{j}\mathbf{g}_{j}^{*}-\Sigma)\otimes\mathbf{g}_{j}^{\otimes 2}\right],\right.

\displaystyle\hskip 72.26999pt\left.Q\Big(\bar{\mathbf{u}}\otimes N^{-1}R^{(ij)}\otimes N^{-1/2}R^{(ij)}\mathbf{u}\otimes\mathbf{u}\otimes\overline{N^{-1}R^{(ij)}}\otimes\overline{N^{-1/2}R^{(ij)}\mathbf{u}}\Big)\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\sum_{\pi_{1}\in\dot{P}_{1,2}}\sum_{\pi_{2}\in\dot{P}_{1,2}}|\mathcal{C}^{(ij)}|^{2}\Big\langle\kappa_{\pi_{1}}(\mathbf{g}_{i})\otimes\kappa_{\pi_{2}}(\mathbf{g}_{j}),

\displaystyle\hskip 108.405ptQ\Big(\bar{\mathbf{u}}\otimes N^{-1}R^{(ij)}\otimes N^{-1/2}R^{(ij)}\mathbf{u}\otimes\mathbf{u}\otimes\overline{N^{-1}R^{(ij)}}\otimes\overline{N^{-1/2}R^{(ij)}\mathbf{u}}\Big)\Big\rangle.

The summand for each pair of partitions
(\pi_{1},\pi_{2})
corresponds to

|\mathcal{C}^{(ij)}|^{2}
times
\mathrm{val}(\mathcal{G},f_{\mathcal{G}})
for a (possibly disconnected)

(S,2L)
