-valid tensor network. For example, the partitions

\pi_{1}=\{\{1,3\},\{2,4\}\}
and
\pi_{2}=\{\{1,2,3,4\}\}
correspond to the
network

\kappa_{4}(\mathbf{g}_{j})
\mathbf{u}
i_{5}
\frac{R^{(ij)}\mathbf{u}}{\sqrt{N}}
i_{4}
\frac{R^{(ij)}}{N}
\kappa_{2}(\mathbf{g}_{i})
\mathbf{u}
i_{6}
i_{7}
i_{1}
\frac{R^{(ij)}}{N}
\kappa_{2}(\mathbf{g}_{i})
\frac{R^{(ij)}\mathbf{u}}{\sqrt{N}}
i_{3}
i_{2}
i_{8}

while the
partitions
\pi_{1}=\{\{1,4\},\{2,3\}\}
and
\pi_{2}=\{\{1,4\},\{2,3\}\}

correspond to the network

\kappa_{2}(\mathbf{g}_{i})
\kappa_{2}(\mathbf{g}_{j})
\frac{R^{(ij)}}{N}
\frac{R^{(ij)}}{N}
i_{2}
i_{3}
i_{7}
i_{6}
\mathbf{u}
\kappa_{2}(\mathbf{g}_{i})
\frac{R^{(ij)}\mathbf{u}}{\sqrt{N}}
i_{1}
i_{8}
\mathbf{u}
\kappa_{2}(\mathbf{g}_{j})
\frac{R^{(ij)}\mathbf{u}}{\sqrt{N}}
i_{5}
i_{4}

More generally, any such monomial
q
may be decomposed in this way, where it is
clear by construction that each resulting
(\mathcal{G},f_{\mathcal{G}})
satisfies conditions (a)
and (b) of Definition
3.16
, that
\mathcal{G}=(V_{\mathcal{G}},E_{\mathcal{G}})

is bipartite and
\text{deg}(v)=\text{deg}(f_{\mathcal{G}}(v))
for all
v\in V_{\mathcal{G}}
.
It satisfies condition (c) by the following reasoning: If

u\in V_{\mathcal{G}}^{m}
, then its label
f_{\mathcal{G}}(u)
must be a copy of

N^{-1}R^{(S)}
or
\overline{N^{-1}R^{(S)}}
arising from a term

\mathcal{Z}_{ijk}^{(S)}
,
\mathcal{B}_{jk}^{(S)}
, or
\mathcal{P}_{j}^{(S)}
. For
\mathcal{Z}_{ijk}^{(S)}
,
this vertex
u
must be type 2 by the constraint
i\neq j
in
\mathcal{Z}_{ijk}^{(S)}
, and the same holds for
\mathcal{B}_{jk}^{(S)}
by the constraint
j\neq k
.
Thus
u\in V_{\mathcal{G}}^{m}
can only be type 1 — i.e. both edges connect to a
single neighbor
v\in V_{\mathcal{G}}^{t}
— if

f_{\mathcal{G}}(u)
arises from a term
\mathcal{P}_{j}^{(S)}
. In this case, its neighbor
v\in V_{\mathcal{G}}^{t}
cannot have
\text{deg}(v)=2

because the partitions belonging to
\dot{P}_{k,m}

cannot have a cardinality-2 block that
represents a pairing of the two copies of
\mathbf{g}_{j}
in
\mathcal{P}_{j}^{(S)}
. Thus

\text{deg}(v)\geq 3
, verifying condition (c). This shows the first claim of
the lemma, that

|\mathbb{E}_{S}q|=|\mathcal{C}^{(S)}|^{m}\cdot\sum_{(\mathcal{G},f_{\mathcal{G}})\in\mathcal{N}}|\mathrm{val}(\mathcal{G},f_{\mathcal{G}})|

for some
m\in[0,C(L)]
and family
\mathcal{N}
of
(S,2L)
-valid tensor networks
with
|\mathcal{N}|\leq C(L)
.

We now prove the remaining claims of the lemma: Since each
q_{l}
contains
exactly one factor
\mathcal{Y}_{i_{l}}^{(S)}
or
\mathcal{Z}_{i_{l}jk}^{(S)}
, there are exactly

2L
appearances of
\mathbf{u}
and
2L
appearances of

N^{-1/2}R^{(S)}\mathbf{u}
in
q
. Thus
|V_{\mathcal{G}}^{l}|=|V_{\mathcal{G}}^{r}|=2L
. There are no
right new leaves or eigen-leaves in the above construction, so

|V_{\mathcal{G}}^{rn}|=|V_{\mathcal{G}}^{e}|=0
, and hence also
|V_{\mathcal{G}}^{r}|=|V_{\mathcal{G}}^{ro}|
. This proves
(a).

Next, let
K\subseteq S
be the subset of distinct indices which appear exactly
once in
(i_{1},\ldots,i_{2L})
. For each
i_{l}\in K
, there is exactly 1 factor
of the form
\mathcal{Y}_{i_{l}}^{(S)}
or
\mathcal{Z}_{i_{l}jk}^{(S)}
in
q
. It follows that
across all tensor vertices labeled by a cumulant of
\mathbf{g}_{i_{l}}
,
there is exactly one adjacent left leaf. This left leaf for each
i_{l}\in K
must then be singular.
Let
U_{\mathcal{G}}\subseteq V_{\mathcal{G}}^{sl}
be the subset of all such
singular left leaves corresponding to the indices
i_{l}\in K
,
so
|U_{\mathcal{G}}|=|K|
. We will partition
U_{\mathcal{G}}
into disjoint subsets

U_{\mathcal{G}}^{1},U_{\mathcal{G}}^{2}
, so that
|K|=|U_{\mathcal{G}}^{1}|+|U_{\mathcal{G}}^{2}|
, showing (b).

For each index
i_{l}\in K
, let
u(i_{l})\in U_{\mathcal{G}}
be the corresponding singular
left leaf identified above, and let
v(i_{l})=t(u(i_{l}))\in V_{\mathcal{G}}^{t}
be its
tensor vertex neighbor. We define
U_{\mathcal{G}}^{1}
to be the set of
u(i_{l})\in U_{\mathcal{G}}
satisfying either:

(1)

i_{l}
appears as a lower index in some
\{q_{l^{\prime}}:l^{\prime}\neq l\}
, or

(2)

i_{l}
does not appear as a lower index in any
\{q_{l^{\prime}}:l^{\prime}\neq l\}
,
and either
\text{deg}(v(i_{l}))=2
or
ro(v(i_{l}))+m2(v(i_{l}))\geq 2
.

To bound
|U_{\mathcal{G}}^{1}|
, let
m_{Z}(l),m_{B}^{*}(l),m_{B}(l)
be the counts of Lemma

3.11
for each
q_{l}
, and let

m_{P}^{*}(l)
be the number of factors
\mathcal{P}_{i_{l}}^{(S)}
in
q_{l}

for which the copy of
N^{-1}R^{(S)}
in
\mathcal{P}_{i_{l}}^{(S)}
labels a type 2
matrix vertex. (Note that this is different from the proof of Lemma

3.5
where
m_{P}^{*}(l)
counted all factors of

\mathcal{P}_{i_{l}}^{(S)}
in
q_{l}
.) Set

m_{Z}=\sum_{l=1}^{2L}m_{Z}(l),\qquad m_{B}^{*}=\sum_{l=1}^{2L}m_{B}^{*}(l),\qquad m_{B}=\sum_{l=1}^{2L}m_{B}(l),\qquad m_{P}^{*}=\sum_{l=1}^{2L}m_{P}^{*}(l).

Lemma
3.11
ensures that the number of vertices

u(i_{l})\in U_{\mathcal{G}}
satisfying condition (1.) is at most

m_{Z}+m_{B}+\frac{1}{2}m_{B}^{*}
.

For condition (2.), if
\text{deg}(v(i_{l}))=2
, let
u^{\prime}
be the vertex other than

u(i_{l})
which is incident to
v(i_{l})
. Then
u^{\prime}
is not a left leaf because

u(i_{l})
is singular. If
u^{\prime}
is a right leaf, then its label
f_{\mathcal{G}}(u^{\prime})
must
be the copy of
N^{-1/2}R^{(S)}\mathbf{u}
multiplying
\mathbf{g}_{i_{l}}^{*}
in some factor

\mathcal{Z}_{ijk}^{(S)}
of
q
with
k=i_{l}
, or multiplying

(\mathbf{g}_{i_{l}}\mathbf{g}_{i_{l}}^{*}-\Sigma)
in the factor
\mathcal{Y}_{i_{l}}^{(S)}
of
q_{l}
.
The former is not possible because
i_{l}
does not appear as a lower index of any

\{q_{l^{\prime}}:l^{\prime}\neq l\}
, and the latter is not possible because the partitions
belonging to
\dot{P}_{k,m}
cannot pair the two copies of
\mathbf{g}_{i_{l}}
in

\mathcal{Y}_{i_{l}}^{(S)}
. Thus
u^{\prime}
is
also not a right leaf, so it must be a type 2 matrix vertex. Since

i_{l}
does not appear as a lower index of any
\{q_{l^{\prime}}:l^{\prime}\neq l\}
, and since

\dot{P}_{k,m}
cannot pair the two copies of
\mathbf{g}_{i_{l}}
in either

\mathcal{Z}_{i_{l}}^{(S)}
or
\mathcal{P}_{i_{l}}^{(S)}
,
the label
f_{\mathcal{G}}(u^{\prime})
must be the matrix
N^{-1}R^{(S)}
arising from a factor

\mathcal{B}_{i_{l}j}^{(S)}
of
q_{l}
. Since
m_{B}^{*}(l)
is even by
Lemma
3.11
, this means
\frac{1}{2}m_{B}^{*}(l)\geq 1
.
By similar reasoning, if
\text{deg}(v(i_{l}))\geq 3
and

ro(v(i_{l}))+m2(v(i_{l}))\geq 2
, then the factor
\mathcal{Y}_{i_{l}}^{(S)}
or

\mathcal{Z}_{i_{l}}^{(S)}
of
q
can contribute at most one right original leaf
or type 2 matrix vertex neighbor for
v(i_{l})
. Thus,
since
ro(v(i_{l}))+m2(v(i_{l}))\geq 2
,
v(i_{l})
has some other
neighboring type 2 matrix vertex, which must arise from
a factor
\mathcal{P}_{i_{l}}^{(S)}
or
\mathcal{B}_{i_{l}j}^{(S)}
of
q_{l}
. Since
m_{B}^{*}(l)
is
even, this means
\frac{1}{2}m_{B}^{*}(l)+m_{P}^{*}(l)\geq 1
. Thus the
number of vertices
u\in U_{\mathcal{G}}
satisfying condition (2.) is at
most
\frac{1}{2}m_{B}^{*}+m_{P}^{*}
. Putting this together,

|U_{\mathcal{G}}^{1}|\leq\underbrace{m_{Z}+m_{B}+\frac{1}{2}m_{B}^{*}}_{\text{condition (1.)}}+\underbrace{\frac{1}{2}m_{B}^{*}+m_{P}^{*}}_{\text{condition (2.)}}=m_{Z}+m_{B}+m_{B}^{*}+m_{P}^{*}\leq|V_{\mathcal{G}}^{m2}|,

the last inequality holding because each factor counted by

m_{Z},m_{B},m_{B}^{*},m_{P}^{*}
has a copy of
N^{-1}R^{(S)}
labeling a different type 2
matrix vertex. This shows (c).

Finally, by construction, all remaining vertices
u(i_{l})\in U_{\mathcal{G}}^{2}=U_{\mathcal{G}}\setminus U_{\mathcal{G}}^{1}
satisfy
\text{deg}(v(i_{l}))\geq 3
and

ro(v(i_{l}))+m2(v(i_{l}))\leq 1
. Also

rn(v(i_{l}))=e(v(i_{l}))=0
, and
l(v(i_{l}))=1
since
u(i_{l})
is singular. Then
the condition
\text{deg}(v(i_{l}))\geq 3
requires
m_{1}(v(i_{l}))\geq 1
.
This shows (d) and completes the proof.
∎

After removing all tensor vertices
v\in V_{\mathcal{G}}^{t}
with
\text{deg}(v)\geq 3
,
the following lemma will be used to bound the value of a tensor network with
only chains and cycles.

Lemma 3.24
.

Suppose the conditions of Lemma
3.6
hold.
Let
(\mathcal{G},f_{\mathcal{G}})
be a
(S,L)
-valid tensor network that contains no
eigen-leaves, and such that
\text{deg}(v)=2
for all
v\in V_{\mathcal{G}}^{t}
. (This
implies that
\mathcal{G}
is a simple graph with no type 1 matrix vertices and only
chains and cycles.) Then for any subset (possibly empty) of singular left
leaves
U_{\mathcal{G}}\subseteq V_{\mathcal{G}}^{sl}
, uniformly over all
|S|\leq L
and
z\in\mathbf{D}
,

|\mathrm{val}(\mathcal{G},f_{\mathcal{G}})|\prec\left(\frac{N^{\delta}}{\sqrt{N}}\right)^{|U_{\mathcal{G}}|}\times\Phi^{|V_{\mathcal{G}}^{r}|+|V_{\mathcal{G}}^{m2}|-|U_{\mathcal{G}}|}

Proof.

By Remark
3.18
, we may consider without loss of
generality the case where
\mathcal{G}
is a single connected component.
We will use repeatedly that under the conditions of Lemma

3.6
, we have also

N^{-1}\|R^{(S)}\|_{F}\leq\max_{\alpha}N^{-1/2}\|R^{(S)}\mathbf{e}_{\alpha}\|_{2}\prec\Phi

Since
\text{deg}(v)=2
for each
v\in V_{\mathcal{G}}^{t}
, all tensor vertices have label

\Sigma
. If
\mathcal{G}
is a chain, we write
u_{1},u_{2}
for the two endpoints of the
chain. We then consider all possible cases for
\mathcal{G}
:

(1) Suppose
\mathcal{G}
is a cycle. Then a representative form of
(\mathcal{G},f_{\mathcal{G}})
is

\frac{R^{(S)}}{N}
\Sigma
\frac{R^{(S)}}{N}
\Sigma
\Sigma
\frac{R^{(S)}}{N}
\Sigma
\frac{R^{(S)}}{N}

The number of vertices with label
N^{-1}R^{(S)}
in the cycle is
|V_{\mathcal{G}}^{m2}|
,
which is at least two since these vertices are type 2. Thus the value is bounded
as

\operatorname{Tr}(N^{-1}R^{(S)}\Sigma)^{|V_{\mathcal{G}}^{m2}|}\leq\|\Sigma\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{|V_{\mathcal{G}}^{m2}|}\times(N^{-1}\|R^{(S)}\|_{F})^{|V_{\mathcal{G}}^{m2}|}\prec\Phi^{|V_{\mathcal{G}}^{m2}|}.

This implies the lemma, since
|U_{\mathcal{G}}|=|V_{\mathcal{G}}^{r}|=0
.

(2) Suppose
\mathcal{G}
is a chain, with endpoints
u_{1},u_{2}\notin U_{\mathcal{G}}
. These
endpoints may be left leaves, right original leaves, or right new leaves,
leading to the following 6 types of representative networks
(\mathcal{G},f_{\mathcal{G}})
:

\mathbf{u}
\Sigma
\dots
\Sigma
\mathbf{u}
\mathbf{u}
\Sigma
\dots
\Sigma
\frac{R^{(S)}\mathbf{x}}{\sqrt{N}}
\frac{R^{(S)}\mathbf{x}}{\sqrt{N}}
\Sigma
\dots
\Sigma
\frac{R^{(S)}\mathbf{x}}{\sqrt{N}}
\mathbf{u}
\Sigma
\dots
\Sigma
\frac{R^{(S)}\mathbf{u}}{\sqrt{N}}
\frac{R^{(S)}\mathbf{u}}{\sqrt{N}}
\Sigma
\dots
\Sigma
\frac{R^{(S)}\mathbf{u}}{\sqrt{N}}
\frac{R^{(S)}\mathbf{u}}{\sqrt{N}}
\Sigma
\dots
\Sigma
\frac{R^{(S)}\mathbf{x}}{\sqrt{N}}

In each case, we may apply
\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}=1
,

\|\Sigma\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq C
,
\|N^{-1}R^{(S)}\|_{F}\prec\Phi
,

\|N^{-1/2}R^{(S)}\mathbf{u}\|_{2}\prec\Phi
, and

\|N^{-1/2}R^{(S)}\mathbf{x}\|_{2}\prec\Phi
to get

|\mathrm{val}(\mathcal{G},f_{\mathcal{G}})|\prec\Phi^{|V_{\mathcal{G}}^{r}|+|V_{\mathcal{G}}^{m2}|}.

We clarify that in particular, if
|V_{\mathcal{G}}^{r}|+|V_{\mathcal{G}}^{m2}|=0
, this is the bound

|\mathbf{u}^{*}\Sigma\mathbf{u}|\prec 1
. Here, since
|U_{\mathcal{G}}|=0
, the lemma also holds.

(3) Suppose
\mathcal{G}
is a chain and exactly one of
u_{1},u_{2}
, say
u_{1}
, belongs to

U_{\mathcal{G}}
. Then
u_{1}
is singular, so its adjacent tensor vertex
t(u_{1})
cannot
be adjacent to any other left leaves, meaning the other neighbor of
t(u_{1})
is
a matrix vertex or a right leaf. If
|V_{\mathcal{G}}^{r}|+|V_{\mathcal{G}}^{m2}|=1
, then we have the
cases

\mathbf{u}
u_{1}
\Sigma
t(u_{1})
\frac{R^{(S)}\mathbf{u}}{\sqrt{N}}
\mathbf{u}
u_{1}
\Sigma
t(u_{1})
\frac{R^{(S)}\mathbf{x}}{\sqrt{N}}
\mathbf{u}
u_{1}
\Sigma
t(u_{1})
\frac{R^{(S)}}{N}
\Sigma
\mathbf{u}

For these cases, we apply the bounds

\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}|\mathbf{u}^{*}\Sigma R^{(S)}\mathbf{u}|\prec\frac{N^{\delta}}{\sqrt{N}},\qquad\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}|\mathbf{u}^{*}\Sigma R^{(S)}\mathbf{x}|\prec\frac{N^{\delta}}{\sqrt{N}},\qquad\frac{1}{N}|\mathbf{u}^{*}\Sigma R^{(S)}\Sigma\mathbf{u}|\prec\frac{N^{\delta}}{N},

which imply the lemma since
|U_{\mathcal{G}}|=1
and
|V_{\mathcal{G}}^{r}|+|V_{\mathcal{G}}^{m2}|=1
.
If
|V_{\mathcal{G}}^{r}|+|V_{\mathcal{G}}^{m2}|\geq 2
, then
t(u_{1})
must be adjacent to a matrix vertex, and we are left with the following possibilities:

\mathbf{u}
u_{1}
\Sigma
t(u_{1})
\frac{R^{(S)}}{N}
\dots
\frac{R^{(S)}}{N}
\Sigma
\mathbf{u}
\mathbf{u}
u_{1}
\Sigma
t(u_{1})
\frac{R^{(S)}}{N}
\dots
\Sigma
\frac{R^{(S)}\mathbf{u}}{\sqrt{N}}
\mathbf{u}
u_{1}
\Sigma
t(u_{1})
\frac{R^{(S)}}{N}
\dots
\Sigma
\frac{R^{(S)}\mathbf{x}}{\sqrt{N}}

In these cases, we may contract the labels
\mathbf{u}
and
\Sigma
on
u_{1}

and
t(u_{1})
with the first
N^{-1}R^{(S)}
, to get

|\mathrm{val}(\mathcal{G},f_{\mathcal{G}})|\prec\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\Phi^{|V_{\mathcal{G}}^{r}|+|V_{\mathcal{G}}^{m2}|}.

For example,

\displaystyle|\mathbf{u}^{*}\Sigma(N^{-1}R^{(S)}\Sigma)^{|V_{\mathcal{G}}^{m2}|}(N^{-1/2}R^{(S)}\mathbf{u})|

\displaystyle\prec\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\|N^{-1/2}\mathbf{u}^{*}\Sigma R^{(S)}\|_{2}\times\|N^{-1}R^{(S)}\|_{F}^{|V_{\mathcal{G}}^{m2}|-1}\times\|N^{-1/2}R^{(S)}\mathbf{u}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\prec\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\Phi^{|V_{\mathcal{G}}^{r}|+|V_{\mathcal{G}}^{m2}|}.

This is stronger than the lemma, because
|U_{\mathcal{G}}|=1
,
\Phi\leq 1
, and
\delta\geq 0
.

(4) Finally, suppose both endpoints
u_{1},u_{2}\in U_{\mathcal{G}}
. Then
|V_{\mathcal{G}}^{r}|=0
.
If
|V_{\mathcal{G}}^{m2}|=1
, then a representative case is

\mathbf{u}
u_{1}
\Sigma
t(u_{1})
\frac{R^{(S)}}{N}
\Sigma
t(u_{2})
\mathbf{u}
u_{2}

We apply the bound

\frac{1}{N}|\mathbf{u}^{*}\Sigma R^{(S)}\Sigma\mathbf{u}|\prec\frac{N^{\delta}}{N}\prec\left(\frac{N^{\delta}}{\sqrt{N}}\right)^{2},

which implies the lemma since
|U_{\mathcal{G}}|=2
and
\Phi\leq 1
.
If
|V_{\mathcal{G}}^{m2}|\geq 2
, then since
u_{1},u_{2}
are singular left leaves, their
adjacent tensor vertices
t(u_{1}),t(u_{2})
must be adjacent to matrix vertices.
A representative case is

\mathbf{u}
u_{1}
\Sigma
t(u_{1})
\frac{R^{(S)}}{N}
\dots
\frac{R^{(S)}}{N}
\Sigma
t(u_{2})
\mathbf{u}
u_{2}

for which we may bound

|\mathbf{u}^{*}\Sigma(N^{-1}R^{(S)}\Sigma)^{|V_{\mathcal{G}}^{m2}|}\mathbf{u}|\prec\frac{1}{N}\|N^{-1/2}\mathbf{u}^{*}\Sigma R^{(S)}\|_{2}\times\|N^{-1}R^{(S)}\|_{F}^{|V_{\mathcal{G}}^{m2}|-2}\times\|N^{-1/2}R^{(S)}\Sigma\mathbf{u}\|_{2}\prec\frac{1}{N}\Phi^{|V_{\mathcal{G}}^{m2}|}.

This again implies the lemma since
|U_{\mathcal{G}}|=2
,
\Phi\leq 1
, and
\delta\geq 0
. This completes the proof.
∎

Proof of Lemma
3.6
(a).

We recall from (
3.15
) that, for any fixed constants
L\geq 1

and
D>0
, we have

(3.22)

\mathbb{E}\left|\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathcal{Y}_{i}^{(i)}\mathcal{Q}_{i}^{(i)}\right|^{2L}\prec\max_{\begin{subarray}{c}i_{1},\ldots,i_{2L}\in[N]\\
q_{1}\in\mathcal{M}_{i_{1},S},\ldots,q_{2L}\in\mathcal{M}_{i_{2L},S}\end{subarray}}\left\{N^{|S|-L}\mathbb{E}|\mathbb{E}_{S}q|\right\}+N^{-\tau^{\prime}(D+1)+L}.

where
S=\{i_{1},\ldots,i_{2L}\}
is the set of distinct indices in

i_{1},\ldots,i_{2L}
, the monomials
q_{l}\in\mathcal{M}_{i_{l},S}
are those arising in
the expansion of
\mathcal{Y}_{i_{l}}^{(i_{l})}\mathcal{Q}_{i_{l}}^{(i_{l})}
via
Lemma
3.11
, and
q=\prod_{l=1}^{L}q_{l}\prod_{l=L+1}^{2L}\bar{q}_{l}
. By Lemma
3.23
and the bound

|\mathcal{C}^{(S)}|\prec 1
, there exists a family
\mathcal{N}
of

(S,2L)
-valid tensor networks for which

\displaystyle|\mathbb{E}_{S}q|

\displaystyle\prec\sum_{(\mathcal{G},f_{\mathcal{G}})\in\mathcal{N}}|\mathrm{val}(\mathcal{G},f_{\mathcal{G}})|\prec\max_{(\mathcal{G},f_{\mathcal{G}})\in\mathcal{N}}|\mathrm{val}(\mathcal{G},f_{\mathcal{G}})|,

where the second inequality applies
|\mathcal{N}|\leq C
. It remains to bound

\mathrm{val}(\mathcal{G},f_{\mathcal{G}})
for each
(\mathcal{G},f_{\mathcal{G}})\in\mathcal{N}
.

The proof for each
(\mathcal{G},f_{\mathcal{G}})\in\mathcal{N}
proceeds by reducing
(\mathcal{G},f_{\mathcal{G}})
via
a sequence of four steps. In
each of the first three steps, we sequentially apply either
Corollary
3.20
or Lemma
3.21

to remove a single tensor vertex
v\in V_{\mathcal{G}}^{t}
with
\text{deg}(v)\geq 3
.
After removing all such vertices, the graph is reduced to chains and cycles, and
we apply Lemma
3.24
in the fourth step to complete the proof.
Letting
U_{\mathcal{G}}^{1},U_{\mathcal{G}}^{2}\subseteq V_{\mathcal{G}}^{sl}
be the disjoint
subsets of singular left leaves in Lemma
3.23
(which are fixed throughout the below process),
the four steps are as follows:

(1)

While there exists a singular left leaf
u\in U_{\mathcal{G}}^{1}\cup U_{\mathcal{G}}^{2}

incident to a tensor vertex
v=t(u)\in V_{\mathcal{G}}^{t}
such that
\text{deg}(v)\geq 3
and
either
m1(v)\geq 1
or
r(v)+m1(v)=0
: Remove
v
, and apply Lemma
3.21
(a) to bound

|\mathrm{val}(\mathcal{G},f_{\mathcal{G}})|\prec\frac{N^{\delta}}{\sqrt{N}}\Phi^{r(v)}\max_{f_{G^{\prime}}\in\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{G}^{\prime}}}|\mathrm{val}(\mathcal{G}^{\prime},f_{\mathcal{G}^{\prime}})|.

(2)

While there exists a singular left leaf
u\in U_{\mathcal{G}}^{1}
incident to a
tensor vertex
v\in V_{\mathcal{G}}^{t}
such that
\text{deg}(v)\geq 3
and
r(v)\geq 1
: Remove
v
, and apply Lemma
3.21
(b) to bound

|\mathrm{val}(\mathcal{G},f_{\mathcal{G}})|\prec\frac{N^{\delta}}{\sqrt{N}}\Phi^{r(v)-1}\max_{f_{G^{\prime}}\in\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{G}^{\prime}}}|\mathrm{val}(\mathcal{G}^{\prime},f_{\mathcal{G}^{\prime}})|.

(3)

While there exists a tensor vertex
v\in V_{\mathcal{G}}^{t}
such that
\text{deg}(v)\geq 3
: Remove
v
, and apply Lemma
3.20
to bound

|\mathrm{val}(\mathcal{G},f_{\mathcal{G}})|\prec\Phi^{r(v)}\max_{f_{G^{\prime}}\in\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{G}^{\prime}}}|\mathrm{val}(\mathcal{G}^{\prime},f_{\mathcal{G}^{\prime}})|.

(4)

At this point, the remaining network
(\mathcal{G},f_{\mathcal{G}})
is such that all

v\in V_{\mathcal{G}}^{t}
satisfy
\text{deg}(v)=2
. Let

|V_{\mathcal{G}}^{ro}(4)|
,
|V_{\mathcal{G}}^{rn}(4)|
,
|V_{\mathcal{G}}^{m2}(4)|
, and
|U_{\mathcal{G}}^{1}(4)|

denote the numbers of right original leaves, right new leaves,
(type 2) matrix vertices, and singular left leaves of
U_{\mathcal{G}}^{1}
in
(\mathcal{G},f_{\mathcal{G}})
.
We apply Lemma
3.24
to bound

|\mathrm{val}(\mathcal{G},f_{\mathcal{G}})|\prec\left(\frac{N^{\delta}}{\sqrt{N}}\right)^{|U_{\mathcal{G}}^{1}(4)|}\times\Phi^{|V_{\mathcal{G}}^{ro}(4)|+|V_{\mathcal{G}}^{rn}(4)|+|V_{\mathcal{G}}^{m2}(4)|-|U_{\mathcal{G}}^{1}(4)|}.

We clarify that in the 4 inequalities above,
(\mathcal{G},f_{\mathcal{G}})
on the left side
denotes the modified network after having applied all previous removals,

r(v)
on the right side denotes the number of right leaves
adjacent to
v
in this modified network
(\mathcal{G},f_{\mathcal{G}})
, and

(\mathcal{G}^{\prime},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{G}^{\prime}})
denotes the family of networks obtained by further
removing
v
from
(\mathcal{G},f_{\mathcal{G}})
according to
Definition
3.19
. Within Steps 1–3,
if there are multiple vertices that may be removed,
we may choose one to remove arbitrarily, but we do
this sequentially as each removal may increase
r(v^{\prime})
for the
remaining vertices
v^{\prime}\in V_{\mathcal{G}}^{t}
. For example, suppose two vertices
u_{1},u_{2}\in U_{\mathcal{G}}^{1}
with adjacent vertices
v_{1}=t(u_{1})
and
v_{2}=t(u_{2})
are
such that
\text{deg}(v_{1})\geq 3
and
\text{deg}(v_{2})\geq 3
,

r(v_{1})+m1(v_{1})=0
and
r(v_{2})+m1(v_{2})=0
, and

v_{1},v_{2}
are adjacent to a common type 2 matrix vertex
u\in V_{\mathcal{G}}^{m}
.
Pictorially, we have

\mathbf{u}
u_{1}
\kappa_{k}(\mathbf{g}_{i})
v_{1}
\dots
\frac{R^{(S)}}{N}
u
\kappa_{k}(\mathbf{g}_{i})
v_{2}
\dots
\mathbf{u}
u_{2}
Apply Step 1
\frac{R^{(S)}\mathbf{x}}{\sqrt{N}}
u
\kappa_{k}(\mathbf{g}_{i})
v_{2}
\dots
\mathbf{u}
u_{2}

Then Step 1 may first remove either
v_{1}
or
v_{2}
.
Upon removing, say,
v_{1}
, the adjacent matrix vertex
u

is replaced by a right new leaf according to
Definition
3.19
. Then
r(v_{2})\geq 1
in the
resulting graph, implying that
v_{2}
is no longer a candidate for removal in
Step 1, and instead will be removed in Step 2.

Since the removal of any vertex
v\in V_{\mathcal{G}}^{t}
does not change
\text{deg}(v^{\prime})
or

m_{1}(v^{\prime})
and can only increase
r(v^{\prime})
for each remaining
vertex
v^{\prime}\in V_{\mathcal{G}}^{t}
, the above procedure is forward-progressing in the sense
that each removal of a tensor vertex in Steps 1–3 cannot result in a tensor
vertex being eligible for removal by a previous step.

For each Step
k\in\{1,2,3\}
, let
|V_{\mathcal{G}}^{ro}(k)|
,
|V_{\mathcal{G}}^{rn}(k)|
,

|U_{\mathcal{G}}^{1}(k)|
, and
|U_{\mathcal{G}}^{2}(k)|
denote the total numbers of right original
leaves, right new leaves, singular left leaves of
U_{\mathcal{G}}^{1}
, and singular
left leaves of
U_{\mathcal{G}}^{2}
that are removed in Step
k
.
Importantly, all vertices
u\in U_{\mathcal{G}}^{2}
have
m1(t(u))\geq 1
by Lemma
3.23
(d), so after all applications of Step 1, the network has
no remaining vertices in
U_{\mathcal{G}}^{2}
. Thus,

|U_{\mathcal{G}}^{2}(2)|=|U_{\mathcal{G}}^{2}(3)|=0
, and the number of total applications of Step 2
is
|U_{\mathcal{G}}^{1}(2)|
. Also, after all applications of Steps 1 and 2, no singular
left leaf
u\in U_{\mathcal{G}}^{1}
has
\text{deg}(t(u))\geq 3
, so Step 3 does not
remove any vertices of
U_{\mathcal{G}}^{1}
, i.e.
|U_{\mathcal{G}}^{1}(3)|=0
.

Thus, letting
\mathrm{val}(\mathcal{G},f_{\mathcal{G}})
denote the value
of the starting network, the above steps yield the bound

\displaystyle|\mathrm{val}(\mathcal{G},f_{\mathcal{G}})|

\displaystyle\prec\underbrace{\Big(\frac{N^{\delta}}{\sqrt{N}}\Big)^{U_{\mathcal{G}}^{1}(1)+U_{\mathcal{G}}^{2}(1)}\Phi^{|V_{\mathcal{G}}^{ro}(1)|+|V_{\mathcal{G}}^{rn}(1)|}}_{\text{Step 1}}\times\underbrace{\Big(\frac{N^{\delta}}{\sqrt{N}}\Big)^{U_{\mathcal{G}}^{1}(2)}\Phi^{|V_{\mathcal{G}}^{ro}(2)|+|V_{\mathcal{G}}^{rn}(2)|-|U_{\mathcal{G}}^{1}(2)|}}_{\text{Step 2}}

\displaystyle\hskip 72.26999pt\times\underbrace{\Phi^{|V_{\mathcal{G}}^{ro}(3)|+|V_{\mathcal{G}}^{rn}(3)|}}_{\text{Step 3}}\times\underbrace{\Big(\frac{N^{\delta}}{\sqrt{N}}\Big)^{|U_{\mathcal{G}}^{1}(4)|}\Phi^{|V_{\mathcal{G}}^{ro}(4)|+|V_{\mathcal{G}}^{rn}(4)|+|V_{\mathcal{G}}^{m2}(4)|-|U_{\mathcal{G}}^{1}(4)|}}_{\text{Step
4}}.

Furthermore,
letting
|V_{\mathcal{G}}^{ro}|
,
|V_{\mathcal{G}}^{rn}|
,
|V_{\mathcal{G}}^{m2}|
,
|U_{\mathcal{G}}^{1}|
, and

|U_{\mathcal{G}}^{2}|
denote these quantities for the original network
(\mathcal{G},f_{\mathcal{G}})
, we
observe the following:

(1)

The singular left leaves removed in Steps 1–2 and those remaining
in Step 4 form a partition of
U_{\mathcal{G}}^{1}\cup U_{\mathcal{G}}^{2}
, so

|U_{\mathcal{G}}^{1}(1)|+|U_{\mathcal{G}}^{2}(1)|+|U_{\mathcal{G}}^{1}(2)|+|U_{\mathcal{G}}^{1}(4)|=|U_{\mathcal{G}}^{1}|+|U_{\mathcal{G}}^{2}|.

(2)

Similarly, the right original leaves removed in Steps 1–4 form a
partition of
V_{\mathcal{G}}^{ro}
, so

\sum_{k=1}^{4}|V_{\mathcal{G}}^{ro}(k)|=|V_{\mathcal{G}}^{ro}|.

(3)

By the removal process of Definition
3.19
, every right
new leaf removed in Steps 1–4 must have been a type 2 matrix vertex in the
original network
(\mathcal{G},f_{\mathcal{G}})
. Together with the type 2 matrix vertices
remaining in Step 4, this accounts for all type 2 matrix vertices of

(\mathcal{G},f_{\mathcal{G}})
. Thus

\sum_{k=1}^{4}|V_{\mathcal{G}}^{rn}(k)|+|V_{\mathcal{G}}^{m2}(4)|=|V_{\mathcal{G}}^{m2}|.

(4)

It is clear that we must have
|U_{\mathcal{G}}^{1}(2)|+|U_{\mathcal{G}}^{1}(4)|\leq|U_{\mathcal{G}}^{1}|
.

Thus, we have the bound

\displaystyle|\mathrm{val}(\mathcal{G},f_{\mathcal{G}})|

\displaystyle\prec\left(\frac{N^{\delta}}{\sqrt{N}}\right)^{|U_{\mathcal{G}}^{1}|+|U_{\mathcal{G}}^{2}|}\times\Phi^{|V_{\mathcal{G}}^{ro}|+|V_{\mathcal{G}}^{m2}|-|U_{\mathcal{G}}^{1}|}

\displaystyle\overset{(a)}{\leq}\left(\frac{N^{\delta}}{\sqrt{N}}\right)^{|K|}\times\Phi^{|V_{\mathcal{G}}^{ro}|}\overset{(b)}{\leq}N^{-|K|/2}\times(N^{\delta}\Phi)^{2L}\overset{(c)}{\leq}N^{-|S|+L}\times(N^{\delta}\Phi)^{2L}

where (a) applies
|U_{\mathcal{G}}^{1}|+|U_{\mathcal{G}}^{2}|=|K|
and

|V_{\mathcal{G}}^{m2}|\geq|U_{\mathcal{G}}^{1}|
from
Lemma
3.23
(b–c),
(b) applies
|K|\leq 2L
and
|V_{\mathcal{G}}^{ro}|=2L
by
Lemma
3.23
(a), and (c) applies the
bound
(2L-|K|)/2+|K|\geq|S|
.
Applying this bound to (
3.22
)
and choosing
D>0
large enough shows

\mathbb{E}\left|\sum_{i=1}^{N}N^{-1/2}\mathcal{Y}_{i}^{(i)}\mathcal{Q}_{i}^{(i)}\right|^{2L}\prec(N^{\delta}\Phi)^{2L},

and the desired bound follows from Markov’s inequality.
∎

3.5.5.
Proof of Lemma
3.6
(b)

The following lemma is similar to Lemma
3.23
.

Lemma 3.25
.

Fix any integer
L\geq 1
. For any
i_{1},\ldots,i_{2L},j_{1},\ldots j_{2L}\in[N]

where
i_{l}\neq j_{l}
, denote
S=\{i_{1},\ldots,i_{2L}\}\cup\{j_{1},\ldots,j_{2L}\}
the set of distinct indices. Let
q_{1},\ldots,q_{2L}
be a sequence of monomials
satisfying the conditions of Lemma
3.12
, where each

q_{l}
is a monomial in the expansion of

\mathcal{B}_{i_{l}j_{l}}^{(i_{l}j_{l})}\mathcal{Q}_{i_{l}}^{(i_{l})}\mathcal{Q}_{j_{l}}^{(i_{l}j_{l})}
. Let

q=\prod_{l=1}^{L}\prod_{l=L+1}^{2L}\bar{q}_{l}
. Then there exists a constant

C\equiv C(L)>0
, an exponent
m\in[0,C]
, and a set
\mathcal{N}
of
(S,4L)
-valid
tensor networks with
|\mathcal{N}|\leq C
such that

|\mathbb{E}_{S}q|=|\mathcal{C}^{(S)}|^{m}\cdot\sum_{(\mathcal{G},f_{\mathcal{G}})\in\mathcal{N}}|\mathrm{val}(\mathcal{G},f_{\mathcal{G}})|

Moreover, for all
(\mathcal{G},f_{\mathcal{G}})\in\mathcal{N}
, there exist disjoint subsets of
tensor vertices
U_{\mathcal{G}}^{1},U_{\mathcal{G}}^{2}\subseteq V_{\mathcal{G}}^{t}
such that the following
holds:

(a)

We have
|V_{\mathcal{G}}^{l}|=|V_{\mathcal{G}}^{r}|=0
. (I.e., there are no leaves, and the
only vertices are tensor vertices and matrix vertices.)

(b)

Let
K\subseteq S
be the subset of indices that appear exactly once
in the list
(i_{1},\ldots,i_{2L},j_{1},\ldots,j_{2L})
. Then
|K|=|U_{\mathcal{G}}^{1}|+|U_{\mathcal{G}}^{2}|
.

(c)

For all
v\in U_{\mathcal{G}}^{1}\cup U_{\mathcal{G}}^{2}
,
\text{deg}(v)
is odd and
\text{deg}(v)\geq 3
.

(d)

We have
|V_{\mathcal{G}}^{m2}|\geq 2L+|U_{\mathcal{G}}^{1}|
.

(e)

For all
v\in U_{\mathcal{G}}^{2}
,
m1(v)\geq 1
.

Proof.

The proof is similar to Lemma
3.23
,
so we omit some details. The same arguments as in
Lemma
3.23
show that

|\mathbb{E}_{S}q|=|\mathcal{C}^{(S)}|^{m}\cdot\sum_{(\mathcal{G},f_{\mathcal{G}})\in\mathcal{N}}|\mathrm{val}(\mathcal{G},f_{\mathcal{G}})|

for some
m\in[0,C(L)]
and family
\mathcal{N}
of
(S,4L)
-valid tensor networks
where
|\mathcal{N}|\leq C(L)
. These networks have no leaves since the terms

\mathcal{B}_{ij}^{(S)}
and
\mathcal{P}_{i}^{(S)}
do not involve the vector
\mathbf{u}
.

Let
K
be the set of indices appearing exactly once in

(i_{1},\ldots,i_{2L},j_{1},\ldots,j_{2L})
. For each
i_{l}\in K
(or
j_{l}\in K
),
Lemma
3.12
(b) ensures that
\mathbf{g}_{i_{l}}
(resp.
\mathbf{g}_{j_{l}}
)
appears an odd number of times in
q_{l}
and an even number of times in

\prod_{l^{\prime}\neq l}q_{l^{\prime}}
.
Thus, in the moment-cumulant expansion, there must exist some cumulant tensor

\kappa_{k}(\mathbf{g}_{i_{l}})
(resp.
\kappa_{k}(\mathbf{g}_{j_{l}})
) with
k
odd. Moreover, since

\mathbb{E}\mathbf{g}_{i_{l}}=\mathbb{E}\mathbf{g}_{j_{l}}=0
, we must have
k\geq 3
. Let
U_{\mathcal{G}}\subseteq V_{\mathcal{G}}^{t}
be a choice of one odd-degree tensor vertex representing an odd-order
cumulant for each
i_{l}\in K
or
j_{l}\in K
; we denote this choice by
v(i_{l})\in U_{\mathcal{G}}
or
v(j_{l})\in U_{\mathcal{G}}
. It follows that
|U_{\mathcal{G}}|=|K|
.

We now partition
U_{\mathcal{G}}
into
U_{\mathcal{G}}^{1}
and
U_{\mathcal{G}}^{2}
satisfying the
conditions (d) and (e) of the lemma. Let
U_{\mathcal{G}}^{1}
be the set of
v(i_{l})
or

v(j_{l})
in
U_{\mathcal{G}}
for which either

(1)

i_{l}
(resp.
j_{l}
) appears as a lower index in some
\{q_{l^{\prime}}:l^{\prime}\neq l\}
, or

(2)

i_{l}
(resp.
j_{l}
) does not appear as a lower index in any

\{q_{l^{\prime}}:l^{\prime}\neq l\}
, and
v(i_{l})
(resp.
v(j_{l})
) has at least three type 2
matrix neighbors.

To bound
|U_{\mathcal{G}}^{1}|
, let

m_{B}^{**}(l),m_{B}^{*1}(l),m_{B}^{*2}(l),m_{B}(l)
denote the values of

m_{B}^{**},m_{B}^{*1},m_{B}^{*2},m_{B}
for
q_{l}
as defined in
Lemma
3.12
, and set

m_{B}^{**}=\sum_{l=1}^{2L}m_{B}^{**}(l),\qquad m_{B}^{*}=\sum_{l=1}^{2L}m_{B}^{*1}(l)+m_{B}^{*2}(l),\qquad m_{B}=\sum_{l=1}^{2L}m_{B}(l).

Each
i_{l}\in K
(or
j_{l}\in K
) is distinct from
\{i_{l^{\prime}},j_{l^{\prime}}:l^{\prime}\neq l\}
,
so Lemma
3.12
(c) ensures that the
number of vertices
v(i_{l}),v(j_{l})\in U_{\mathcal{G}}
satisfying condition (1.)
is at most
m_{B}+\frac{1}{2}m_{B}^{*}
.

For condition (2.), denote further

m_{P}^{*1}(l)
as the number of factors of
\mathcal{P}_{i_{l}}^{(S)}
appearing in
q_{l}

for which its copy of
N^{-1}R^{(S)}
labels a type 2 matrix vertex.
Define similarly
m_{P}^{*2}(l)
as the number of such factors of

\mathcal{P}_{j_{l}}^{(S)}
in
q_{l}
, and set

E(l)=m_{B}^{**}(l)+\frac{1}{2}(m_{B}^{*1}(l)+m_{B}^{*2}(l))+m_{P}^{*1}(l)+m_{P}^{*2}(l)-1.

Consider any
l\in\{1,\ldots,2L\}
.
Suppose first that both
i_{l},j_{l}\in K
, and
v(i_{l}),v(j_{l})\in U_{\mathcal{G}}

and satisfy condition (2.).
Then either (i)
m_{B}^{**}(l)\geq 3
; (ii)
m_{B}^{**}(l)=2
in
which case
m_{B}^{*1}(l),m_{B}^{*2}(l)\geq 1
by Lemma

3.12
(b); (iii)
m_{B}^{**}(l)=1
in which case

m_{B}^{*1}(l)+m_{P}^{*1}(l)\geq 2

and
m_{B}^{*2}(l)+m_{P}^{*2}(l)\geq 2
since
v(i_{l}),v(j_{l})
both have at least
three type 2 matrix neighbors; or (iv)
m_{B}^{**}(l)=0
in which
case
m_{B}^{*1}(l)+m_{P}^{*1}(l)\geq 3

and
m_{B}^{*2}(l)+m_{P}^{*2}(l)\geq 3
. In all four cases (i–iv),
we verify that
E(l)\geq 2
.
Now suppose that only
v(i_{l})\in U_{\mathcal{G}}
satisfies condition (2.) (so
either
j_{l}\notin K
or
v(j_{l})\in U_{\mathcal{G}}
does not satisfy condition (2.)).
Then either (i)
m_{B}^{**}(l)\geq 2
;
(ii)
m_{B}^{**}(l)=1
and
m_{B}^{*1}(l)+m_{P}^{*1}(l)\geq 2
;
or (iii)
m_{B}^{**}(l)=0
and
m_{B}^{*1}(l)+m_{P}^{*1}(l)\geq 3
.
In case (iii), we have
also
m_{B}^{*2}(l)\geq 1
since
m_{B}^{*2}(l)
is odd by Lemma

3.12
(b). Thus in all three cases
(i–iii), we verify that
E(l)\geq 1
. Finally, we have
E(l)\geq 0
for every

l=1,\ldots,2L
by Lemma
3.12
(a). Thus, we conclude that the
number of vertices
v(i_{l}),v(j_{l})\in U_{\mathcal{G}}
satisfying condition (2.) is at
most
\sum_{l=1}^{2L}E(l)
, and so

|U_{\mathcal{G}}^{1}|\leq\underbrace{m_{B}+\frac{1}{2}m_{B}^{*}}_{\text{condition (1.)}}+\underbrace{\sum_{l=1}^{2L}E(l)}_{\text{condition (2.)}}\leq m_{B}+m_{B}^{*}+m_{B}^{**}+m_{P}^{*}-2L,

where we have set also
m_{P}^{*}=\sum_{l=1}^{2L}m_{P}^{*1}(l)+m_{P}^{*2}(l)
. Here

m_{B}+m_{B}^{*}+m_{B}^{**}+m_{P}^{*}\leq|V_{\mathcal{G}}^{m2}|
, the total number of type 2 matrix
vertices, showing statement (d) of the lemma.

Finally, each remaining vertex
v\in U_{\mathcal{G}}^{2}=U_{\mathcal{G}}\setminus U_{\mathcal{G}}^{1}
satisfies

\text{deg}(v)\geq 3
and
m2(v)\leq 2
, implying that
m1(v)\geq 1
. This shows
(e) and completes the proof.
∎

Proof of Lemma
3.6
(b).

Recall from (
3.20
) that, for any fixed constants
L\geq 1
and

D>0
,

(3.23)

\mathbb{E}\left|\sum_{i\neq j}\bar{\mathbf{v}}(i)\mathbf{v}(j)\mathcal{B}_{ij}^{(ij)}\mathcal{Q}_{i}^{(i)}\mathcal{Q}_{j}^{(ij)}\right|^{2L}\prec\max_{\begin{subarray}{c}i_{1},\ldots,i_{2L},j_{1},\ldots,j_{2L}\in[N]\\
q_{1}\in\mathcal{M}_{i_{1}j_{1},S},\ldots,q_{2L}\in\mathcal{M}_{i_{2L}j_{2L},S}\end{subarray}}\Big\{N^{|K|/2}\mathbb{E}|\mathbb{E}_{S}q|\Big\}+O_{\prec}(N^{-\tau^{\prime}(D+1)+2L}).

By Lemma
3.25
, there
exists a family
\mathcal{N}
of
(S,4L)
-valid tensor networks
(\mathcal{G},f_{\mathcal{G}})

for which

|\mathbb{E}_{S}q|\prec\max_{(\mathcal{G},f_{\mathcal{G}})\in\mathcal{N}}|\mathrm{val}(\mathcal{G},f_{\mathcal{G}})|.

Similar to the proof of Lemma
3.6
(a),
we reduce
(\mathcal{G},f_{\mathcal{G}})
in a sequence of
four steps. These steps will successively remove tensor vertices from the
network, introducing right new leaves and also eigen-leaves to
the network.
Letting
U^{1}_{\mathcal{G}},U_{\mathcal{G}}^{2}\subseteq V_{\mathcal{G}}^{t}
be the subsets of tensor
vertices in Lemma
3.25
, these steps are:

(1)

While there exists a tensor vertex
v\in U_{\mathcal{G}}^{2}
: Remove
v
and
apply Lemma
3.21
(a) to bound

|\mathrm{val}(\mathcal{G},f_{\mathcal{G}})|\prec\frac{N^{\delta}}{\sqrt{N}}\Phi^{r(v)}\max_{f_{\mathcal{G}^{\prime}}\in\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{G}^{\prime}}}|\mathrm{val}(\mathcal{G}^{\prime},f_{\mathcal{G}^{\prime}})|.

(2)

While there exists a tensor vertex
v\in U_{\mathcal{G}}^{1}
:

(a)

If there exists such a vertex with either
m1(v)\geq 1
,

r(v)\geq 1
, or both
e(v)=1
and
r(v)+m1(v)=0
, remove
v
and
apply either Lemma
3.21
(a) or (b) to bound

|\mathrm{val}(\mathcal{G},f_{\mathcal{G}})|\prec\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\Phi^{r(v)-1}\max_{f_{\mathcal{G}^{\prime}}\in\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{G}^{\prime}}}|\mathrm{val}(\mathcal{G}^{\prime},f_{\mathcal{G}^{\prime}})|.

(Lemma
3.21
(a) would give a stronger bound with

r(v)
in place of
r(v)-1
, and we weaken this to
r(v)-1
above.)

(b)

If all vertices
v\in U_{\mathcal{G}}^{1}
satisfy

e(v)+r(v)+m1(v)=0
, then since
\text{deg}(v)\geq 3
, each such
v
is adjacent to
at least three type 2 matrix vertices. Pick one of them, say
u\in V_{\mathcal{G}}^{m2}
.
Decomposing the label
N^{-1}R^{(S)}
of
u
as

N^{-1}\sum_{\alpha}|\lambda_{\alpha}-z|^{-1}\mathbf{v}_{\alpha}\mathbf{v}_{\alpha}^{*}
,
let
(\mathcal{G}^{\prime\prime},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{G}^{\prime\prime}})
denote the family of networks obtained
by replacing
u
with two eigen-leaves having a common
label in
\{\mathbf{v}_{\alpha}:\alpha\in[N]\}
. Pictorially, this
corresponds to the following operation:

\kappa_{k}(\mathbf{g}_{i})
\frac{R^{(S)}}{N}
\frac{R^{(S)}}{N}
\dots
\frac{R^{(S)}}{N}
\frac{R^{(S)}}{N}
\frac{R^{(S)}}{N}
\dots
\frac{R^{(S)}}{N}
\frac{R^{(S)}}{N}
\vdots
\frac{1}{N}\sum_{\alpha}\frac{1}{|\lambda_{\alpha}-z|}\times
\kappa_{k}(\mathbf{g}_{i})
\frac{R^{(S)}}{N}
\frac{R^{(S)}}{N}
\dots
\frac{R^{(S)}}{N}
\frac{R^{(S)}}{N}
\dots
\frac{R^{(S)}}{N}
\frac{R^{(S)}}{N}
\vdots
\mathbf{v}_{\alpha}
\mathbf{v}_{\alpha}

By the given bound
N^{-1}\|R^{(S)}\|_{1}\prec 1
, we have

|\mathrm{val}(\mathcal{G},f_{\mathcal{G}})|\prec\max_{f_{\mathcal{G}^{\prime\prime}}\in\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{G}^{\prime\prime}}}|\mathrm{val}(\mathcal{G}^{\prime\prime},f_{\mathcal{G}^{\prime\prime}})|

The vertex
v
in
(\mathcal{G}^{\prime\prime},f_{\mathcal{G}^{\prime\prime}})
now satisfies
e(v)=1
and
r(v)+m1(v)=0
,
so we may apply Lemma
3.21
(a) to remove
v
and bound
the resulting value. Letting
(\mathcal{G}^{\prime},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{G}^{\prime}})
denote the family of networks
obtained by removing
v
from some
(\mathcal{G}^{\prime\prime},f_{\mathcal{G}^{\prime\prime}})
in this way, this yields

|\mathrm{val}(\mathcal{G},f_{\mathcal{G}})|\prec\frac{N^{\delta}}{\sqrt{N}}\Phi^{r(v)}\max_{f_{\mathcal{G}^{\prime}}\in\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{G}^{\prime}}}|\mathrm{val}(\mathcal{G}^{\prime},f_{\mathcal{G}^{\prime}})|.

(3)

While there exists a tensor vertex
v\in V_{\mathcal{G}}^{t}
such that
\text{deg}(v)\geq 3
: Remove
v
, and apply Corollary
3.20
to bound

|\mathrm{val}(\mathcal{G},f_{\mathcal{G}})|\prec\Phi^{r(v)}\max_{f_{\mathcal{G}^{\prime}}\in\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{G}^{\prime}}}|\mathrm{val}(\mathcal{G}^{\prime},f_{\mathcal{G}^{\prime}})|.

(4)

At this point there are no type 1 matrix vertices,
and we have
\text{deg}(v)=2
for all
v\in V_{\mathcal{G}}^{t}
so

\mathcal{G}
contains only chains and cycles. The endpoints of a chain can be either right (new) leaves or eigen-leaves. Then, applying the bounds

\displaystyle\operatorname{Tr}(N^{-1}R^{(S)}\Sigma)^{k}

\displaystyle\prec\|N^{-1}R^{(S)}\|_{F}^{k}\prec\Phi^{k},

\displaystyle(N^{-1/2}R^{(S)}\mathbf{x})^{*}(N^{-1}R^{(S)})^{k}(N^{-1/2}R^{(S)}\mathbf{y})

\displaystyle\prec\|N^{-1}R^{(S)}\|_{F}^{k}\|N^{-1/2}R^{(S)}\mathbf{x}\|_{2}\|N^{-1/2}R^{(S)}\mathbf{y}\|_{2}\prec\Phi^{k+2},

\displaystyle\mathbf{v}_{\alpha}^{*}(N^{-1}R^{(S)})^{k}(N^{-1/2}R^{(S)}\mathbf{x})

\displaystyle\prec\|N^{-1}R^{(S)}\|_{F}^{k}\|N^{-1/2}R^{(S)}\mathbf{x}\|_{2}\prec\Phi^{k+1},

\displaystyle\mathbf{v}_{\alpha}^{*}(N^{-1}R^{(S)})^{k}\mathbf{v}_{\beta}

\displaystyle\prec\|N^{-1}R^{(S)}\|_{F}^{k}\prec\Phi^{k}

uniformly over
\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}\in\mathcal{U}
and eigenvectors
\mathbf{v}_{\alpha},\mathbf{v}_{\beta}
of
R^{(S)}
, we have

|\mathrm{val}(\mathcal{G},f_{\mathcal{G}})|\prec\Phi^{|V_{\mathcal{G}}^{m2}(4)|+|V_{\mathcal{G}}^{r}(4)|}.

where
|V_{\mathcal{G}}^{m2}(4)|,|V_{\mathcal{G}}^{r}(4)|
denote the numbers of type 2 matrix
vertices and right (new) leaves in the network at the start of Step 4.

We clarify that as in Lemma
3.6
(a),
Steps 1–3 are applied sequentially, where each application of
Step 2 applies Step 2(b) only when there are no vertices
v\in U_{\mathcal{G}}^{1}

satisfying the conditions of Step 2(a). At the conclusion of Step 1, all
vertices of
U_{\mathcal{G}}^{2}
are removed from the network.
Each time Step 2(b) is applied, it creates an eigen-leaf in the
resulting network
(\mathcal{G}^{\prime},f_{\mathcal{G}^{\prime}})
. If this eigen-leaf is adjacent to a
different vertex
v^{\prime}\in U_{\mathcal{G}}^{1}
, then Step 2(b) cannot be applied again until

v^{\prime}
is removed by a further application of Step 2(a). Thus, Step 2 preserves
the property that every
v\in U_{\mathcal{G}}^{1}
always has a number of adjacent
eigen-leaves
e(v)
that is 0 or 1. Then at the conclusion of Step 2, all
vertices of
U_{\mathcal{G}}^{1}
are removed from the network.

For each Step
k\in\{1,2,3\}
, let
|V_{\mathcal{G}}^{r}(k)|
denote the total
number of right (new) leaves removed in Step
k
. Denote also

|U_{\mathcal{G}}^{1}(2a)|
and
|U_{\mathcal{G}}^{1}(2b)|
as the total numbers of vertices in

U_{\mathcal{G}}^{1}
that are removed in Step 2(a) and 2(b), respectively. Then the above
procedure gives the bound

\displaystyle|\mathrm{val}(\mathcal{G},f_{\mathcal{G}})|

\displaystyle\prec\underbrace{\Big(\frac{N^{\delta}}{\sqrt{N}}\Big)^{|U_{\mathcal{G}}^{2}|}\Phi^{|V_{\mathcal{G}}^{r}(1)|}}_{\text{Step 1}}\times\underbrace{\Big(\frac{N^{\delta}}{\sqrt{N}}\Big)^{|U_{\mathcal{G}}^{1}|}\Phi^{|V_{\mathcal{G}}^{r}(2)|-|U_{\mathcal{G}}^{1}(2a)|}}_{\text{Step 2}}\times\underbrace{\Phi^{|V_{\mathcal{G}}^{r}(3)|}}_{\text{Step 3}}\times\underbrace{\Phi^{|V_{\mathcal{G}}^{m2}(4)|+|V_{\mathcal{G}}^{r}(4)|}}_{\text{Step 4}}

Each type 2 matrix vertex of the original network
(\mathcal{G},f_{\mathcal{G}})
is either
turned into a right new leaf in Steps 1–4, turned into an eigen-leaf in
Step 2(b), or remains a type 2 matrix vertex in Step 4. Moreover, each application of Step 2(b) turns exactly one matrix vertex into an eigen-leaf.
Thus

\sum_{k=1}^{4}|V_{\mathcal{G}}^{r}(k)|+|V_{\mathcal{G}}^{m2}(4)|=|V_{\mathcal{G}}^{m2}|-|U_{\mathcal{G}}^{1}(2b)|.

Combining with
|U_{\mathcal{G}}^{1}(2a)|+|U_{\mathcal{G}}^{1}(2b)|=|U_{\mathcal{G}}^{1}|
and

|U_{\mathcal{G}}^{1}|+|U_{\mathcal{G}}^{2}|=|K|
(by Lemma
3.25
) gives

\displaystyle|\mathrm{val}(\mathcal{G},f_{\mathcal{G}})|

\displaystyle\prec\Big(\frac{N^{\delta}}{\sqrt{N}}\Big)^{|K|}\Phi^{|V_{\mathcal{G}}^{m2}|-|U_{\mathcal{G}}^{1}|}\leq N^{-|K|/2}(N^{2\delta}\Phi)^{2L},

the second inequality applying
|V_{\mathcal{G}}^{m2}|\geq 2L+|U_{\mathcal{G}}^{1}|
from
Lemma
3.25
and
|K|\leq 4L
.
Applying this to
(
3.23
) and choosing
D>0
large enough shows that
(
3.23
) is
O_{\prec}((N^{2\delta}\Phi)^{2L})
, so the result follows
again from Markov’s inequality.
∎

3.5.6.
Proof of Lemma
3.6
(c)

The following lemma is similar to Lemmas

3.23
and

3.25
.

Lemma 3.26
.

Fix any integer
L\geq 1
. For any
i_{1},\ldots,i_{2L}\in[N]
, denote

S=\{i_{1},\ldots,i_{2L}\}
the set of distinct indices. Let
q_{1},\ldots,q_{2L}

be a sequence of monomials satisfying the conditions of
Lemma
3.13
, where each
q_{l}
is a monomial from the
expansion of
\mathcal{X}_{i_{l}}^{(i_{l})}\mathcal{Q}_{i_{l}}^{(i_{l})}
, and let

q=\prod_{l=1}^{L}q_{l}\prod_{l=L+1}^{2L}\bar{q}
.
Then there exists a constant
C\equiv C(L)>0
, an exponent
m\in[0,C]
, and a set

\mathcal{N}
of
(S,2L)
-valid tensor networks with
|\mathcal{N}|\leq C
, such that

|\mathbb{E}_{S}q|=|\mathcal{C}^{(S)}|^{m}\cdot\sum_{(\mathcal{G},f_{\mathcal{G}})\in\mathcal{N}}|\mathrm{val}(\mathcal{G},f_{\mathcal{G}})|

Moreover, for all
(\mathcal{G},f_{\mathcal{G}})\in\mathcal{N}
, there exist disjoint subsets of tensor vertices
U_{\mathcal{G}}^{1},U_{\mathcal{G}}^{2}\subseteq V_{\mathcal{G}}^{t}
such that the following holds:

(a)

We have
|V_{\mathcal{G}}^{ro}|=2L
and

|V_{\mathcal{G}}^{l}|=|V_{\mathcal{G}}^{rn}|=|V_{\mathcal{G}}^{e}|=0
. (I.e., there are no left leaves, right
new leaves, or eigen-leaves, and only right original leaves.)

(b)

For all
v\in U_{\mathcal{G}}^{1}\cup U_{\mathcal{G}}^{2}
,
\text{deg}(v)
is odd
and
\text{deg}(v)\geq 3
.

(c)

Let
K\subseteq S
be the subset of indices appearing exactly once in

(i_{1},\ldots,i_{2L})
. Then
|K|=|U_{\mathcal{G}}^{1}|+|U_{\mathcal{G}}^{2}|
.

(d)

We have
|V_{\mathcal{G}}^{ro}|+|V_{\mathcal{G}}^{m2}|\geq 2L+|U_{\mathcal{G}}^{1}|
.

(e)

For all
v\in U_{\mathcal{G}}^{2}
,
m1(v)\geq 1
.

Proof.

As in Lemmas
3.23
and Lemma

3.25
, we have

|\mathbb{E}_{S}q|=|\mathcal{C}^{(S)}|^{m}\cdot\sum_{(\mathcal{G},f_{\mathcal{G}})\in\mathcal{N}}|\mathrm{val}(\mathcal{G},f_{\mathcal{G}})|

for some
m\in[0,C(L)]
and family
\mathcal{N}
of
(S,2L)
-valid tensor networks
where
|\mathcal{N}|\leq C(L)
. It is clear that
|V_{\mathcal{G}}^{l}|=|V_{\mathcal{G}}^{rn}|=|V_{\mathcal{G}}^{e}|=0
in
this representation. The claim

|V_{\mathcal{G}}^{ro}|=2L
follows from the fact that there is exactly 1 factor of the
form
\mathcal{X}_{i_{l}}^{(S)}
or
\{\mathcal{X}_{j}^{(S)}\}_{j\in S^{(i_{l})}}
in each
q_{l}
,
establishing (a).

As in Lemma
3.25
, for each
i_{l}\in K
,

\mathbf{g}_{i_{l}}
appears an odd number of times in
q_{l}
and an even number of times
in
\prod_{l^{\prime}\neq l}q_{l^{\prime}}
, so we may identify a subset
U_{\mathcal{G}}=\{v(i_{l}):i_{l}\in K\}\subseteq V_{\mathcal{G}}^{t}
of tensor vertices representing an odd-order cumulant
of
\mathbf{g}_{i_{l}}
for each
i_{l}\in K
;
thus
|U_{\mathcal{G}}|=|K|
, and
\text{deg}(v)\geq 3
and
\text{deg}(v)
is
odd for each
v\in U_{\mathcal{G}}
.
We partition
U_{\mathcal{G}}
into
U_{\mathcal{G}}^{1}\cup U_{\mathcal{G}}^{2}
by letting
U_{\mathcal{G}}^{1}
be the set
of vertices
v(i_{l})
for which either

(1)

i_{l}
appears as a lower index in some
\{q_{l^{\prime}}:l^{\prime}\neq l\}
, or

(2)

i_{l}
does not appear as a lower index in any
\{q_{l^{\prime}}:l^{\prime}\neq l\}
, and

ro(v(i_{l}))+m2(v(i_{l}))\geq 3
.

Let
m_{X}^{*}(l),m_{X}(l),m_{B}^{*}(l),m_{B}(l)
denote the counts of Lemma

3.13
for each
q_{l}
, and let
m_{P}^{*}(l)
denote the
number of factors
\mathcal{P}_{i_{l}}^{(S)}
of
q_{l}
which correspond to type 2 matrix
vertices. Set

m_{X}^{*}=\sum_{l=1}^{2L}m_{X}^{*}(l),\quad m_{X}=\sum_{l=1}^{2L}m_{X}(l),\quad m_{B}^{*}=\sum_{l=1}^{2L}m_{B}^{*}(l),\quad m_{B}=\sum_{l=1}^{2L}m_{B}(l),\quad m_{P}^{*}=\sum_{l=1}^{2L}m_{P}(l)

and set also

E(l)=m_{X}^{*}(l)+\frac{1}{2}(m_{X}(l)+m_{B}^{*}(l))+m_{P}^{*}(l)-1.

By Lemma
3.13
, the number of vertices
v(i_{l})\in U_{\mathcal{G}}

satisfying (1.) is at most
\frac{1}{2}m_{X}+m_{B}+\frac{1}{2}m_{B}^{*}
. For each

v(i_{l})\in U_{\mathcal{G}}
satisfying (2.), we have either
m_{X}^{*}(l)=1
in which case

m_{B}^{*}(l)+m_{P}^{*}(l)\geq 2
, or
m_{X}^{*}(l)=0
in which case
m_{B}^{*}(l)+m_{P}^{*}(l)\geq 3
and also
m_{X}(l)=1
by Lemma
3.13
(a). Thus in
both cases,
E(l)\geq 1
. We have also
E(l)\geq 0
for each
l=1,\ldots,2L

by Lemma
3.13
, so the number of vertices
v(i_{l})\in U_{\mathcal{G}}
satisfying (2.) is at most
\sum_{l=1}^{2L}E(l)
, implying

|U_{\mathcal{G}}^{1}|\leq\frac{1}{2}m_{X}+m_{B}+\frac{1}{2}m_{B}^{*}+\sum_{l=1}^{2L}E(l)\leq m_{X}^{*}+m_{X}+m_{B}^{*}+m_{B}+m_{P}^{*}-2L\leq|V_{\mathcal{G}}^{ro}|+|V_{\mathcal{G}}^{m2}|-2L.

This shows (d). For each remaining
v\in U_{\mathcal{G}}^{2}
, we have
\text{deg}(v)\geq 3

and
ro(v)+m2(v)\leq 2
, implying
m1(v)\geq 1
, which shows (e).
∎

Proof of Lemma
3.6
(c).

Recall from (
3.21
) that, for any fixed constants
L\geq 1
and

D>0
,

(3.24)

\mathbb{E}\left|\sum_{i=1}^{N}\bar{\mathbf{v}}(i)\mathcal{X}_{i}^{(i)}\mathcal{Q}_{i}^{(i)}\right|^{2L}\prec\mathop{\max_{i_{1},\ldots,i_{2L}\in[N]}}_{q_{1}\in\mathcal{M}_{i_{1},S},\ldots,q_{2L}\in\mathcal{M}_{i_{2L},S}}\Big\{N^{|K|/2}\mathbb{E}|\mathbb{E}_{S}q|\Big\}+O_{\prec}(N^{-\tau^{\prime}(D+1)+L}).

By Lemma
3.26
, there exists a family

\mathcal{N}
of
(S,2L)
-valid tensor networks for which

\displaystyle|\mathbb{E}_{S}q|

\displaystyle\prec\max_{(\mathcal{G},f_{\mathcal{G}})\in\mathcal{N}}|\mathrm{val}(\mathcal{G},f_{\mathcal{G}})|.

By following the same four steps and arguments as in the proof of Lemma

3.6
(b), we arrive at the same bound

\displaystyle\mathrm{val}(\mathcal{G},f_{\mathcal{G}})

\displaystyle\prec\underbrace{\Big(\frac{N^{\delta}}{\sqrt{N}}\Big)^{|U_{\mathcal{G}}^{2}|}\Phi^{|V_{\mathcal{G}}^{r}(1)|}}_{\text{Step 1}}\times\underbrace{\Big(\frac{N^{\delta}}{\sqrt{N}}\Big)^{|U_{\mathcal{G}}^{1}|}\Phi^{|V_{\mathcal{G}}^{r}(2)|-|U_{\mathcal{G}}^{1}(2a)|}}_{\text{Step 2}}\times\underbrace{\Phi^{|V_{\mathcal{G}}^{r}(3)|}}_{\text{Step 3}}\times\underbrace{\Phi^{|V_{\mathcal{G}}^{m2}(4)|+|V_{\mathcal{G}}^{r}(4)|}}_{\text{Step 4}},

where the only difference is that
|V_{\mathcal{G}}^{r}(k)|
for each Step
k\in\{1,2,3,4\}
counts right original leaves in addition to right new leaves.
By the same arguments as in Lemma
3.6
(b), we have

\sum_{k=1}^{4}|V_{\mathcal{G}}^{r}(k)|+|V_{\mathcal{G}}^{m2}(4)|=|V_{\mathcal{G}}^{m2}|+|V_{\mathcal{G}}^{ro}|-|U_{\mathcal{G}}^{1}(2b)|,

|U_{\mathcal{G}}^{1}(2a)|+|U_{\mathcal{G}}^{1}(2b)|=|U_{\mathcal{G}}^{1}|
, and
|U_{\mathcal{G}}^{1}|+|U_{\mathcal{G}}^{2}|=|K|
, so

\displaystyle|\mathrm{val}(\mathcal{G},f_{\mathcal{G}})|

\displaystyle\prec\Big(\frac{N^{\delta}}{\sqrt{N}}\Big)^{|K|}\Phi^{|V_{\mathcal{G}}^{ro}|+|V_{\mathcal{G}}^{m2}|-|U_{\mathcal{G}}^{1}|}\leq N^{-|K|/2}(N^{\delta}\Phi)^{2L},

the last inequality using
|V_{\mathcal{G}}^{ro}|+|V_{\mathcal{G}}^{m2}|\geq 2L+|U_{\mathcal{G}}^{1}|
and

K\leq 2L
.
Applying this above and choosing
D>0
large enough shows that
(
3.24
) is
O_{\prec}((N^{\delta}\Phi)^{2L})
, so the result follows
again by Markov’s inequality.
∎

4.
Proofs of the main results

We now show Theorems
2.5
,
2.8
,
and
2.10
. Many of the arguments are close to those of

[
76
,
13
,
69
,
54
]
,
which we reproduce here for the reader’s convenience.

4.1.
Resolvent bounds

For each
z=E+i\eta\in\mathbb{C}^{+}
, let us define the control parameters

\Lambda=\max_{i,j=1}^{N}|\widetilde{R}_{ij}-\widetilde{m}_{0}\mathbf{1}\{i=j\}|,\qquad\Theta=|\widetilde{m}-\widetilde{m}_{0}|,\qquad\Psi_{\Theta}=\sqrt{\frac{\operatorname{Im}{\widetilde{m}_{0}}+\Theta}{N\eta}}.

Fixing a constant
C_{0}>0
, we define the (
z
-dependent) event

\Xi=\{\Lambda\leq N^{-\tau/C_{0}}\}.

Lemma 4.1

(Global bounds)
.

Suppose Assumptions
1
and
2
hold. Then
for all
i\in[N]
and

z=E+i\eta\in\mathbb{C}^{+}
satisfying
|z|\leq C
for a constant
C>0
, we have

|\widetilde{m}|\leq\eta^{-1},\quad|\widetilde{R}_{ii}|\leq\eta^{-1},\quad|z\widetilde{R}_{ii}|=|1+N^{-1}\mathbf{g}_{i}^{*}R^{(i)}\mathbf{g}_{i}|^{-1}\leq C\eta^{-1},\quad N^{-1/2}\|R^{(i)}\|_{F}\leq\eta^{-1},

\|(I+\widetilde{m}\Sigma)^{-1}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq C\eta^{-1},\quad\|(I+\widetilde{m}^{(i)}\Sigma)^{-1}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq C\eta^{-1}.

Furthermore, uniformly over
i\in[N]
and

z=E+i\eta\in\mathbb{C}^{+}
with
|z|\leq C
,

|\widetilde{m}|^{-1}\prec\eta^{-1},\quad|\widetilde{m}-\widetilde{m}^{(i)}|\prec N^{-1}\eta^{-3}.

Proof.

The first four bounds follow from

\|\widetilde{R}\|_{\mathrm{op}},\|\widetilde{R}^{(i)}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\eta^{-1}
.
For
\|(I+\widetilde{m}\Sigma)^{-1}\|_{\mathrm{op}}
, letting

\lambda_{i}
denote the
i
-th eigenvalue of
\widetilde{K}
, note that

\operatorname{Im}{[z\widetilde{m}(z)]}=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\frac{\eta\lambda_{i}}{|\lambda_{i}-z|^{2}}\geq 0.

Then for any
\alpha\in[n]
,

|z+z\sigma_{\alpha}\widetilde{m}(z)|\geq|\eta+\sigma_{\alpha}\operatorname{Im}{[z\widetilde{m}(z)]}|\geq\eta
,
so
\|(I+\widetilde{m}\Sigma)^{-1}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq|z|\eta^{-1}\leq C\eta^{-1}
,
and similarly

\|(I+\widetilde{m}^{(i)}\Sigma)^{-1}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq C\eta^{-1}
.
For the bound on
|\widetilde{m}|^{-1}
,

|\widetilde{m}|\geq\operatorname{Im}{\widetilde{m}}=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\frac{\eta}{(\lambda_{i}-E)^{2}+\eta^{2}}\geq\frac{\eta}{\max_{i}(\lambda_{i}-E)^{2}+\eta^{2}}.

Applying
\|\widetilde{K}\|_{\mathrm{op}}=\|K\|_{\mathrm{op}}\prec 1
from Lemma

3.8
(b) and
|z|\leq C
yields
\max_{\alpha}(\lambda_{\alpha}-E)^{2}+\eta^{2}\prec 1
, so
|\widetilde{m}|^{-1}\prec\eta^{-1}
.
For
|\widetilde{m}-\widetilde{m}^{(i)}|
, apply (
3.4
) to obtain

|\widetilde{m}-\widetilde{m}^{(i)}|=\gamma|m-m^{(i)}|=|z\widetilde{R}_{ii}N^{-2}\mathbf{g}_{i}^{*}(R^{(i)})^{2}\mathbf{g}_{i}|\prec\eta^{-1}(N^{-1}\|R^{(i)}\|_{F})^{2}\leq N^{-1}\eta^{-3},

where we applied Assumptions
1
and
2

to show
\mathbf{g}_{i}^{*}(R^{(i)})^{2}\mathbf{g}_{i}\prec|\operatorname{Tr}(R^{(i)})^{2}\Sigma|+\|(R^{(i)})^{2}\|_{F}\prec\|R^{(i)}\|_{F}^{2}
.
∎

Lemma 4.2

(Local bounds)
.

Suppose Assumptions
1
and
2
hold, and

\mathbf{D}=\{z\in\mathbb{C}^{+}:E\in U,\,\eta\in[N^{-1+\tau},1]

is a regular spectral domain.
Then for any fixed
L\geq 0
, uniformly over all
z\in\mathbf{D}
,
S\subseteq[N]

with
|S|\leq L
, and
i,j\notin S
with
i\neq j
,

|\widetilde{R}^{(S)}_{ii}|\mathbf{1}_{\Xi}\prec 1,\quad|\widetilde{R}^{(S)}_{ii}|^{-1}\mathbf{1}_{\Xi}\prec 1,\quad|z\widetilde{R}^{(S)}_{ii}|\mathbf{1}_{\Xi}=|1+N^{-1}\mathbf{g}_{i}^{*}R^{(iS)}\mathbf{g}_{i}|^{-1}\mathbf{1}_{\Xi}\prec 1,\quad|\widetilde{R}_{ij}^{(S)}|\mathbf{1}_{\Xi}\prec\Psi_{\Theta},

|\widetilde{m}^{(S)}|\mathbf{1}_{\Xi}\prec 1,\quad|\widetilde{m}^{(S)}|^{-1}\mathbf{1}_{\Xi}\prec 1,\quad\|(I+\widetilde{m}^{(S)}\Sigma)^{-1}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\mathbf{1}_{\Xi}\prec 1.

N^{-1}\|R^{(S)}\|_{F}\mathbf{1}_{\Xi}\prec\Psi_{\Theta},\quad N^{-1}\|R^{(S)}\|_{1}\mathbf{1}_{\Xi}\prec 1.

Moreover, uniformly also over deterministic unit vectors
\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}\in\mathbb{C}^{n}
,

|\widetilde{m}-\widetilde{m}^{(S)}|\mathbf{1}_{\Xi}\prec\Psi_{\Theta}^{2},\quad|\widetilde{R}_{ii}^{(S)}-\widetilde{m}^{(S)}|\mathbf{1}_{\Xi}\prec\Psi_{\Theta},

|\mathbf{x}^{*}R\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{x}^{*}R^{(S)}\mathbf{y}|\mathbf{1}_{\Xi}\prec\frac{\|R\mathbf{x}\|_{2}}{\sqrt{N}}\cdot\frac{\|R\mathbf{y}\|_{2}}{\sqrt{N}},\quad\|R^{(S)}\mathbf{x}\|_{2}\mathbf{1}_{\Xi}\prec\|R\mathbf{x}\|_{2}.

Proof.

The bounds

|\widetilde{R}_{ii}^{(S)}|\mathbf{1}_{\Xi}\prec 1,\quad|\widetilde{R}_{ii}^{(S)}|^{-1}\mathbf{1}_{\Xi}\prec 1,\quad|z\widetilde{R}_{ii}^{(S)}|\mathbf{1}_{\Xi}=|1+N^{-1}\mathbf{g}_{i}^{*}R^{(iS)}\mathbf{g}_{i}|^{-1}\mathbf{1}_{\Xi}\prec 1

follow from
|z|,|\widetilde{m}_{0}(z)|\asymp 1
in
Definition
2.3
for regularity of
\mathbf{D}
,
the definition of
\Xi
, and iterative application of the resolvent identity
(
3.2
).

We next prove
|\widetilde{m}-\widetilde{m}^{(S)}|\mathbf{1}_{\Xi}\prec\Psi_{\Theta}^{2}
by induction on
|S|
. For
|S|=1
, say
S=\{i\}
, we have

|\widetilde{m}-\widetilde{m}^{(i)}|=\gamma|m-m^{(i)}|=|N^{-1}\operatorname{Tr}R-N^{-1}\operatorname{Tr}R^{(i)}|.

Applying the Sherman-Morrison identity (
3.4
) and
Assumption
2
yields

|\widetilde{m}-\widetilde{m}^{(i)}|\mathbf{1}_{\Xi}=|z\widetilde{R}_{ii}||N^{-2}\mathbf{g}_{i}^{*}R^{(i)2}\mathbf{g}_{i}|\mathbf{1}_{\Xi}\prec N^{-2}\|R^{(i)}\|_{F}^{2}\mathbf{1}_{\Xi}\prec\Psi_{\Theta}^{2}+(N\eta)^{-1}|\widetilde{m}-\widetilde{m}^{(i)}|\mathbf{1}_{\Xi},

where the last bound uses Ward’s identity (
3.5
),

\displaystyle N^{-2}\|R^{(i)}\|_{F}^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{\gamma\operatorname{Im}{m^{(i)}}}{N\eta}=\frac{\operatorname{Im}{\widetilde{m}^{(i)}}}{N\eta}+\frac{(1-\gamma)\eta|z|^{-2}}{N\eta}

\displaystyle\prec\frac{\operatorname{Im}{\widetilde{m}_{0}}+\Theta}{N\eta}+\frac{|\widetilde{m}-\widetilde{m}^{(i)}|}{N\eta}+\frac{1}{N}\prec\Psi_{\Theta}^{2}+\frac{|\widetilde{m}-\widetilde{m}^{(i)}|}{N\eta}.

Since
(N\eta)^{-1}\leq N^{-\tau}\leq 1/2
for large
N
, rearranging proves the
case
|S|=1
. Now
assume the claim holds for
|S|=s
. For
i\notin S
with
|S|=s
, let
S=\{i_{1},\dots,i_{s}\}
and
S_{k}=\{i_{1},\dots,i_{k}\}
. Iteratively applying (
3.4
) gives

\displaystyle|\widetilde{m}-\widetilde{m}^{(iS)}|\mathbf{1}_{\Xi}

\displaystyle=\left|z\widetilde{R}^{(S)}_{ii}N^{-2}\mathbf{g}_{i}^{*}(R^{(iS)})^{2}\mathbf{g}_{i}+\sum_{k=1}^{s}z\widetilde{R}^{(S_{k-1})}_{i_{k}i_{k}}N^{-2}\mathbf{g}_{i_{k}}^{*}(R^{(S_{k})})^{2}\mathbf{g}_{i_{k}}\right|\mathbf{1}_{\Xi}

\displaystyle\prec\Psi_{\Theta}^{2}+(N\eta)^{-1}|\widetilde{m}-\widetilde{m}^{(iS)}|\mathbf{1}_{\Xi}+(N\eta)^{-1}\sum_{k=1}^{s}|\widetilde{m}-\widetilde{m}^{(S_{k})}|\mathbf{1}_{\Xi}

\displaystyle\prec\Psi_{\Theta}^{2}+(N\eta)^{-1}|\widetilde{m}-\widetilde{m}^{(iS)}|\mathbf{1}_{\Xi}

where the last line applies the inductive hypothesis.
Rearranging completes the induction.

Since
|\widetilde{m}-\widetilde{m}_{0}|=\Theta\leq\Lambda
,
the bounds
|\widetilde{m}^{(S)}|\mathbf{1}_{\Xi}\prec 1
,

|\widetilde{m}^{(S)}|^{-1}\mathbf{1}_{\Xi}\prec 1
, and

\|(I+\widetilde{m}^{(S)}\Sigma)^{-1}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\mathbf{1}_{\Xi}\prec 1
then follow from
the definition of
\Xi
and the corresponding statements for
\widetilde{m}_{0}

in Definition
2.3
for the regularity of
\mathbf{D}
.
The bound
N^{-1}\|R^{(S)}\|_{F}\mathbf{1}_{\Xi}\prec\Psi_{\Theta}
follows from
Ward’s identity as applied above, and

|\widetilde{R}_{ij}^{(S)}|\mathbf{1}_{\Xi}\prec\Psi_{\Theta}
follows from
the resolvent identity (
3.3
) and Assumption

2
.

For
N^{-1}\|R^{(S)}\|_{1}
, corresponding to
z=E+i\eta\in\mathbf{D}
, define

L=\max\{l\in\mathbb{N}:\eta 2^{l-1}<1\},\quad\eta_{l}=2^{l}\eta\text{ for }l=0,\dots,L-1,\quad\eta_{L}=1.

Then
L\leq C\log N
and
\eta_{l}/\eta_{l-1}\leq 2
. Let

\{\lambda_{\alpha}\}_{\alpha=1}^{n}
be the eigenvalues of
K^{(S)}
. Define
\eta_{-1}=0
,
\eta_{L+1}=\infty
, and

U_{l}=\{\alpha:\eta_{l-1}\leq|\lambda_{\alpha}-E|<\eta_{l}\},\quad l=0,\dots,L+1.

Then

N^{-1}\|R^{(S)}\|_{1}=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{\alpha=1}^{n}\frac{1}{|\lambda_{\alpha}-z|}=\sum_{l=0}^{L+1}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{\alpha\in U_{l}}\frac{1}{|\lambda_{\alpha}-z|}.

For
l=1,\dots,L
, since
\eta_{l-1}\leq|\lambda_{\alpha}-E|<\eta_{l}
for

\alpha\in U_{l}
and
|\lambda_{\alpha}-z|\geq|\lambda_{\alpha}-E|
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{\alpha\in U_{l}}\frac{1}{|\lambda_{\alpha}-z|}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{n}\sum_{\alpha\in U_{l}}\frac{\eta_{l}}{(\lambda_{\alpha}-E)^{2}}\leq\frac{2}{n}\sum_{\alpha\in U_{l}}\frac{\eta_{l}}{(\lambda_{\alpha}-E)^{2}+\eta_{l-1}^{2}}\leq\frac{2\eta_{l}}{\eta_{l-1}}\operatorname{Im}m(E+i\eta_{l-1}).

Since the map
\eta\mapsto\eta\operatorname{Im}m(E+i\eta)
is nondecreasing,

\operatorname{Im}m(E+i\eta_{l-1})\leq\frac{\eta_{l}}{\eta_{l-1}}\operatorname{Im}m(E+i\eta_{l})
.
Then, applying
\eta_{l}/\eta_{l-1}\leq 2
,

\frac{1}{n}\sum_{\alpha\in U_{l}}\frac{1}{|\lambda_{\alpha}-z|}\leq 8\operatorname{Im}m(E+i\eta_{l}).

For
l=0
, since
0\leq|\lambda_{\alpha}-E|<\eta_{0}=\eta
for
\alpha\in U_{0}
, we have similarly

\frac{1}{n}\sum_{\alpha\in U_{0}}\frac{1}{|\lambda_{\alpha}-z|}\leq\frac{2}{n}\sum_{\alpha\in U_{0}}\frac{\eta}{(\lambda_{\alpha}-E)^{2}+\eta^{2}}\leq 2\operatorname{Im}m(E+i\eta)\leq 4\operatorname{Im}m(E+i\eta_{1}).

For
l=L+1
, since
\eta_{L}=1
,
|\lambda_{\alpha}-E|\geq 1
for
\alpha\in U_{L+1}
, and
\max_{\alpha}\lambda_{\alpha}=\|K\|_{\mathrm{op}}\prec 1

by Lemma
3.8
(b), we have

\frac{1}{n}\sum_{\alpha\in U_{L+1}}\frac{1}{|\lambda_{\alpha}-z|}\leq\frac{1}{n}\sum_{\alpha\in U_{L+1}}\frac{|\lambda_{\alpha}-E|+\eta}{(\lambda_{\alpha}-E)^{2}+\eta^{2}}\prec\frac{1}{n}\sum_{\alpha\in U_{L+1}}\frac{1}{(\lambda_{\alpha}-E)^{2}+1}\leq\operatorname{Im}m(E+i\eta_{L}).

Thus

(4.1)

N^{-1}\|R^{(S)}\|_{1}\prec\sum_{l=1}^{L}\operatorname{Im}m^{(S)}(E+i\eta_{l}).

Since
\gamma m^{(S)}=\widetilde{m}^{(S)}+(1-\gamma)z^{-1}
and
\gamma\asymp 1
, this implies

N^{-1}\|R^{(S)}\|_{1}\mathbf{1}_{\Xi}\prec\left(\sum_{l=1}^{L}\operatorname{Im}\widetilde{m}^{(S)}(E+i\eta_{l})+\frac{\eta_{l}}{|E+i\eta_{l}|^{2}}\right)\mathbf{1}_{\Xi}\prec 1

where the last bound uses
\operatorname{Im}\widetilde{m}^{(S)}(E+i\eta_{l})\mathbf{1}_{\Xi}\prec 1
,

\eta_{l}/|E+i\eta_{l}|^{2}\prec 1
since
|E|\geq\delta
by Definition

2.3
for regularity of
\mathbf{D}
, and

L\leq C\log N\prec 1
.

For
|\widetilde{R}_{ii}^{(S)}-\widetilde{m}^{(S)}|
, applying the resolvent
identity (
3.2
), we have on
\Xi
that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\widetilde{R}_{ii}^{(S)}}

\displaystyle=-z-zN^{-1}\mathbf{g}_{i}^{*}R^{(iS)}\mathbf{g}_{i}

\displaystyle=-z-zN^{-1}\operatorname{Tr}\Sigma R^{(iS)}+O_{\prec}(N^{-1}\|R^{(iS)}\|_{F})

\displaystyle=-z-zN^{-1}\operatorname{Tr}\Sigma R-z^{2}\widetilde{R}_{ii}^{(S)}N^{-2}\mathbf{g}_{i}^{*}R^{(iS)}\Sigma R^{(iS)}\mathbf{g}_{i}+O_{\prec}(N^{-1}\|R^{(iS)}\|_{F})

\displaystyle=-z-zN^{-1}\operatorname{Tr}\Sigma R-z^{2}\widetilde{R}_{ii}^{(S)}N^{-2}\operatorname{Tr}\Sigma R^{(i)}\Sigma R^{(iS)}+O_{\prec}(N^{-2}\|R^{(iS)}\|_{F}^{2})+O_{\prec}(N^{-1}\|R^{(iS)}\|_{F})

\displaystyle=-z-zN^{-1}\operatorname{Tr}\Sigma R+O_{\prec}(N^{-2}\|R^{(iS)}\|_{F}^{2})+O_{\prec}(N^{-1}\|R^{(iS)}\|_{F}).

Thus, for
i\neq j
,

\left|\frac{1}{\widetilde{R}_{ii}^{(S)}}-\frac{1}{\widetilde{R}_{jj}^{(S)}}\right|\mathbf{1}_{\Xi}\prec\Psi_{\Theta}^{2}+\Psi_{\Theta}\prec\Psi_{\Theta},

and so

|\widetilde{R}_{ii}^{(S)}-\widetilde{m}^{(S)}|\mathbf{1}_{\Xi}\leq\frac{1}{N}\sum_{j=1}^{N}|\widetilde{R}_{ii}^{(S)}-\widetilde{R}_{jj}^{(S)}|\mathbf{1}_{\Xi}\leq\frac{1}{N}\sum_{j=1}^{N}|\widetilde{R}_{ii}^{(S)}\widetilde{R}_{jj}^{(S)}|\left|\frac{1}{\widetilde{R}_{ii}^{(S)}}-\frac{1}{\widetilde{R}_{jj}^{(S)}}\right|\mathbf{1}_{\Xi}\prec\Psi_{\Theta}.

Finally, for
|\mathbf{x}^{*}R\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{x}^{*}R^{(S)}\mathbf{y}|
, the bound

|\mathbf{g}_{i}^{*}R^{(S)}\mathbf{x}|\prec\|R^{(S)}\mathbf{x}\|_{2}
in Lemma
3.8
(a)
and iterative application of the Sherman-Morrison identity
(
3.4
) yield

|\mathbf{x}^{*}R\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{x}^{*}R^{(S)}\mathbf{y}|\mathbf{1}_{\Xi}\prec\|R\mathbf{x}\|_{2}\|R\mathbf{y}\|_{2}/N
.
The last claim for
\|R^{(S)}\mathbf{x}\|_{2}
then follows from

\|R^{(S)}\mathbf{x}\|_{2}^{2}\mathbf{1}_{\Xi}=\frac{\operatorname{Im}{\mathbf{x}^{*}R^{(S)}\mathbf{x}}}{\eta}\mathbf{1}_{\Xi}\prec\frac{\operatorname{Im}{\mathbf{x}^{*}R\mathbf{x}}}{\eta}+\frac{|\mathbf{x}^{*}R\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{x}^{*}R^{(S)}\mathbf{x}|}{\eta}\mathbf{1}_{\Xi}\prec\|R\mathbf{x}\|_{2}^{2}+\frac{\|R\mathbf{x}\|_{2}^{2}}{N\eta}\prec\|R\mathbf{x}\|_{2}^{2}.

∎

4.2.
Proof of entrywise law (Theorem
2.5
)

We now begin analysis of the Marchenko-Pastur fixed point equation.

Lemma 4.3
.

For any
A\in\mathbb{C}^{n\times n}
and
z\in\mathbb{C}^{+}
, we have

(4.2)

\operatorname{Tr}RA-\operatorname{Tr}(-zI-z\widetilde{m}\Sigma)^{-1}A=\frac{1}{z}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\frac{d_{i}(A)}{1+N^{-1}\mathbf{g}_{i}^{*}R^{(i)}\mathbf{g}_{i}},

where

d_{i}(A)=N^{-1}\mathbf{g}_{i}^{*}R^{(i)}A(I+\widetilde{m}\Sigma)^{-1}\mathbf{g}_{i}-N^{-1}\operatorname{Tr}RA(I+\widetilde{m}\Sigma)^{-1}\Sigma.

In particular, recalling the function
z_{0}(m)
from (
2.7
)
and setting
A=I
in (
4.2
),

(4.3)

z_{0}(\widetilde{m})-z=-\frac{1}{\widetilde{m}}\cdot\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\frac{d_{i}(I)}{1+N^{-1}\mathbf{g}_{i}^{*}R^{(i)}\mathbf{g}_{i}}.

Proof.

The argument is the same as
[
76
, Eq. (2.4)]
:
For any
B\in\mathbb{C}^{n\times n}
,

\displaystyle\operatorname{Tr}B=\operatorname{Tr}(K-zI)RB

\displaystyle={-}z\operatorname{Tr}RB+\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathbf{g}_{i}^{*}RB\mathbf{g}_{i}={-}z\operatorname{Tr}RB+\sum_{i=1}^{N}\frac{N^{-1}\mathbf{g}_{i}^{*}R^{(i)}B\mathbf{g}_{i}}{1+N^{-1}\mathbf{g}_{i}^{*}R^{(i)}\mathbf{g}_{i}}

where the last equality applies the Sherman-Morrison identity
(
3.4
). Taking
B=I_{n}
,
applying
\operatorname{Tr}R(z)=nm(z)=N\widetilde{m}(z)-(n-N)/z
, and rearranging gives

\widetilde{m}={-}\frac{1}{Nz}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\frac{1}{1+N^{-1}\mathbf{g}_{i}^{*}R^{(i)}\mathbf{g}_{i}}.

Then taking
B=A(I+\widetilde{m}\Sigma)^{-1}
and identifying
d_{i}(A)
gives

\displaystyle\operatorname{Tr}(I+\widetilde{m}\Sigma)^{-1}A

\displaystyle=-z\operatorname{Tr}RA(I+\widetilde{m}\Sigma)^{-1}+\sum_{i=1}^{N}\frac{d_{i}(A)+N^{-1}\operatorname{Tr}RA(I+\widetilde{m}\Sigma)^{-1}\Sigma}{1+N^{-1}\mathbf{g}_{i}^{*}R^{(i)}\mathbf{g}_{i}}

\displaystyle=-z\operatorname{Tr}RA(I+\widetilde{m}\Sigma)^{-1}-z\widetilde{m}\operatorname{Tr}RA(I+\widetilde{m}\Sigma)^{-1}\Sigma+\sum_{i=1}^{N}\frac{d_{i}(A)}{1+N^{-1}\mathbf{g}_{i}^{*}R^{(i)}\mathbf{g}_{i}}

\displaystyle={-}z\operatorname{Tr}RA+\sum_{i=1}^{N}\frac{d_{i}(A)}{1+N^{-1}\mathbf{g}_{i}^{*}R^{(i)}\mathbf{g}_{i}}.

Rearranging shows (
4.2
),
and specializing to
A=I
shows (
4.3
).
∎

The following lemma will allow us to simplify the analysis by replacing factors
of
(I+\widetilde{m}\Sigma)^{-1}
in
d_{i}(A)

with their deterministic counterpart
(I+\widetilde{m}_{0}\Sigma)^{-1}
.

Lemma 4.4
.

Suppose Assumptions
1
and
2
hold, and

\mathbf{D}
is a regular spectral domain.
Then there exists a constant
l\geq 1
such that, uniformly over
all
z\in\mathbf{D}
, deterministic matrices
A\in\mathbb{C}^{n\times n}
, and
i\in[N]
,

d_{i}(A)\mathbf{1}_{\Xi}=\sum_{k=0}^{l}(\widetilde{m}-\widetilde{m}_{0})^{k}\left[N^{-1}\operatorname{Tr}(\mathbf{g}_{i}\mathbf{g}_{i}^{*}-\Sigma)R^{(i)}A\Sigma^{k}(I+\widetilde{m}_{0}\Sigma)^{-k-1}\right]\mathbf{1}_{\Xi}+O_{\prec}(\Psi_{\Theta}^{2}\|A\|_{\mathrm{op}}).

Moreover, if
A=\mathbf{u}\mathbf{v}^{*}
for vectors
\mathbf{u},\mathbf{v}\in\mathbb{C}^{n}
, then

\displaystyle d_{i}(\mathbf{u}\mathbf{v}^{*})\mathbf{1}_{\Xi}

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=0}^{l}(\widetilde{m}-\widetilde{m}_{0})^{k}\left[N^{-1}\operatorname{Tr}(\mathbf{g}_{i}\mathbf{g}_{i}^{*}-\Sigma)R^{(i)}\mathbf{u}\mathbf{v}^{*}\Sigma^{k}(I+\widetilde{m}_{0}\Sigma)^{-k-1}\right]\mathbf{1}_{\Xi}

\displaystyle\hskip 21.68121pt+O_{\prec}(N^{-2}\|R^{(i)}\mathbf{u}\|_{2}\|\mathbf{v}^{*}\Sigma^{k+1}(I+\widetilde{m}_{0}\Sigma)^{-k-1}R^{(i)}\|_{2})+O_{\prec}(N^{-1}\Psi_{\Theta}\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}\|\mathbf{v}\|_{2}).

Proof.

Iteratively applying the identity
A^{-1}-B^{-1}=A^{-1}(B-A)B^{-1}
gives, for any integer
l\geq 1
,

(I+\widetilde{m}\Sigma)^{-1}=\sum_{k=0}^{l}(\widetilde{m}-\widetilde{m}_{0})^{k}\Sigma^{k}(I+\widetilde{m}_{0}\Sigma)^{-k-1}+(\widetilde{m}-\widetilde{m}_{0})^{l+1}\Sigma^{l+1}(I+\widetilde{m}_{0}\Sigma)^{-l-1}(I+\widetilde{m}\Sigma)^{-1},

using the commutativity of
\Sigma
,
(I+\widetilde{m}_{0}\Sigma)^{-1}
, and
(I+\widetilde{m}\Sigma)^{-1}
. Thus, for any
i\in[N]
,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N}\mathbf{g}_{i}^{*}R^{(i)}A(I+\widetilde{m}\Sigma)^{-1}\mathbf{g}_{i}-\frac{1}{N}\operatorname{Tr}RA(I+\widetilde{m}\Sigma)^{-1}\Sigma

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=0}^{l}(\widetilde{m}-\widetilde{m}_{0})^{k}\left[\frac{1}{N}\mathbf{g}_{i}^{*}R^{(i)}A\Sigma^{k}(I+\widetilde{m}_{0}\Sigma)^{-k-1}\mathbf{g}_{i}-\frac{1}{N}\operatorname{Tr}RA\Sigma^{k}(I+\widetilde{m}_{0}\Sigma)^{-k-1}\Sigma\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 72.26999pt+(\widetilde{m}-\widetilde{m}_{0})^{l+1}\frac{1}{N}\mathbf{g}_{i}^{*}R^{(i)}A\Sigma^{l+1}(I+\widetilde{m}_{0}\Sigma)^{-l-1}(I+\widetilde{m}\Sigma)^{-1}\mathbf{g}_{i}

\displaystyle\hskip 72.26999pt-(\widetilde{m}-\widetilde{m}_{0})^{l+1}\frac{1}{N}\operatorname{Tr}RA\Sigma^{l+1}(I+\widetilde{m}_{0}\Sigma)^{-l-1}(I+\widetilde{m}\Sigma)^{-1}\Sigma.

Since
\Theta\leq\Lambda\leq N^{-\tau/C_{0}}
on
\Xi
, the remainder terms satisfy

\displaystyle\left|(\widetilde{m}-\widetilde{m}_{0})^{l+1}\frac{1}{N}\mathbf{g}_{i}^{*}R^{(i)}A\Sigma^{l+1}(I+\widetilde{m}_{0}\Sigma)^{-l-1}(I+\widetilde{m}\Sigma)^{-1}\mathbf{g}_{i}\right|\mathbf{1}_{\Xi}

\displaystyle\leq|\widetilde{m}-\widetilde{m}_{0}|^{l+1}\frac{1}{N}\|\mathbf{g}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}\|R^{(i)}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\|A\|_{\mathrm{op}}\|\Sigma\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{l+1}\|(I+\widetilde{m}_{0}\Sigma)^{-1}\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{l+1}\|(I+\widetilde{m}\Sigma)^{-1}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\mathbf{1}_{\Xi}

\displaystyle\prec N^{-(l+1)\tau/C_{0}}\eta^{-1}\|A\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq N^{-(l+1)\tau/C_{0}+1}\|A\|_{\mathrm{op}},

using
\eta\geq N^{-1+\tau}>N^{-1}
, and similarly

\displaystyle\left|(\widetilde{m}-\widetilde{m}_{0})^{l+1}\frac{1}{N}\operatorname{Tr}RA\Sigma^{l+1}(I+\widetilde{m}_{0}\Sigma)^{-l-1}(I+\widetilde{m}\Sigma)^{-1}\Sigma\right|\mathbf{1}_{\Xi}\prec N^{-(l+1)\tau/C_{0}+1}\|A\|_{\mathrm{op}}.

For each
0\leq k\leq l
,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N}\mathbf{g}_{i}^{*}R^{(i)}A\Sigma^{k}(I+\widetilde{m}_{0}\Sigma)^{-k-1}\mathbf{g}_{i}\mathbf{1}_{\Xi}-\frac{1}{N}\operatorname{Tr}RA\Sigma^{k}(I+\widetilde{m}_{0}\Sigma)^{-k-1}\Sigma\mathbf{1}_{\Xi}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N}\operatorname{Tr}(\mathbf{g}_{i}\mathbf{g}_{i}^{*}-\Sigma)R^{(i)}A\Sigma^{k}(I+\widetilde{m}_{0}\Sigma)^{-k-1}\mathbf{1}_{\Xi}+\frac{N^{-2}\mathbf{g}_{i}^{*}R^{(i)}A\Sigma^{k+1}(I+\widetilde{m}_{0}\Sigma)^{-k-1}R^{(i)}\mathbf{g}_{i}}{1+N^{-1}\mathbf{g}_{i}^{*}R^{(i)}\mathbf{g}_{i}}\mathbf{1}_{\Xi},

and applying Assumption
2
, Cauchy-Schwarz, and Lemma
4.2
yields

\displaystyle\frac{N^{-2}|\mathbf{g}_{i}^{*}R^{(i)}A\Sigma^{k+1}(I+\widetilde{m}_{0}\Sigma)^{-k-1}R^{(i)}\mathbf{g}_{i}|}{|1+N^{-1}\mathbf{g}_{i}^{*}R^{(i)}\mathbf{g}_{i}|}\mathbf{1}_{\Xi}

\displaystyle\prec|N^{-2}\operatorname{Tr}\Sigma R^{(i)}A\Sigma^{k+1}(I+\widetilde{m}_{0}\Sigma)^{-k-1}R^{(i)}|\mathbf{1}_{\Xi}+N^{-2}\|R^{(i)}A\Sigma^{k+1}(I+\widetilde{m}_{0}\Sigma)^{-k-1}R^{(i)}\|_{F}\mathbf{1}_{\Xi}

\displaystyle\prec(N^{-1}\|R^{(i)}\|_{F})^{2}\|A\|_{\mathrm{op}}\mathbf{1}_{\Xi}\prec\Psi_{\Theta}^{2}\|A\|_{\mathrm{op}}.

If
A=\mathbf{u}\mathbf{v}^{*}
, then we may apply instead

\displaystyle\frac{N^{-2}|\mathbf{g}_{i}^{*}R^{(i)}\mathbf{u}\mathbf{v}^{*}\Sigma^{k+1}(I+\widetilde{m}_{0}\Sigma)^{-k-1}R^{(i)}\mathbf{g}_{i}|}{|1+N^{-1}\mathbf{g}_{i}^{*}R^{(i)}\mathbf{g}_{i}|}\mathbf{1}_{\Xi}\prec N^{-2}\|R^{(i)}\mathbf{u}\|_{2}\|\mathbf{v}^{*}\Sigma^{k+1}(I+\widetilde{m}_{0}\Sigma)^{-k-1}R^{(i)}\|_{2}\mathbf{1}_{\Xi}.

The result follows by choosing
l
large enough so that

N^{-(l+1)\tau/C_{0}+1}\leq N^{-1}\Psi_{\Theta}\leq\Psi_{\Theta}^{2}
, noting that
l

is a constant depending only on
\tau
and
C_{0}
.
∎

Lemma 4.5
.

Suppose Assumptions
1
and
2
hold, and

\mathbf{D}
is a regular spectral domain.
Then there exists a constant
l\geq 1
such that, uniformly over
z\in\mathbf{D}
,

\displaystyle[z_{0}(\widetilde{m})-z]\mathbf{1}_{\Xi}

\displaystyle=O_{\prec}(\Psi_{\Theta}^{2})+O_{\prec}\left(\max_{0\leq k\leq l}\left|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\frac{N^{-1}\operatorname{Tr}(\mathbf{g}_{i}\mathbf{g}_{i}^{*}-\Sigma)R^{(i)}\Sigma^{k}(I+\widetilde{m}_{0}\Sigma)^{-k-1}}{1+N^{-1}\mathbf{g}_{i}^{*}R^{(i)}\mathbf{g}_{i}}\right|\mathbf{1}_{\Xi}\right)

\displaystyle=O_{\prec}(\Psi_{\Theta}).

Proof.

By Lemma
4.3
,

[z_{0}(\widetilde{m})-z]\mathbf{1}_{\Xi}=-\frac{1}{\widetilde{m}}\cdot\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\frac{d_{i}(I)}{1+N^{-1}\mathbf{g}_{i}^{*}R^{(i)}\mathbf{g}_{i}}\mathbf{1}_{\Xi}.

Applying Lemma
4.4
with
A=I
gives, for a sufficiently large integer
l\geq 1
,

d_{i}(I)\mathbf{1}_{\Xi}=\sum_{k=0}^{l}(\widetilde{m}-\widetilde{m}_{0})^{k}\left[N^{-1}\operatorname{Tr}(\mathbf{g}_{i}\mathbf{g}_{i}^{*}-\Sigma)R^{(i)}\Sigma^{k}(I+\widetilde{m}_{0}\Sigma)^{-k-1}\right]\mathbf{1}_{\Xi}+O_{\prec}(\Psi_{\Theta}^{2}).

Since
\Theta=|\widetilde{m}-\widetilde{m}_{0}|\leq\Lambda\leq N^{-\tau/C_{0}}\leq 1
on
\Xi
and
l
is fixed, substituting the expansion of
d_{i}(I)
into the expression for
z_{0}(\widetilde{m})-z
and applying the bounds from Lemma
4.2
yield the first equality.
Moreover, by Assumption
2
, uniformly over all
0\leq k\leq l
,

|N^{-1}\operatorname{Tr}(\mathbf{g}_{i}\mathbf{g}_{i}^{*}-\Sigma)R^{(i)}\Sigma^{k}(I+\widetilde{m}_{0}\Sigma)^{-k-1}|\mathbf{1}_{\Xi}\prec N^{-1}\|R^{(i)}\|_{F}\mathbf{1}_{\Xi}\prec\Psi_{\Theta}.

Then the second equality follows since
\Psi_{\Theta}^{2}\prec\Psi_{\Theta}
.
∎

Lemma 4.6
.

Suppose Assumptions
1
and
2
hold, and

\mathbf{D}
is a regular spectral domain. Then, uniformly over
z=E+i\eta\in\mathbf{D}

with
\eta=1
, we have
\Lambda(z)\prec N^{-1/4}
.

Proof.

Let
d_{i}(I)
be as in Lemma
4.3
. As in
[
13
]
,
we decompose
d_{i}(I)=d_{i,1}+d_{i,2}+d_{i,3}+d_{i,4}
, where

\displaystyle d_{i,1}

\displaystyle=N^{-1}\mathbf{g}_{i}^{*}R^{(i)}(I+\widetilde{m}\Sigma)^{-1}\mathbf{g}_{i}-N^{-1}\mathbf{g}_{i}^{*}R^{(i)}(I+\widetilde{m}^{(i)}\Sigma)^{-1}\mathbf{g}_{i},

\displaystyle d_{i,2}

\displaystyle=N^{-1}\mathbf{g}_{i}^{*}R^{(i)}(I+\widetilde{m}^{(i)}\Sigma)^{-1}\mathbf{g}_{i}-N^{-1}\operatorname{Tr}\Sigma R^{(i)}(I+\widetilde{m}^{(i)}\Sigma)^{-1},

\displaystyle d_{i,3}

\displaystyle=N^{-1}\operatorname{Tr}\Sigma R^{(i)}(I+\widetilde{m}^{(i)}\Sigma)^{-1}-N^{-1}\operatorname{Tr}\Sigma R(I+\widetilde{m}^{(i)}\Sigma)^{-1},

\displaystyle d_{i,4}

\displaystyle=N^{-1}\operatorname{Tr}\Sigma R(I+\widetilde{m}^{(i)}\Sigma)^{-1}-N^{-1}\operatorname{Tr}\Sigma R(I+\widetilde{m}\Sigma)^{-1}.

Applying the bounds from Lemma
4.1
and Assumption
2
yields

\displaystyle|d_{i,1}|

\displaystyle=|\widetilde{m}-\widetilde{m}^{(i)}||N^{-1}\mathbf{g}_{i}^{*}R^{(i)}(I+\widetilde{m}\Sigma)^{-1}\Sigma(I+\widetilde{m}^{(i)}\Sigma)^{-1}\mathbf{g}_{i}|

\displaystyle\prec|\widetilde{m}-\widetilde{m}^{(i)}|(\|R^{(i)}\|_{\mathrm{op}}+N^{-1}\|R^{(i)}\|_{F})\|(I+\widetilde{m}\Sigma)^{-1}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\|(I+\widetilde{m}^{(i)}\Sigma)^{-1}\|_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle\prec N^{-1}\eta^{-6},

\displaystyle|d_{i,2}|

\displaystyle\prec N^{-1}\|R^{(i)}(I+\widetilde{m}^{(i)}\Sigma)^{-1}\|_{F}\leq N^{-1}\|R^{(i)}\|_{F}\|(I+\widetilde{m}^{(i)}\Sigma)^{-1}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\prec N^{-1/2}\eta^{-2},

\displaystyle|d_{i,3}|

\displaystyle=\frac{|N^{-2}\mathbf{g}_{i}^{*}R^{(i)}(I+\widetilde{m}^{(i)}\Sigma)^{-1}\Sigma R^{(i)}\mathbf{g}_{i}|}{|1+N^{-1}\mathbf{g}_{i}^{*}R^{(i)}\mathbf{g}_{i}|}\prec N^{-2}\|R^{(i)}\|_{F}^{2}\eta^{-2}\prec N^{-1}\eta^{-4},

\displaystyle|d_{i,4}|

\displaystyle=|\widetilde{m}-\widetilde{m}^{(i)}||N^{-1}\operatorname{Tr}\Sigma R(I+\widetilde{m}^{(i)}\Sigma)^{-1}\Sigma(I+\widetilde{m}\Sigma)^{-1}|

\displaystyle\leq|\widetilde{m}-\widetilde{m}^{(i)}|\|\Sigma\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}\|(I+\widetilde{m}^{(i)}\Sigma)^{-1}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\|(I+\widetilde{m}\Sigma)^{-1}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\|R\|_{\mathrm{op}}\prec N^{-1}\eta^{-6}.

Since
\eta=1
, we have
|d_{i}(I)|\prec N^{-1/2}
. Thus, by Lemma
4.3
,

\displaystyle z_{0}(\widetilde{m})-z=-\frac{1}{\widetilde{m}}\cdot\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\frac{O_{\prec}(N^{-1/2})}{1+N^{-1}\mathbf{g}_{i}^{*}R^{(i)}\mathbf{g}_{i}}=O_{\prec}(N^{-1/2}).

For any small
\varepsilon>0
and large
D>0
, there exists
N_{0}(\varepsilon,D)
such that for all
N\geq N_{0}
,

\mathbb{P}\left(|z_{0}(\widetilde{m})-z|\leq N^{\varepsilon}N^{-1/2}\right)\geq 1-N^{-D}.

Since
N^{\varepsilon}N^{-1/2}
satisfies the requirements for
\Delta
in Definition

2.3
, on this event,

|\widetilde{m}-\widetilde{m}_{0}|\leq\frac{CN^{\varepsilon}N^{-1/2}}{\sqrt{\kappa+\eta}+N^{\varepsilon/2}N^{-1/4}}\leq CN^{\varepsilon/2}N^{-1/4}.

Since
\varepsilon
is arbitrary,
\Theta\prec N^{-1/4}
. By Lemma
4.2
with
\eta=1
, uniformly over distinct
i,j\in[N]
,

|\widetilde{R}_{ii}-\widetilde{m}_{0}|\leq|\widetilde{R}_{ii}-\widetilde{m}|+\Theta\prec\Psi_{\Theta}+\Theta\prec N^{-1/4},\quad|\widetilde{R}_{ij}|\prec\Psi_{\Theta}\prec N^{-1/2}.

∎

The following lemma now establishes a weak local law over all
z\in\mathbf{D}

using a stochastic continuity argument (see e.g.
[
69
, Lemma
6.12]
).

Lemma 4.7
.

Suppose Assumptions
1
and
2
hold, and

\mathbf{D}
is a regular spectral domain. Then uniformly over
z=E+i\eta\in\mathbf{D}
,
we have
\Lambda(z)\prec(N\eta)^{-1/4}\leq N^{-\tau/4}
.

Proof.

For any
z=E+i\eta\in\mathbf{D}
, let
L(z)
be as in Definition
2.3
.
Then
M=|L(z)|\leq N^{5}
. Define
z_{0}=E+i
,
z_{M}=z
, and
z_{k}:=E+i\eta_{k}=E+i(1-kN^{-5})
for
1\leq k\leq M-1
. For a small enough
\varepsilon>0
, introduce the events

\Omega_{k}:=\Omega(z_{k}):=\left\{|\widetilde{m}(z_{k})-\widetilde{m}_{0}(z_{k})|\leq\frac{CN^{2\varepsilon}(N\eta_{k})^{-1/2}}{\sqrt{\kappa+\eta_{k}}+N^{\varepsilon}(N\eta_{k})^{-1/4}}\right\},

\mathcal{E}_{k}:=\mathcal{E}(z_{k}):=\left\{\Lambda(z_{k})\leq N^{2\varepsilon}(N\eta_{k})^{-1/4}\right\},

where
\eta_{k}=\operatorname{Im}z_{k}
. Choose a large enough
D>0
. We proceed with a
stochastic continuity argument. For
k=0
, by Lemma
4.6
and
Definition
2.3
, we have

\mathbb{P}((\Omega_{0}\cap\mathcal{E}_{0})^{c})\leq N^{-D}.

For any
k\geq 1
, since
\Lambda
is
2N^{2}
-Lipschitz continuous over
z\in\mathbf{D}
, and
|\eta_{k}-\eta_{k-1}|=N^{-5}
, on the event

\Omega_{k-1}\cap\mathcal{E}_{k-1}
we have
\Lambda(z_{k})\leq N^{2\varepsilon}(N\eta_{k-1})^{-1/4}+2N^{2-5}\leq N^{2\varepsilon-\tau/4}+2N^{-3}
,
implying that for
C_{0}>5
, small enough
\varepsilon>0
, and large enough
N
,
the event
\Xi(z_{k})=\{\Lambda(z_{k})\leq N^{-\tau/C_{0}}\}
holds. Thus, by Lemma
4.5
,

\mathbb{P}\left(\Omega_{k-1}\cap\mathcal{E}_{k-1}\cap\left\{|z_{0}(\widetilde{m}(z_{k}))-z_{k}|\geq N^{2\varepsilon}(N\eta_{k})^{-1/2}\right\}\right)\leq N^{-D},

where we used
\Psi_{\Theta}(z_{k})\mathbf{1}_{\Xi(z_{k})}\prec(N\eta_{k})^{-1/2}
. Since the
function
\Delta(w)=N^{2\varepsilon}(N\operatorname{Im}w)^{-1/2}
satisfies the requirements of
Definition
2.3
, it follows that

\mathbb{P}\left(\Omega_{k-1}\cap\mathcal{E}_{k-1}\cap\left\{|\widetilde{m}(z_{k})-\widetilde{m}_{0}(z_{k})|\geq\frac{CN^{2\varepsilon}(N\eta_{k})^{-1/2}}{\sqrt{\kappa+\eta_{k}}+N^{\varepsilon}(N\eta_{k})^{-1/4}}\right\}\right)\leq N^{-D},

which implies
\mathbb{P}(\Omega_{k-1}\cap\mathcal{E}_{k-1}\cap\Omega_{k}^{c})\leq N^{-D}
. On the other hand, by Lemma
4.2
, on the event
\Omega_{k-1}\cap\mathcal{E}_{k-1}\cap\Omega_{k}
we have

\Lambda(z_{k})\mathbf{1}_{\Omega_{k-1}\cap\mathcal{E}_{k-1}\cap\Omega_{k}}\prec(\Psi_{\Theta}(z_{k})+\Theta(z_{k}))\mathbf{1}_{\Omega_{k-1}\cap\mathcal{E}_{k-1}\cap\Omega_{k}}\prec N^{\varepsilon}(N\eta_{k})^{-1/4},

which gives
\mathbb{P}(\Omega_{k-1}\cap\mathcal{E}_{k-1}\cap\Omega_{k}\cap\mathcal{E}_{k}^{c})\leq N^{-D}
for sufficiently large
N
. Therefore,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\Omega_{k-1}\cap\mathcal{E}_{k-1}\cap\left(\Omega_{k}\cap\mathcal{E}_{k}\right)^{c}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}(\Omega_{k-1}\cap\mathcal{E}_{k-1}\cap\Omega_{k}^{c})+\mathbb{P}\left(\Omega_{k-1}\cap\mathcal{E}_{k-1}\cap\Omega_{k}\cap\mathcal{E}_{k}^{c}\right)\leq 2N^{-D}.

In general, for any
0\leq k\leq M
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}((\Omega_{k}\cap\mathcal{E}_{k})^{c})

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}((\Omega_{0}\cap\mathcal{E}_{0})^{c})+\sum_{j=1}^{k}\mathbb{P}\left(\Omega_{j-1}\cap\mathcal{E}_{j-1}\cap\left(\Omega_{j}\cap\mathcal{E}_{j}\right)^{c}\right)

\displaystyle\leq N^{-D}+2kN^{-D}\leq CN^{5}N^{-D}=CN^{5-D}.

Since
D
is arbitrary, this proves the claim.
∎

Remark 4.8
.

Let us take
C_{0}>5
in the definition
\Xi=\{\Lambda\leq N^{-\tau/C_{0}}\}
.
Then this implies that
\mathbf{1}_{\Xi}\prec 1
uniformly
over
z\in\mathbf{D}
, so all preceding estimates hold with
\mathbf{1}_{\Xi}
replaced by 1.

Proof of Theorem
2.5
.

Applying Lemma
4.5
gives

(4.4)

z_{0}(\widetilde{m})-z=O_{\prec}(\Psi_{\Theta}^{2})+O_{\prec}\left(\max_{0\leq k\leq l}\left|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\frac{N^{-1}\operatorname{Tr}(\mathbf{g}_{i}\mathbf{g}_{i}^{*}-\Sigma)R^{(i)}\Sigma^{k}(I+\widetilde{m}_{0}\Sigma)^{-k-1}}{1+N^{-1}\mathbf{g}_{i}^{*}R^{(i)}\mathbf{g}_{i}}\right|\right).

Now suppose for some constant
c\in(0,1]
we have
\Theta\prec(N\eta)^{-c}
. Then

\Psi_{\Theta}\prec\sqrt{\frac{\operatorname{Im}{\widetilde{m}_{0}}+(N\eta)^{-c}}{N\eta}}:=\Phi.

Note that Definition
2.3
implies
\eta\lesssim\operatorname{Im}\widetilde{m}_{0}\lesssim 1
, so
N^{-1/2}\lesssim\Phi\lesssim N^{-\tau/2}
.
By Lemma
4.2
, uniformly over all
S\subseteq[N]
with
|S|\leq L
a fixed constant, we have
N^{-1}\|R^{(S)}\|_{F}\prec\Phi
, and also
\Gamma^{(S)}=\max_{i}|\widetilde{R}_{ii}^{(S)}-\widetilde{m}^{(S)}|\prec\Psi_{\Theta}+\Theta\prec N^{-c\tau/2}
.
Thus, by the fluctuation averaging result of
Lemma
3.4
, for all
0\leq k\leq l
,

\displaystyle\left|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\frac{N^{-1}\operatorname{Tr}(\mathbf{g}_{i}\mathbf{g}_{i}^{*}-\Sigma)R^{(i)}\Sigma^{k}(I+\widetilde{m}_{0}\Sigma)^{-k-1}}{1+N^{-1}\mathbf{g}_{i}^{*}R^{(i)}\mathbf{g}_{i}}\right|

\displaystyle\prec\|\Sigma\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{k}\|(I+\widetilde{m}_{0}\Sigma)^{-1}\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{k+1}\Phi^{2}\prec\Phi^{2}.

This implies
|z_{0}(\widetilde{m})-z|\prec\Phi^{2}
. Then by the stability
condition of Definition
2.3
and the bound
\operatorname{Im}\widetilde{m}_{0}(z)\leq C\sqrt{\kappa+\eta}
,

\displaystyle\Theta=|\widetilde{m}-\widetilde{m}_{0}|\prec\frac{\frac{\operatorname{Im}{\widetilde{m}_{0}}+(N\eta)^{-c}}{N\eta}}{\sqrt{\kappa+\eta}+\sqrt{\frac{\operatorname{Im}{\widetilde{m}_{0}}+(N\eta)^{-c}}{N\eta}}}

\displaystyle\prec\frac{1}{N\eta}+\frac{(N\eta)^{-c-1}}{(N\eta)^{-c/2-1/2}}\prec(N\eta)^{-c/2-1/2}.

Thus, we have shown the implication

\Theta\prec(N\eta)^{-c}\Rightarrow\Theta\prec(N\eta)^{-c/2-1/2}
.
For any
\varepsilon>0
, starting with
c=1/4
and iterating this a constant
C_{\varepsilon}
number of times yields
\Theta\prec(N\eta)^{-1+\varepsilon}
. Since
\varepsilon>0
is arbitrary,
\Theta\prec(N\eta)^{-1}
. Substituting into
\Psi_{\Theta}
gives

\Psi_{\Theta}\prec\sqrt{\frac{\operatorname{Im}{\widetilde{m}_{0}}}{N\eta}}+\frac{1}{N\eta}:=\Psi.

Then by Lemma
4.2
,

|\widetilde{R}_{ii}-\widetilde{m}_{0}|\prec\Theta+\Psi_{\Theta}\prec\Psi
and

|\widetilde{R}_{ij}|\prec\Psi
, which shows (
2.13
).
For (
2.12
), applying the above bounds with
c=1

gives
|z_{0}(\widetilde{m})-z|\prec\Psi^{2}
,
so applying Definition
2.3
once more gives

|\widetilde{m}-\widetilde{m}_{0}|\prec\frac{\Psi^{2}}{\sqrt{\kappa+\eta}+\Psi}.

Since
\widetilde{m}-\widetilde{m}_{0}=\gamma(m-m_{0})
and
\gamma\asymp 1
,
the same bound holds for
m-m_{0}
, so this shows
(
2.12
). Since all quantities in
(
2.13
) and
(
2.12
) are
N^{2}
-Lipschitz over
z\in\mathbf{D}
,
taking a union bound over a covering net shows that with probability

1-CN^{-D}
, these statements hold simultaneously for every
z\in\mathbf{D}
.
∎

Corollary
2.7
now follows from known arguments,
which we summarize briefly here. The following lemma is a version of

[
54
, Lemmas 10.3, 10.4]
(see also the asymptotic exact
separation result of
[
12
]
),
clarifying that no bulk/edge regularity is needed for this statement, and that
it holds also for values
a\in(0,\infty)
below the smallest left edge.

Lemma 4.9
.

Suppose Assumptions
1
and
2
hold.
Let
\lambda_{1},\ldots,\lambda_{N}
be the eigenvalues of
\widetilde{K}
, and for
each
a\in(0,\infty)
, let

(4.5)

\displaystyle\widehat{N}(a)=\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathbf{1}\{\lambda_{i}\geq a\},\qquad N(a)=N\int_{a}^{\infty}\rho_{0}(x)dx.

Then for any constants
\delta,D>0
, there exists a constant

C\equiv C(\delta,D)>0
such that if
a\in(0,\infty)
satisfies

(a-\delta,a+\delta)\cap\operatorname{supp}(\widetilde{\mu}_{0})=\emptyset
, then with
probability at least
1-CN^{-D}
,

\widehat{N}(a)=N(a).

Proof.

The averaged local law (
2.12
) applied
for
z=E+i\eta\in\mathbf{D}^{o}(\delta,\tau)
with any fixed constant

\tau\in(0,1/2)
implies
|\widetilde{m}(z)-\widetilde{m}_{0}(z)|\ll(N\eta)^{-1}
. Then, since
\operatorname{Im}\widetilde{m}_{0}\lesssim\eta\ll(N\eta)^{-1}
by (
2.10
) and regularity of

\mathbf{D}^{o}(\delta,\tau)
(c.f. Lemma
2.4
),
we have
\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathbf{1}\{\lambda_{i}\in[E-\eta,E+\eta]\}\leq 2N\eta\operatorname{Im}\widetilde{m}(z)\ll 1
, so

\widetilde{K}
has no eigenvalue in
[E-\eta,E+\eta]
. Thus,
(
2.12
) implies for some constant

C\equiv C(\delta,D)>0
, with probability
1-CN^{-D}
,

(4.6)

\widetilde{K}\text{ has no eigenvalues in }\{x\in\mathbb{R}:|x|\leq\delta^{-1},\operatorname{dist}(x,\operatorname{supp}(\widetilde{\mu}_{0})\cup\{0\})\geq\delta\}.

To show (
4.5
), we begin with the case where
G
is
Gaussian, i.e.
\widetilde{K}=N^{-1}X^{*}\Sigma X
where
X\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times N}

has i.i.d.
N(0,1)
entries and assume without loss of generality
\Sigma=\operatorname{diag}(\sigma_{1},\ldots,\sigma_{n})
with
\sigma_{1}\geq\ldots\geq\sigma_{n}
. Let
